A bus-level trace in `fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v`, using one counter in one clock domain, arms on a READ command to the word holding `0x0800009c` and shows the part behaving correctly: the command appears with `dq=0000`, two clocks later `dq=d421` -- the correct halfword for bytes `21 d4` -- and it is held stable thereafter, which is exactly CL=2. The controller issues `dqm=00` on reads, so no lane is masked and the model's whole-word drive is not implicated."
  consequence: "The part, the pin-level model, the bus, the DQM handling and the word and halfword collection paths are all excluded by measurement. The corruption happens between the bus and `cpu_rdata`, inside `sdram_gba`'s collection for the byte arms, `casez (ds_latch[0])` mapping `4'b0001` to `{4{dq_in[7:0]}}`. Any further work should instrument `dq_in` on the collect edge for a byte read in the retry loop rather than re-examining the bus. Wording rule: this is a model-versus-design result about `sdram_gba`'s logic under this port's simulation timing, and it says nothing about the physical board, which runs the same controller on a real part and cannot be diagnosed with this instrument."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/run_cal_a/ca.log` and `build/run_cal_c/cc.log`, both 3000 million steps, scored by byte enable with `tools/check_cartread.py`'s column"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v`: the `TINYGBA_BUS_TRACE` block, one counter, arming on the READ to `A=04e`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v`: the collect stage's `casez (ds_latch[0])` and the `dq_in` wire"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/diag/sdram_model.v`: `rd_pipe`/`rd_valid_pipe` and the `dq_out <= rd_pipe[0]` drive"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08. The bus window is 24 consecutive clocks from `build/run_bus2/bus.log`, showing `dq=0000` at the command and `dq=d421` two clocks later, held. The width breakdown is computed from the calibrated captures rather than read by eye. An earlier attempt with separate counters in the controller and the model reported a spurious 16-cycle offset and was discarded in favour of the single-domain bus trace."

- record_id: BRD-035
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The pin-level SDRAM model let a deselected die keep driving DQ, and that was the logo-check failure"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "The constant `0x75` that the BIOS logo check rejected at `0x0800009c` was driven onto the bus by the SDRAM model's deselected die. `fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v` wires die 0 to `nCS` and die 1 to `~nCS` onto one shared `SDRAM_DQ`, and `fpga/gba/diag/sdram_model.v` withdrew its drive only on `cs && command == CMD_RD`. A die therefore released the bus only when it was itself selected and receiving a new read, so a deselected die with data still in its read pipeline drove indefinitely and overrode the selected die. `build/run_bt6`, printing both instances unconditionally on one counter, shows this directly: at the cycle die 0 drove `d421` for `0x0800009c`, die 1 simultaneously drove `f374`, and the bus settled on `f775` whose low byte is the rejected `0x75`. A real part tri-states whenever `nCS` is high, so the defect can only exist in a model. The repair is one condition, `if (command == CMD_RD)` in place of `if (cs && command == CMD_RD)`: `command` is decoded from RAS, CAS and WE, which every die sees, so a deselected die releases the bus when any read is issued. Gating on `cs` alone is measured wrong: the controller raises `nCS` immediately after the command while the data appears two clocks later, so that form suppressed the word it should drive."
  consequence: "The logo retry loop is gone and cartridge reads went from 112 of 4,000 correct to 753 of 764 on a clean rebuild at 3000 million steps, measured with `tools/check_cartread.py`'s calibrated column. The 11 remaining failures are all 32-bit word reads of the literal pool at `0x080002b0` to `0x080002e4` and are the separate one-word lag BRD-032 records, so the machine still does not boot: those literals feed the entry code's own checks. Wording rule: this is a model defect fixed in a model, and it says nothing about the physical board. BRD-033 still governs -- the model is not the board's failure -- and no bitstream was built or deployed from it."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/diag/sdram_model.v`: the DQ withdrawal condition, before and after"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v`: `cs_die0 = SDRAM_nCS` and `cs_die1 = ~SDRAM_nCS` wired to two instances sharing `SDRAM_DQ`"
    - "This repository, `build/run_bt6/bt6.log`: per-die `DIE c=... size=... cs=... oen=... dq_out=...` lines around the `0x0800009c` read"
    - "This repository, `build/run_fin/fn.log` and `build/run_mfix2/m2.log`: the corrected model at 3000 million steps"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08. The bus window shows both dies driving at once with the selected die holding the correct word; after the one-line change a clean rebuild reports 753 of 764 reads matching the ROM against 112 of 4,000 before, and the retry count at `0x0800009c` falls from 3,881 to zero. The frames remain a single colour at a mean of 252, so the fix is real and insufficient."

- record_id: BRD-036
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "With correct read timing the BIOS boot logo renders through the real SDRAM controller"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "With the pin-level model's deselected-die bus defect corrected (BRD-035) and the 32-bit read acknowledgement moved to the high-halfword collection, `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` runs the official BIOS far enough to render its boot screen: frames 300 to 700 of a 3000-million-step run show the GAME BOY wordmark and the Nintendo mark with the logo's colour cycle animating, then the field goes white at frame 800. `tools/check_cartread.py` reports 448 of 448 cartridge data reads matching the ROM with zero failures, against 753 of 764 before the acknowledgement change and 112 of 4,000 before the bus fix. The cartridge fetch cycle count falls from 18,375,892 to 18, which is close to the fifteen accesses BRD-023 records from the board with the same cartridge. A `DISPCNT <= 0x0403` store is never issued, so the display is never enabled and the cartridge's own self-checks are not reached."
  consequence: "The controller's read path is sound once the acknowledgement coincides with the complete word, so the memory system is no longer a suspect in the model and the read timing question is closed. The stopping point is now shared with the board rather than being a model artefact, which makes it worth solving once for both. It also means BRD-033's claim that the model is not the board's failure needs re-testing: that record was written when the model was reading corrupted data and diverging by six orders of magnitude in fetch count, and a fetch count that now tracks the board's within a few accesses does not support the same conclusion. Wording rule: the model renders the BIOS logo, and the cartridge still does not run in the model or on the board."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/run_ack/ak.log` and frames `build/run_ack/ak_frame0300..0800.ppm`, 3000 million steps"
    - "This repository, `build/run_wt/wt.log`: the 32-bit collect trace showing `hi=0` producing `e1540400` and `hi=1` producing `08000400` four clocks later"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v` and `gba_memory.sv`: the acknowledgement move and the output-mux window"
    - "This repository, BRD-023 for the board's fifteen accesses with the same cartridge"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08 at 3000 million steps: 448 of 448 reads match, the frame dumps show the animated GAME BOY logo between frames 300 and 700, and the fetch-cycle counter reads 18. The frames were inspected as images rather than by colour count alone."

- record_id: TCL-026
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A simulation-only source list means a model fix cannot reach the board, and the build script is the place to check"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "`fpga/gba/diag/sdram_model.v` and `fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v` are compiled by `tools/sim/build_gba_sim.sh` and are absent from `tools/build_tinygba.sh`'s synthesis source list, whose only `diag/` entry is `sdram_probe.v`. `gba_sdram_rtl.v` is reached from `gbatang_top.sv` only under `TINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL`, a define no synthesised build sets. A bitstream built from a tree whose only changes are in those files is therefore byte-identical to one built before them, and flashing it tests nothing about the change. This was confirmed by reading the two source lists rather than by building: a build-and-diff would have cost about fifty minutes to demonstrate a fact already visible in the file lists." 
  consequence: "Before proposing a board session for a fix, check which source list compiles the changed files. A model-only change is still worth making, because the model is the only instrument that can be single-stepped, but it cannot be validated on hardware and no bitstream should be built on its account. The converse also holds: a board result cannot confirm a model fix, and the two instruments answer different questions. On this design the board has a real part that tri-states when deselected, so a class of model defect cannot exist there at all."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/build_tinygba.sh`: the synthesis source list and its single `diag/sdram_probe.v` entry"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/build_gba_sim.sh`: `sdram_gba.v`, `gba_sdram_rtl.v` and `sdram_model.v` in the Verilator list"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: the `TINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` gate around the `gba_sdram_rtl` instance"
  verification: "Confirmed 2026-11-08 by listing the `diag/` files each script references and by grepping the synthesis script for the model's filename, which appears only inside a comment in `sdram_gba.v`. No build was run to establish this."

- record_id: BRD-037
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The acknowledgement change was a reach regression measured as a fix, and entry 36 is withdrawn"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "The commit that moved the 32-bit read acknowledgement to the high-halfword collect reduced how far the machine gets and was reported as a repair because the reads it did perform were correct. Four configurations were built from the same tree and measured at 3000 million steps on `test_logo` under `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL`, scored with `tools/check_cartread.py`. The die-selection fix alone (`cf240f8`) reaches `0x080002e4`, issues the literal-pool load at `0x080002b0`, and reports 451 of 462 reads matching with 11 failures. Adding the controller acknowledgement change with the output mux untouched leaves that identical at 451 of 462, so it moves nothing measurable alone. Adding also the wide output mux that drove both `rom_data` and `ram_rdata` on the acknowledgement cycle cuts reach to `0x080000de`, removes the pool load entirely, and reports 448 of 448 reads correct -- which is the figure entry 36 called a fix. Narrowing that mux to `ram_rdata` alone restores reach to `0x080002e4` and the 11 failures return. Tying the mux to `cpu_en` instead cuts reach to `0x080000de` again. The restored tree is byte-identical to `cf240f8` by `git diff`, and reproduces the baseline exactly."
  consequence: "Entry 36 and BRD-036 overstate the result and must not be cited as a read-timing fix: the wide mux clobbered the instruction-fetch path, so the machine stopped earlier and its surviving reads were clean by construction. The BIOS logo that rendered in that run rendered because the fetch path still worked up to that point. What stands from the cycle is the pin-level model's deselected-die defect, which took reads from 112 of 4,000 to 753 of 764 and removed a 3,881-fold retry loop at `0x0800009c`, and which was verified before the acknowledgement change existed. The eleven literal-pool word reads still return the previous word and their cause is not established; three timing attempts have now failed against three different confounds. Wording rule for any future fix: report reach and read accuracy together, and treat a run that is correct because it stopped earlier as a regression, not a repair."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/run_base/bs.log`: the restored baseline, 462 reads, 451 matching, reach `0x080002e4`"
    - "This repository, `build/run_va/va.log` and `build/run_n2/n2.log`: the controller-ack-only and data-port-narrowed configurations, both 451 of 462"
    - "This repository, `build/run_ack/ak.log`: the configuration entry 36 described, 448 of 448, reach `0x080000de`, no pool load"
    - "This repository, `build/run_cen/cn.log` and `build/run_ex/ex.log`: the `cpu_en`-tied variants, both stopping at `0x080000de`"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08 by four builds and four 3000-million-step runs, all scored with the same calibrated column. `git diff cf240f8 -- fpga/gba/src/memory/` on the restored tree returns nothing, and the restored baseline's read counts and reach reproduce the controller-only run exactly."

- record_id: TCL-027
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A fix must be checked for reach as well as correctness, because stopping earlier makes the survivors look right"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "A change that reduces how far a program gets will appear to improve any metric computed over the instructions it does execute. Here, driving both output ports on the SDRAM acknowledgement cycle corrupted instruction fetches, so `test_logo` stopped 28 bytes earlier and never issued its literal-pool load; every one of the 448 reads it still performed was correct, giving a 448-of-448 result that read as a complete fix while the machine had actually regressed from reaching `0x080002e4` to `0x080000de`. The same tree without that mux, or with the mux narrowed to the data port, reported 451 of 462 with 11 failures and greater reach. The two metrics disagree in opposite directions, and only the pair distinguishes a repair from a truncation."
  consequence: "Report reach and accuracy together for any change to a boot path, and treat a reduction in reach as disqualifying regardless of how clean the surviving measurements are. On this project the concrete form is the highest cartridge address reached plus the read accuracy from `tools/check_cartread.py`, both quoted in every result: `0x080002e4` with 451 of 462 is strictly better than `0x080000de` with 448 of 448, because the first is still executing the entry code and the second has stopped in the middle of it. This is the same class as the earlier rule about runs that end before their budget, applied to a different axis."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/run_ack/ak.log` against `build/run_va/va.log` and `build/run_base/bs.log`"
    - "This repository, core-log entry 37, which records the isolation by four builds"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: the wide mux that drove `rom_data` as well as `ram_rdata` on the acknowledgement cycle"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08: the same ROM at the same 3000-million-step budget reported 448 of 448 correct with reach `0x080000de` in one configuration and 451 of 462 with reach `0x080002e4` in another, and the higher-accuracy result was the broken one."

- record_id: BRD-038
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The remaining word-read defect is independent of the clk16/clk67 phase across all twelve offsets"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "The eleven remaining cartridge read failures under `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` are not an artefact of the simulator's clock phase. `TINYGBA_CLK16_SHIFT` was swept across all twelve offsets the harness supports -- a twelve-step counter where `clk16` spans six steps and `clk67` spans three, so each offset moves the `clk16` edge relative to `clk67` by one step -- with `test_logo` at 3000 million steps per run. Every one of the twelve produced 864 reads matching the ROM, 11 failing, a highest cartridge address of `0x080002e4`, and a cartridge fetch cycle count of 149,742,181. All twelve runs printed their `Total frames` summary, and phase 0, which is the default, reproduces the baseline exactly. The eleven failures are exclusively 32-bit word reads, 11 of 532, with every narrower width clean; each returns the word belonging to the previous transaction, and repeated reads of the same address converge on the correct value."
  consequence: "The clk16-to-clk67 relationship is excluded as a cause for this defect, so it is deterministic logic in `sdram_gba`'s collection or `gba_memory`'s assembly rather than a timing coincidence the simulator imposes. This closes, for the memory path, a question entry 10 opened and entry 19's twelve-offset sweep answered only for a standalone raster model. The consequence for method is that further timing changes are not indicated: the signature is the first 32-bit word read of each distinct address returning its predecessor while narrower reads of the same device succeed, which points at how `cpu_rdata` is assembled across the two halfword collects rather than at when the acknowledgement arrives relative to either clock. Wording rule: this is a model result and says nothing about the physical board, which BRD-033 still governs."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/phase/p1` through `build/phase/p11/r.log`, each a full 3000-million-step run at one `TINYGBA_CLK16_SHIFT` value, plus `build/phase/p0/r.log` for the default"
    - "This repository, `tools/score_phases.py`, which reports per-phase accuracy and reach"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/sim_main.cpp`: `clk16_shift_get()` and the twelve-step `clk16_level()` counter"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08 across twelve completed runs, each confirmed to have printed its `Total frames` summary before any number was read. Every phase reported 864/11 at reach `0x080002e4`; phase 0 matched the independently produced baseline in `build/run_base/bs.log`."

- record_id: TCL-028
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Background jobs launched with `&` die with the launching shell; detach them with `setsid`"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "A simulation launched as `( cd dir && CMD ... ) &` from a shell that then exits does not survive: the child is killed when its parent shell session ends, and its redirected log stops mid-line with no summary. Measured 2026-11-08 on a twelve-run phase sweep: the first attempt launched eleven runs this way and all eleven were dead within sixty seconds, leaving logs of 70 and 136 lines that contained no `Total frames` line. Relaunching the identical commands as `setsid nohup env ... &` kept all eleven alive for the full eighteen minutes each needed and all twelve runs (including the single sequential one) completed and printed their summaries."
  consequence: "Launch long runs with `setsid` and verify completion by counting summary lines before reading any number out of them, because a truncated log is indistinguishable from a short result at a glance and produced a wrong reading of a whole sweep in this project. This pairs with the existing rules about run length and about reading only completed runs: those govern how long to run, this governs whether the run survived. A sweep in particular must be checked run by run, since a phase that stops early looks exactly like a phase that reached a different conclusion."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/phase/p*/r.log` from the first attempt: 70 and 136 lines, zero summary lines, all processes gone within a minute"
    - "This repository, `build/phase/p*/r.log` from the `setsid` attempt: all twelve with `Total frames` and identical fetch counts"
    - "This repository, `tools/score_phases.py`, which was extended to report reach so a truncated run is visible as a low reach rather than read as a result"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08: eleven processes alive at fifteen and at seventy-five seconds after a `setsid` launch, versus zero within sixty seconds of the plain `&` launch, with the logs differing only in completeness."

- record_id: BRD-039
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "LEAD: the CPU consumes a 32-bit cartridge read one cycle before the controller's word is complete, and cpu_en is not cpu_ready"
  status: LEAD
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "A consumer-side trace across the literal-pool block shows why every acknowledgement change in this session had no effect. Each 32-bit cartridge data read takes four cycles through `MAIN`, `REQ1_WAIT`, `REQ2_START` and `REQ2_WAIT`. At the `REQ2_WAIT` cycle where `cpu_en` is high, the mux presents the buffered word from the previous transaction while the controller is presenting the low-halfword splice: for `0x080002b0` at cycle 76106114 the state is `REQ2_WAIT`, `cpu_en` is 1, `sdram_rdata` holds `e1540400`, `ram_rdata` holds `e154e154`, and the correct `08000400` appears only on the next cycle at 76106115, in `MAIN`, with `cpu_en` 0. The shape repeats on every pool read in the 140-cycle window. The controlling detail is the enable decode: `cpu_en` for a concurrent rom-and-ram access is `state == REQ2_BRAM | (state == REQ2_START | state == REQ2_WAIT) & sdram_ready`, which does not reference `cpu_ready`. Moving `cpu_ready` in `sdram_gba` therefore cannot move the cycle on which `cpu_en` asserts once the FSM is returning to `MAIN`."
  consequence: "This is recorded as a lead rather than a finding because the mechanism is established but no fix has been applied or measured. It does account for all three failed attempts in this session with one cause: the acknowledgement change left the eleven failures untouched, widening the output mux fixed reads by breaking fetches, and tying the mux to `cpu_en` truncated the machine, because each was aimed at `cpu_ready` rather than at the enable. The candidate repair is to register `sdram_ready` and add a `MAIN`-state term to the concurrent rom-and-ram branch of `cpu_en`, applying only to 32-bit accesses so the 16-bit path that already works is not delayed. Before that is attempted, trace a 16-bit data read in the same window to confirm it completes in one collect."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/run_pool/pt.log`: 140 consecutive cycles from the first 32-bit read of `0x080002b0`, printing state, enables, `sdram_rdata`, `ram_rdata_buf` and `ram_rdata`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: the concurrent rom-and-ram branch of `cpu_en` and the output mux's `state == MAIN` guard"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v`: the low-halfword continuation that acks a 32-bit read, and the `{cpu_rdata[31:16], dq_in}` composition it produces"
  verification: "Not verified. The trace is from one completed 3000-million-step run whose summary was confirmed before reading it, and the cycle numbers are reproducible, but no change has been built against it and the proposed repair is untested."

- record_id: TCL-029
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A trace that arms on an address must also check that the address is inside the image"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "A consumer-side trace was armed on a 16-bit cartridge data read with the condition `ram_be == 4'b0011` and `ram_addr[15:0] >= 16'h02b0`, and it fired on `0x08008c20`, which is offset 35,872 into a 4,096-byte cartridge. The window it captured is therefore an out-of-range access whose returned value is meaningless either way, so it could not distinguish a working 16-bit path from a broken one, and the comparison it was built for was not obtained. The run itself was valid -- 3000 million steps, `Total frames` summary confirmed before reading anything -- so the fault is entirely in the arming condition, which tested the low 16 bits of an address without testing the high bits that identify the region or the image bound."
  consequence: "An address-selective trace must bound the address on both ends and check the region nibble, because the CPU does make out-of-range accesses and one of them will be the first match. On this design the region is `ram_addr[27:24]` and the image bound for the test cartridge is offset 0x1000; a condition that omits either will eventually fire on a garbage access and produce a window that looks like data. The concrete lesson from this cycle is that the comparison did not need a new trace at all: `tools/check_cartread.py` already scores 55 low and 55 high halfword reads as correct against the 32-bit path's 11 failures, so the width asymmetry was measured before the trace was written. Check whether an existing capture already answers the question before building an instrument to answer it."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/run_hw/hw.log`: the 24-cycle window armed at `0x08008c20`, with its `Total frames` summary present"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: the arming condition, since reverted"
    - "This repository, `tools/check_cartread.py` output on `build/run_base/bs.log`: 55 low and 55 high halfwords correct, 11 of 532 word reads failing"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08: the image is 4,096 bytes (`ls -l` on `build/test/test_logo.gba`), the armed address is offset 35,872, and the captured window shows an acknowledgement of an out-of-range access. The existing scored capture independently shows the halfword path correct in bulk."

- record_id: BRD-040
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "test_logo does not stop: eight seconds after release the CPU is in the blink delay loop, past the DISPCNT<=0x0403 store"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "`build/stl_s5` (`TINYGBA_FETCHTRACE` plus `TINYGBA_STALLTRACE`, layout `v5`, `.fs` SHA-256 `04dc4311896e5aab122f22b39d38c00f027fa6ae60948eb80301d2abef861aa8`) keeps the last sixteen completed CPU memory cycles and freezes them after 2^22 clk16 with no completed cycle or 2^27 clk16 after release. With `test_logo.gba`, three freshly flashed loads decoded identically: the stall trigger never fired, the time trigger froze the ring after exactly 26,863,572 completed cycles, the longest gap ended by a completed cycle was 276,009 clk16, and tens of millions of further cycles completed after the freeze. The sixteen-cycle tail is the cartridge's `delay` loop, `0x080002A4` `E2544001` `subs r4, r4, #1`, `0x080002A8` `1AFFFFFD` `bne delay` and the prefetched `0x080002AC` `EAFFFFED` `b blink`, each fetch equal to the ROM word. At the freeze the memory FSM is moving between `MAIN` and `REQ1_WAIT` on cartridge fetches, `dma_on` is low, all DMA channels are idle and the BIOS intro's FIFO-B sound DMA has stopped at 29,352 beats."
  consequence: "The `delay` loop is reached only through the straight-line code after the ROM, IWRAM, EWRAM and VRAM checks, the background fill, the column loop and the `strh` of `0x0403` to `DISPCNT`, so on this image the CPU executes past the point BRD-023 and BRD-025 inferred it never reached. Those records inferred it from a sticky `DISPCNT<=0x0403` bit and the drawer's register copy on older images; this instrument does not show whether the store lands in `DISPCNT`, so the display fault, if it persists, is after the CPU and not a CPU wedge. Do not build another tail instrument for `test_logo.gba`; an overlay-off image of the current tree is the direct test of whether the blink square is drawn."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/stl_cap/logo1/decode.txt`, `logo2/decode.txt` and `logo3/decode.txt`, decoded by `tools/decode_fetchtrace.py --layout v5 --expect build/test/test_logo.gba`"
    - "This repository, `build/test/test.s`: the `blink` and `delay` loops after the `ldr r0, =0x0403; strh r0, [r11]` store"
    - "This repository, the STALLTRACE recorder in `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08 on the board, three loads each preceded by a fresh flash, every page assembled from at least nine frames with the first frame after each page change dropped; the marker `0x57A1` was present and the three decodes agree word for word."

- record_id: BRD-041
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Super Mario Advance does not stall and the DMA REQ1_START deadlock is not observed: at eight seconds the CPU is in the BIOS sound mixer with the memory FSM in MAIN and DMA idle"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "On `build/stl_s5` with Super Mario Advance, three freshly flashed loads decoded identically. The stall trigger (2^22 clk16 with no completed CPU memory cycle) never fired; the time trigger froze the ring after exactly 17,459,560 completed cycles, the longest gap ended by a completed cycle was 327,688 clk16, which is a halted frame, and cycles kept completing after the freeze. At the freeze the `gba_memory` FSM is in `MAIN` with `dma_on_r`, `double_req` and `dma_sdram` low, `dma_on` and `dma_ena` low, `cpu_en` high, `halt` low, no `sdram_ready` pulse since the last completed cycle, the DMA arbiter idle with every channel's module idle, and 126,524 DMA read and write beats completed. The sixteen-cycle tail is BIOS code: the resampling mix loop at `0x000020A0` to `0x000020CC` (`strb r0, [r5, #0x630]`, `ldrb r0, [r5]`, `strb r0, [r5], #1`, `subs r8, r8, #1`), which stores bytes to IWRAM `0x03004532` and reads and writes `0x03003F02`, then `b 0x2004` into the pitch-step compare at `0x00002004` to `0x0000200C`. After the freeze the live DMA beat counters keep rising, with the last DMA read from IWRAM `0x030049D8` and the last write to `0x040000A4`, FIFO B, and live samples show `halt` high between bursts."
  consequence: "The lead that a DMA SDRAM access completes in `REQ1_START` and leaves the FSM waiting in `REQ1_WAIT` with `cpu_en` held low does not describe this board run: no stall of even a quarter second occurs within about twenty seconds of release, and at the sampled moment the FSM and DMA are idle. Super Mario Advance's machine is alive and running the BIOS sound driver, not frozen; BRD-029's `halt=1` and frozen cartridge-fetch count are consistent with a CPU that has left the cartridge for BIOS code and halts between interrupts. The `REQ1_START` hop remains a code-reading observation with no board symptom, and the `cpu_en` repair of entries 39 and 40 has no board fault to address."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/stl_cap/sma1/decode.txt`, `sma2/decode.txt` and `sma3/decode.txt`, decoded by `tools/decode_fetchtrace.py --layout v5 --expect build/test/roms/sma.gba`"
    - "This repository, `build/test/official_bios.hex` disassembled with capstone at `0x1F80` to `0x20E0`"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-08 on the board, three loads each preceded by a fresh flash; the three decodes agree to the cycle, the marker `0x57A1` was present, and every snapshot and counter flop was confirmed as a `DFFRE` with logic-driven D or CE in `build/stl_s1/tinygba.json`."

- record_id: BRD-042
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "LEAD: Super Mario Advance leaves the cartridge for the BIOS and its own IRQ handler is never entered"
  status: LEAD
  verified_date: 2026-11-08
  statement: "A static reading of Super Mario Advance's `crt0`, not a measurement, shows `0x080000D8` to `0x080000EC` loading `0x03007FFC`, storing `0x080000FC` (an ARM IRQ handler in cartridge ROM) to it, then `bx` to Thumb `AgbMain` at `0x08000281`. On the board the CPU makes exactly 84 cartridge instruction fetches (BRD-029) and is then measured alive in BIOS code with the BIOS sound mixer running and FIFO-B sound DMA active (BRD-041). If an interrupt were dispatched through the game's handler pointer, each would fetch from `0x080000FC` and raise the cartridge-fetch count, so either no interrupt is dispatched to it or the pointer the BIOS reads is not the one the game stored. Separately, `0x080000FC` is exactly the DMA3 source recorded in BRD-025 for `DMA3CNT <= 0x85001F00`, and sound DMA keeps running under the game but stops under `test_logo.gba`."
  consequence: "This is a lead because no control-flow transition has been captured: the instrument froze at a fixed time and saw only the mixer. The next measurement is the cartridge-to-BIOS transition itself and every exception-vector fetch with the handler pointer the BIOS IRQ stub reads from `0x03FFFFFC`, which would also show whether the BRD-025 DMA3 entry is really the `crt0` store of `0x080000FC` seen through the MMIO ring."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/test/roms/sma.gba` disassembled with capstone at `0x080000C0` to `0x08000340` and its literals at `0x08000270` and `0x08000274`"
    - "This repository, records BRD-025, BRD-029 and BRD-041"
  verification: "Not verified. The crt0 reading is static, and the coincidence of addresses and the sound-DMA difference are observations that no captured transition yet explains."

- record_id: BRD-043
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The cartridge's `DISPCNT <= 0x0403` store is issued with 0xFFE0, so the display is never enabled"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-12
  statement: "`build/v6_s9` (`TINYGBA_FETCHTRACE` + `TINYGBA_STALLTRACE`, layout `v6`, `.fs` SHA-256 `71324ac4442c8ac01a80efbaaf1f1c81c2bed7ea3e4a94b88dc0a63b13fd36d6`) adds sticky records of every write to `0x04000000` and of the DISPCNT value the GPU drawer registers. With `test_logo.gba`, five page-3 frames spread over eight seconds agree on every field: 213 writes to `0x04000000`, **zero of them carrying `0x0403`**, `last_dc_data=0xFFE0`, and the bus saw `0x0000=1`, `0x0403=0`, `0xFFE0=1`. The drawer meanwhile holds forced blank with BG0..BG3 all enabled, and its sticky states read: cartridge entry value `0x0000` seen, display-on value `0x0403` **never seen**, BIOS handover value `0x1F80` seen. The two writes the cartridge makes to `0x04000000` are therefore `0x0000` and then `0xFFE0`, not `0x0403`; `0xFFE0` has bit 7 (forced blank) and bits 8..11 (BG0..BG3) set, which is exactly the drawer state observed and produces the white backdrop that entry 42 saw. BRD-040 independently measured this same cartridge executing the blink loop past the store, so the store is issued and the CPU is past it; what is wrong is the data it carries."
  consequence: "This is the fault the project has been circling since BRD-022, stated on the board for the first time with both ends of the chain measured in one image: the store is issued, the drawer never receives `0x0403`, and the value it receives instead is `0xFFE0`. It also withdraws BRD-023's withdrawn lead. Ordering the ring entry in BRD-023 that carried `ram_addr=0x04000000`, `ram_wdata=0xFFE0FFE0` was correct data seen through a broken packing, and entry 27 was right to withdraw it only because the *packing* was wrong, not because the observation was. Do not look for the fault between the CPU and the register: the register is faithfully storing what the bus delivered, and the bus delivered the wrong word."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/v6_s9/` — gates, routed netlist and bitstream, seed 9"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: `s_dc`, `last_dc_data`, `dc403_n`, `s_gpu0`/`s_gpu403`/`s_gpu_bios`"
    - "This repository, `tools/decode_fetchtrace.py --layout v6`, page 3 entries 3..4"
    - "Captures `/tmp/v6/f04.png` through `/tmp/v6/f31.png`, 2026-11-12, MJPEG pinned at 1920x1080 per BRD-012"
  verification: "Observed on the board 2026-11-12 after flashing `build/v6_s9` one-wire and streaming `build/test/test_logo.gba` over `/dev/ttyUSB1`. Five page-3 frames, each classified by its page strip first (TCL-019), agree field for field; all 16 new v6 registers were confirmed present and driven in the routed netlist before the load (`tools/gatelevel_report.py`, 0 undriven bits) and the flip-flop count rose by 116 against an expected 115."

- record_id: BRD-044
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The 0x0403 store carried 0xFFE0 because the literal load returned the preceding pool word"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "On a v7 placement whose `clk67` sits only on the measured-good spines (`build/v7_s13`, `.fs` SHA-256 `9d33814de7a0da70066b5f836f276652edb4de395b50bfdef04b47a83bb18b57`), the 32-bit cartridge data read of `0x080002E0` returns `0x0000FFE0` -- the word that lives at `0x080002DC` -- instead of the `0x00000403` the image holds there, while the reads of `0x080002DC` and `0x080002E4` both return their correct words. The record is direct rather than inferred: layout v7 latches `ram_rdata` on the read itself, the byte enables are `1111`, the read count for `0x2E0` is 1, and two page-3 frames captured seconds apart agree field for field. Three of four fresh loads then carry that value the whole way, which is what closes the chain BRD-043 left open: the read returns `0xFFE0`, the `strh` to `0x04000000` puts `0xFFE0` on the bus (sticky `0xFFE0` seen, `0x0403` never seen), and the drawer's own DISPCNT copy settles at forced blank with BG0..BG3 set. The one load that did not agree wrote `0xFEE0`, which is `0xFFE0` with bit 8 clear and is a value that appears nowhere in the cartridge image; it is recorded under BRD-048's neighbour note rather than folded in. The lag is not confined to the one address: a tail-ring fetch of `0x0800028C` returned `0xEAFFFFED`, the word belonging to `0x080002AC`, giving 7 of 8 exact against the image at reach `0x080002AC`."
  consequence: "The one-word read lag BRD-038 and BRD-039 characterised in the Verilator model is now measured on the physical board and is the reason the display never left forced blank, so the parked `cpu_en` repair of entries 39 and 40 is unblocked and becomes the next change. It must be required to show both properties together per TCL-027 -- the white field gone and reach undiminished -- because stopping the machine earlier would make the survivors look right. The park's original justification, that the board showed no read fault for it to address, no longer holds. Separate from the lag and not explained by it, one load of four lost bit 8 between a correct `0xFFE0` return and the bus, so a load-dependent data-bit fault exists on the read-to-store path and no change aimed at the transaction ordering addresses it; that needs its own instrument and must not be conflated with this repair."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/v7_s13/` (gates, `clock-plane.log`) and `build/deploy_v7s13/` (`SHA256SUMS`)"
    - "Captures `/tmp/v7s13/F3.png` and `/tmp/v7s13/F5.png` (both page 3, agreeing), `/tmp/v7s13/L2_*`, `L3_*`, `L4_*` (three further loads), `/tmp/v7s13/P0_5.png` (tail ring)"
    - "This repository, `tools/decode_fetchtrace.py`: `decode_v7_pool()` and the entry-5/6 field order, re-derived against `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv` slots `5'd20`..`5'd27`"
    - "This repository, `build/test/test_logo.gba`: `0x2DC`=`0x0000FFE0`, `0x2E0`=`0x00000403`, `0x2E4`=`0x00007FFF`"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 on the board: four fresh loads of `build/deploy_v7s13` with `test_logo.gba`, page 3 captured and decoded each time. Loads 2, 3 and 4 agree completely, reading `0x0000FFE0` for `0x080002E0`, writing `0xFFE0` to `0x04000000`, and leaving the drawer at `0xFFE0`; load 1 read the same `0xFFE0` but its store carried `0xFEE0`. Every pool word was scored against the cartridge image rather than against a constant compiled into the instrument (TCL-024), and reach plus accuracy were read together (TCL-027)."


- record_id: BRD-045
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Super Mario Advance wedges in REQ1_WAIT behind a DMA3 IWRAM write, and makes no progress at all"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-12
  statement: "On `build/v6_s9` with Super Mario Advance (`build/test/roms/sma.gba`, SHA-256 `d29dec02caacbf449e2a93c6042258b0669542e102eb0e6fd0eb92a5411e0244`), the stall trigger fired rather than the time trigger: with no completed CPU memory cycle for 2^22 clk16 the ring froze and the snapshot read `gba_memory` FSM state 2 (`REQ1_WAIT`), `dma_on_r=1`, `double_req=1`, `dma_sdram=1`, `dma_on`/`dma_ena` high, `dma_rnw=0` (a write), `cpu_en` low, `rom_en` and `ram_cen` low, `halt` low, `dma_addr=0x03001190`, `ram_addr=0x03007DEC` with SDRAM byte address `0x000000FC`, the DMA arbiter switched to channel 3 with channel 3 on and allowed, and **zero `sdram_ready` pulses since the FSM last changed state, with `clk16` in that state saturated at 65535**. The freeze happened after 9,309,508 completed cycles and 102,825 DMA read beats / 102,824 write beats. Three page-3 frames spanning six seconds all read `completed CPU cycles after freeze = 0`, so the machine is not merely slow: it has stopped."
  consequence: "This is the DMA `REQ1_START` defect that entry 14 recorded as a code reading and BRD-041 reported as unobserved, now measured on the board: a DMA SDRAM access leaves the FSM waiting in `REQ1_WAIT` with `cpu_en` held low and no acknowledgement, and the CPU never completes another cycle. It does not contradict BRD-041, whose three loads ran on to eight seconds and never stalled; it establishes that the machine is bimodal across loads of one fixed bitstream, which is BRD-014's finding recurring -- one load hangs in the first second, another runs for twenty. Any future attempt to reproduce this must take several loads and say which behaviour was seen, because a single capture is a coin flip. `dma_addr=0x03001190` is the destination BRD-025 and BRD-042 record for the DMA3 copy from `0x080000FC`, so the deadlock is on the cartridge's own self-copy, which is the first thing `crt0` does."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/v6_s9/` — seed 9, gates clean, zero hold violations"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: the v6 freeze snapshot and the live counters on page 3"
    - "Captures `/tmp/sma/f06.png`, `f16.png` and `f17.png`, 2026-11-12"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: the `REQ1_WAIT` branch that waits on `sdram_ready`"
  verification: "Observed on the board 2026-11-12, one load, three page-3 frames over six seconds agreeing exactly and all reading zero completed cycles after the freeze."

- record_id: BRD-046
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Super Mario Advance never fetches 0x080000FC, and the BIOS IRQ stub reads 0 from 0x03FFFFFC"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-12
  statement: "This answers BRD-042 with a measurement instead of a static reading. On `build/v6_s9` with Super Mario Advance, the layout-v6 sticky records read: cartridge fetch seen since the last BIOS fetch = 1 with cartridge-to-BIOS transitions = 0, so after the BIOS hands over, no BIOS-region instruction fetch follows a cartridge fetch at all; the IRQ vector at `0x00000018` and the FIQ vector at `0x0000001C` were fetched, and the SWI vector at `0x00000008` was not; the BIOS IRQ stub's read of the handler pointer at `0x03FFFFFC` happened (255 reads, the counter saturating) and the last word it read was `0x00000000`; and **`0x080000FC` was never fetched**. The last DMA read address recorded at the same time was `0x080000FC` with the last DMA write to `0x03007BFC`, and the cartridge fetch count was 84, reproducing BRD-029 exactly. On `test_logo.gba` the same build reads `0x080000FC` fetched = 1, which is shown as a prefetch rather than an execution because the ARM7TDMI prefetches past a taken branch and that address lies inside the test cartridge's literal pool."
  consequence: "BRD-042's two open alternatives are both closed for this cartridge: no interrupt is dispatched through the game's handler, and the pointer the BIOS reads is zero rather than the game's stored value, so the game's ARM IRQ handler at `0x080000FC` is never entered and is never even fetched. The `0x080000FC` coincidence with the BRD-025 DMA3 source is confirmed from the other side, since the last DMA read address is that word. Note the 255 saturating reads of `0x03FFFFFC` all happen during the BIOS intro, before handover, so a zero handler pointer there is the BIOS's own default and says nothing about what the game stored; the absence of any cartridge-to-BIOS transition afterwards means the BIOS never looks again on this load. Because BRD-045 shows this load dies in the first second, do not conclude that interrupts are never dispatched in a load that survives; the measurement describes a run that stopped."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/v6_s9/`, page 3 entries 3..4 decoded by `tools/decode_fetchtrace.py --layout v6`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: `s_xfer`, `xfer_n`, `s_vec8`/`s_vec18`/`s_vec1c`, `s_hnd`, `hnd_val`, `s_cfc`"
    - "This repository, records BRD-042 and BRD-045"
  verification: "Observed on the board 2026-11-12 across three page-3 frames that agree field for field, with every v6 register confirmed present and driven in the routed netlist before the load."

- record_id: TCL-030
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A diagnostic packing assigned to a plain register truncates as silently as a part-select one"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-12
  statement: "`tools/check_ring_width.py` checked only concatenations assigned through a `+:` part-select, which is the form the ring and the MMIO ring use. The readout muxes assign instead to a plain 32-bit register with a `case` label in front, as in `5'd20: stl_word_mux = { ... };`, and that form truncates exactly as silently: a 33-bit concatenation assigned to a 32-bit reg drops its top bit with no error and no warning at this flow's verbosity. A check for the plain form and for whole-identifier assignments (`name = other;`, where the widths must match) was added after this form was found to be unchecked, and it immediately found one pre-existing slip: the page-1 status word packed `{26'h0, s_dc, mm_wrapped, mm_n}`, 33 bits into 32. That one was harmless because the bit dropped was padding, and it is now written as `25'h0` so the gate can measure it. The extended gate checks 51 plain assignments on `gbatang_top.sv` where it previously checked none."
  consequence: "Both additions were mutation-tested before being trusted: widening a padding field to make 33 bits into 32 reports `concatenation for 'stl_word_mux' is 33 bits, declared 32 bits` and exits 1, and assigning an 8-bit register to a 32-bit one reports the width mismatch, while an unchanged tree still exits 0. A gate that only covers the syntax its author happened to be using is worth very little, and this one had been covering roughly half of the diagnostic packings in this file while reporting a clean pass."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/check_ring_width.py`: `PLAIN`, `PLAIN_IDENT` and `check_plain()`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: the `tr_word_mux` and `stl_word_mux` readout muxes"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-12: the gate reports 5 part-select packings, 2 strides and 51 plain assignments checked, and both new failure modes reproduce under mutation while the unmodified tree passes."

- record_id: BRD-047
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "LEAD: a v7 placement whose clk67 took SPINE0/TOP0 executed a different program, while the v6 bitstream on the same board did not"
  status: LEAD
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "`build/v7_s9` (`TINYGBA_FETCHTRACE` + `TINYGBA_STALLTRACE`, layout `v7`, `.fs` SHA-256 `0d3d58da1d022a1cdf1e5f918c5ec83065aac6d3cace16a5362a00374a1aad86`) passed every build gate with zero hold violations, but on the board it behaved unlike any prior image. Three fresh loads all agreed: 32,192,194 completed CPU cycles before the freeze, 211 writes to `0x04000000`, the last carrying `0x0080`, none carrying `0xFFE0` or `0x0403`, and a tail ring showing the CPU looping at `0x0800020C` to `0x0800025C` rather than in the blink loop `stl_s5` and `v6_s9` both measured. Five page-0 frames agreed field for field, so this was not a torn capture. Scoring those fetches against the cartridge image exposed one systematic error: five of nine differed from the ROM, and every difference had byte 2 incremented by one (`0xE3A07001` read as `0xE3A17001`, `0xE1A07817` as `0xE1A17817`, `0xE11A0007` as `0xE11B0007`, `0xE2888001` as `0xE2898001`), while the other four were exact. Re-flashing `build/deploy_v6/tinygba.fs` on the same board with the same cartridge immediately restored the correct blink loop and byte-exact words, so the difference is the bitstream and not the board state, the loader, or the capture path. The one build-visible difference found is the clock plane: `clk67` on `v7_s9` is carried by `SPINE0/TOP0` as well as the two measured-good spines, and `SPINE0/TOP0` has no record on this board, whereas `v6_s9`'s `clk67` sits only on `SPINE16/TOP0` and `SPINE25/TOP1`, both measured good. `clk16` differs too, `v7_s9` omitting `SPINE0/TOP3` and `SPINE1/TOP3`, but every prior image has sat on unmeasured `clk16` pips and none behaved like this."
  consequence: "This is recorded as a lead and not as a finding, and the v7 reading is withdrawn rather than interpreted: no result from `v7_s9` may be used to confirm or refute BRD-044, and the pool-read records it produced (all three reads absent) describe a run that was not executing the cartridge correctly. If it holds, it is the strongest instance of BRD-019 yet, because it separates two bitstreams of nearly the same netlist with a purely placement-level difference, and it predicts that the same source placed at a seed whose `clk67` stays on the two measured-good spines will read correctly. The test is a v7 seed whose clock-plane report shows `clk67` confined to `SPINE16/TOP0` and `SPINE25/TOP1`; if that build reads the pool correctly the cause is the spine, and if it repeats this behaviour the cause is in the v7 logic instead and the search moves to the added comparators. Seeds 10 to 16 of this netlist were still routing when the session ended and none of them was inspected for its clock plane."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/v7_s9/` (gates, `clock-plane.log`) and `build/deploy_v6/` (the control image)"
    - "This repository, `build/v7_s9/clock-plane.log`: `clk67 SPINE0/TOP0=unmeasured, SPINE16/TOP0=good, SPINE25/TOP1=good`"
    - "This repository, `build/v6_s9/clock-plane.log`: `clk67 SPINE16/TOP0=good, SPINE25/TOP1=good` with no `SPINE0/TOP0`"
    - "Captures `/tmp/v7/f01.png` to `/tmp/v7/f17.png`, `/tmp/v7/L2/`, `/tmp/v7/L3/`, and the control `/tmp/ab/`"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 by A/B on one board within one session: three loads of the v7 image agreeing, scored against `build/test/test_logo.gba`, then a re-flash of the v6 image restoring correct behaviour. The cause is not established, because no v7 placement with a clean `clk67` plane was available to test. Tested 2026-11-13: `build/v7_s13` satisfies the condition (`clk67` only on `SPINE16/TOP0` and `SPINE25/TOP1`) and runs the cartridge correctly, returning the blink loop at `0x080002AC` with 7 of 8 tail-ring fetches exact, so the prediction held; it remains a lead rather than a finding because `v7_s13` also differs from `v7_s9` in `clk16`, `clk50` and `hclk`, and `build/v7_s18` shows that one of those differences alone can kill the picture (BRD-048).


- record_id: BRD-048
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "A placement whose hclk has no measured-good spine produces a bitstream whose HDMI does not sync at all"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "`build/v7_s18` passed every build gate -- zero hold violations, `clk16` 18.891, `clk50` 79.064, `clk67` 33.675 under the documented GBATang allowance and `hclk` 99.532 MHz -- and its `clk67` satisfies BRD-047's test condition exactly, sitting only on `SPINE16/TOP0` and `SPINE25/TOP1`, both measured good. On the board it produces no picture at all: the operator reported the HDMI link as unsynchronised, and the capture card returned a pinned 1920x1080 frame that is pure black with 255 unique colours, which is the fallback mode BRD-012 records rather than a machine drawing nothing. `tools/clock_plane_select.py` shows why it is the one packed seed of seven with that property: `hclk` on `v7_s18` crosses only `SPINE16/BOTTOM2` and `SPINE24/BOTTOM2`, neither of which this board has ever measured, while every other packed placement of this netlist -- `v7_s11`, `v7_s13`, `v7_s14`, `v7_s15`, `v7_s17`, `v7_s24` -- and both images that have ever displayed, `v6_s9` and `v7_s9`, carry `hclk` across at least one measured-good `BOTTOM2` pip (`SPINE19` and/or `SPINE26`). Re-flashing `build/deploy_v7s13`, whose `clk67` is the same two good spines but whose `hclk` includes `SPINE19/BOTTOM2` and `SPINE26/BOTTOM2`, restores sync immediately: 2,461 unique colours with the grey pillarbox and the trace's dark-blue bit cells, and the cartridge then runs its blink loop."
  consequence: "`hclk` is the 74.25 MHz pixel clock, so a placement can be electrically clean, meet every gate, and still produce a board that shows nothing -- and BRD-019's measured-good table is the reason it can be predicted before flashing. The selection rule for any board image therefore cannot key on `clk67` alone, which is the cartridge clock and decides whether the program runs, nor on a clean hold count: it must require that `hclk` retains at least one measured-good `BOTTOM2` pip, because that is the clock the video path lives on. `tools/clock_plane_select.py` is the tool for this and compares every clock against a reference netlist at once, rather than the one clock a reader happened to be thinking about; that narrow habit is what produced this cycle's wasted flash. This is the second instance of a placement-level difference separating a working board from a dead one (BRD-047 is the first) and it strengthens BRD-019 from a wording rule into a build-time filter that is worth enforcing."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/v7_s18/clock-plane.log` and `build/v7_s13/clock-plane.log`"
    - "This repository, `tools/clock_plane_select.py`, which prints each clock's pip set against the reference and reports the measured-good state of every pip"
    - "Captures `/tmp/v7s18/probe.png` and `/tmp/v7s18/L1.png` (both pure black, 255 colours) against `/tmp/v7s13/flash0.png` (2,461 colours) and `/tmp/v7s13/L1.png`"
    - "This repository, `tools/clock_plane_report.py`: the measured-good table, where `clkdiv SPINE19/BOTTOM2` and `clkdiv SPINE26/BOTTOM2` are `good[this project]`"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 on the board within one session: `build/deploy_v7s18` flashed and observed by the operator to leave the HDMI link unsynchronised, with its capture matching the BRD-012 fallback signature; `build/deploy_v7s13` flashed on the same board, same cartridge, same capture path, and sync restored. The two bitstreams are the same netlist (SHA-256 of `tinygba.json` identical, `5c4edf8a9c24b65791e98cdaed81e2e03265d28815ee2912d6824483b07d1138`) placed at different seeds, so the difference is placement and nothing else.

- record_id: BRD-049
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The controller's ready strobe crosses from clk67 into clk16 with no synchroniser, and three blocks sample it"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "`sdram_gba` registers `cpu_ready` in the `clk67` domain: every one of its eight assignments sits in the single `always @(posedge clk)` block at line 189 of `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v`, and that module is instantiated with `.clk(clk67), .mclk(clk16)`. The signal leaves as a bare net, `cpu_mem_ready`, declared at `gbatang_top.sv:1192` with no register between the two domains: it is driven by the controller's `.cpu_ready()` port and consumed directly by `gba_memory`'s `.sdram_ready()` input (line 1252), by `gba_dma` (line 1322) and by a third instantiation (line 1337), all in the `clk16` domain. A search of the path for any synchroniser returns nothing. Because `clk67` is exactly four times `clk16` on this design, `cpu_ready` is high for a single `clk67` cycle, which is a quarter of a `clk16` period -- CORRECTED 2026-11-14: this is wrong. Every assignment to `cpu_ready` sits inside the `cycle[3]` slot, and `cycle` is a one-hot shift register over four values, so the slot asserts once per `clk16` period and the strobe is a full `clk16` period wide, four `clk67` cycles. `gbatang_top.sv`'s own comment states the measured figure, 556,000 `clk16` samples against 345,000 `clk67` rises, and the two-flop synchroniser added later was justified on exactly that width. The error mattered: designing against a one-`clk67`-cycle strobe would make the synchroniser look unsafe when it is not. A separate claim in the analysis behind `DISSENT-req2-start-mechanism-2026-11-14.md`, that a 16-bit CPU access produces a two-`clk16`-period strobe because the controller re-accepts the request once, was not verified here and is not asserted: the Thumb probe written to test that mechanism passed on a bitstream carrying the defect, so the mechanism is unconfirmed., and `gba_memory` uses it as a level -- `state == REQ1_WAIT & sdram_ready`, `sdram_ready ? MAIN : REQ2_WAIT`, and the `cpu_en` decode -- rather than as a handshake. Nothing in the design guarantees that a pulse a quarter of the receiving period wide is seen on the intended `clk16` edge once routing delay on three separate fan-out branches is accounted for. The code comment at `gba_memory.sv:385` describes the signal as 'a single-clk16 pulse', which is not what the RTL produces; the strobe is one `clk67` cycle."
  consequence: "This is a load-bearing hazard on the exact boundary that carries BRD-044's one-word lag, and it is the reason a clock-plan or phase difference between `clk16` and `clk67` can change what the CPU consumes without any logic changing. It does not by itself prove the lag is a timing fault: the model, which has ideal wires, reproduces the lag deterministically, so the mechanism there is the controller acknowledging after the first halfword rather than a missed strobe. What this record establishes is that the physical handshake is unsafe by construction, so a hardware measurement of it cannot be attributed to logic alone while BRD-019 remains open. The repair, if the hazard is to be closed, is a two-flop synchroniser in `clk16` plus an edge detect, or a proper four-phase handshake, and that is a change to the boundary rather than to either state machine."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v`: line 89 `output reg cpu_ready`, and the eight assignments at lines 321, 329, 354, 402, 456, 470, 488 and 491, all inside the `always @(posedge clk)` block opened at line 189"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: line 1192 `wire cpu_mem_ready`, line 1296 `.cpu_ready(cpu_mem_ready)`, lines 1252, 1322 and 1337 the three `.cpu_ready`/`.sdram_ready` consumers"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: the level-style uses of `sdram_ready` at lines 459, 563, 566, 585, 590 and 596"
  verification: "Read from the RTL on 2026-11-13, not inferred: the clock domains come from the two instantiation lines (`sdram_gba` on `.clk(clk67)`, `gba_memory` on `.clk(clk16)`), the absence of a synchroniser from a search of every reference to `cpu_mem_ready`, and the one-cycle width from `clk67` being 4x `clk16` on both the model and the board's PLL plan. Not measured on hardware: no board reading has isolated this path, and the hazard is stated as a property of the netlist rather than as a fault that has been observed firing."

- record_id: TCL-031
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The Verilator harness can rule out static clock phase but cannot represent arrival delay or skew"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "The harness derives all three clocks from a single counter in `tools/sim/sim_main.cpp`: `clk50 = (clkcnt & 3) < 2`, `clk16 = ((clkcnt + shift) % 12) < 6` and `clk67 = (clkcnt % 3) < 1`, stepping twelve times per `clk16` period so the 3:1 and 4:1 ratios are exact. `TINYGBA_CLK16_SHIFT` moves `clk16` against the other two one step at a time over its whole period. Computed from those expressions, that sweep does cover the sampling-phase question rather than being degenerate: for shifts 0, 3, 6 and 9 a `clk16` rising edge lands where `clk67` is high, and for the other eight it lands where `clk67` is low, so the twelve offsets give three distinct relative phases. Entry 38's sweep therefore genuinely tested whether the 32-bit read defect depends on which `clk67` level a `clk16` edge sees, and answered no across both cases. What the harness cannot represent is anything about arrival: every clock is an ideal zero-delay wire and the relation between them is fixed arithmetic, so propagation delay from a `clk67` flop to a `clk16` sampler, skew between two separate clock trees, metastability, and placement-dependent routing differences do not exist in it at all."
  consequence: "A model result over this interface settles static phase and says nothing about arrival delay or skew, and those are the quantities this board has been shown to be sensitive to: BRD-047 separates two bitstreams of one netlist by a `clk67` spine and gets a different program, and BRD-048 separates two by an `hclk` spine and gets a picture against no picture. The practical rule is that a fault reproduced in the harness is a property of the logic unless it is on a boundary where an unsynchronised strobe crosses domains (BRD-049), and in that case the harness has no authority to rule timing out. Any future sweep of this kind must say which of the two it has tested; entry 38's result was sound for the question it asked and was read as though it covered both."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/sim_main.cpp`: `clk16_level()`, the `clkcnt % 3` and `clkcnt & 3` expressions, and the twelve-step comment above `loading_step()`"
    - "This repository, `tools/score_phases.py` and `build/phase/p0`..`p11`, the twelve-offset sweep entry 38 records"
    - "This repository, records BRD-047, BRD-048 and BRD-049"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 by evaluating the harness's own clock expressions over all twelve shift values: one `clk16` rising edge per twelve steps in every case, with `clk67` high at that edge for four of the twelve shifts and low for eight. The limitation follows from the source, since the clocks are assignments to top-level pins from one counter and no delay is modelled anywhere."

- record_id: TCL-032
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Upstream's clock-lane assignment cannot be built on this flow, and swapping the lanes cannot change clock phase anyway"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "Entry 33 left one experiment open: give `clk16` and `clk67` upstream's `CLKOUT` assignment on the shared PLL, which is `CLKOUT1` for `clk16` and `CLKOUT2` for `clk67`, instead of this port's swap, and see whether the one-word read lag survives. Both halves of that experiment are now settled and neither supports the hypothesis. First, the assignment does not build. Read from the GW5AST-138C database, the only pips in the device that drive a clock-plane wire from a PLL output are `TLPLL0CLK0 <- MPLLCLKOUT0` and `TLPLL0CLK2 <- MPLLCLKOUT2` on the left-edge tiles and `TRPLL0CLK0 <- MPLLCLKOUT0` on the right-edge tiles: `CLKOUT1` reaches nothing beyond the tile-local `F1` lane. So `clk16` on `CLKOUT1` has no plane pip, exactly as `clk67` on `CLKOUT1` had none before this port gave it a BUFG. `CLOCK_LOC \"clk16\" BUFG` was applied as the same remedy, and the result is that `clk16` crossed no bridge pip at all and the clock-plane gate refused the placement with `NO PLANE PIP: 'clk16' crosses no bridge pip, so it is on general routing`. The BUFG route works for `clk67` because it carries 255 sinks; it does not work for `clk16`, which carries 2381. That is a structural refusal, not seed sensitivity, so upstream's literal assignment is unbuildable here. Second, and independent of the build: the swap cannot alter the clocks' relative phase in the first place. Both arrangements use the same VCO (`FVCO` 1809 MHz from `Fpfd` 27 and `MDIV` 67) and the same dividers, `ODIV0` 36, one lane at 27 and one at 108, giving 50.25, 67.00 and 16.75 MHz in both. Upstream puts 16.75 MHz on the /108 divider and 67 MHz on the /27 divider, and so does this port; only which output pin carries which result differs. What a swap changes is each clock's insertion delay and its routing to the plane, not the divider relationship that sets when one clock's edges fall relative to the other's."
  consequence: "Entry 33's open item is closed as a negative, and the phase hypothesis for BRD-044 is weakened rather than refuted: the two arrangements this experiment was meant to separate are electrically the same at the dividers, so a lag that survived in both would not have meant what entry 33 hoped. Two things follow. First, a phase explanation for the one-word lag would have to come from arrival delay on the plane and fabric routing rather than from the PLL plan, which is the quantity BRD-047 and BRD-048 show this board is sensitive to, and the only instrument that can measure it is the board and not the harness (TCL-031). Second, the port's clock swap is not a gratuitous deviation from upstream: `clk16` must be on `CLKOUT0` or `CLKOUT2` to reach the plane on this device, `clk50` holds `CLKOUT0`, so `CLKOUT2` is the only site `clk16` can occupy, and the port's arrangement is forced. Do not re-run this experiment; the build fails by construction and the arithmetic is settled."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/e49_s9/clock-plane.log`: `NO PLANE PIP: 'clk16' crosses no bridge pip`, with `clk67` on `SPINE19/TOP2` and `SPINE27/TOP2` from `CLKOUT2`"
    - "apicula `GW5AST-138C.msgpack.xz` read through `apycula.chipdb.load_chipdb`: the PLL-plane pip pairs listed above, with `TLPLL0CLK2` present on the left-edge site and absent for `CLKOUT1`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/pll/pll_16.v`: `MDIV_SEL(67)`, `ODIV0_SEL(36)`, `ODIV1_SEL(27)`, `ODIV2_SEL(108)`; the attempted swap set `ODIV1_SEL(108)` and `ODIV2_SEL(27)` with matching port renames"
    - "Upstream `/run/media/vash/GIT/tangcore/gbatang` at `77fd071`, `src/pll/pll_33.v` and `src/gbatang_top.sv:114`, the assignment being reproduced"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13: the swapped netlist was synthesised from the committed source (14,921 flip-flops and 148 block RAM, identical to `build/v7_s9`, so no logic changed) and placed at seed 9 from eight seeds launched; seed 9 reached a routed netlist and was refused by the clock-plane gate on `clk16` having no plane pip, at which point the remaining seeds were stopped because the failure is structural. The frequency arithmetic was computed from the PLL parameters rather than recalled."

- record_id: BRD-050
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Synchronising the controller's ready strobe into clk16 removes the one-word read lag, in the model and on the board"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "Adding a two-flop synchroniser to `cpu_mem_ready` in `gbatang_top.sv`, clocked by `clk16`, changes the read path completely in the Verilator model under `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL`. `test_logo.gba` at 3000 million steps now reports 47 cartridge data reads, all 47 matching the image with zero failures, a highest address of `0x08000C00` and 889 frames, against the unmodified tree's 984 reads with 973 correct, eleven failures, a highest address of `0x080002E4` and 865 frames. The specific read BRD-044 turned on returns its own word for the first time: `addr=080002E0 data_now=00000403 data_prev=0000FFE0`, where `data_prev` is exactly the predecessor the board measured, so the two candidate columns in the harness now disagree and the calibrated column confirms the fix. The display register follows: the `0x0403` store appears four times and `0xFFE0` never, against the unmodified tree's `0xFFE0` twice and `0x0403` never. The mechanism is the one BRD-039 identified, with the delay placed on the handshake instead of on `cpu_en`: `sdram_gba` acknowledges at the first halfword collect, when `cpu_rdata` holds a fresh low half and a stale high half, and the second collect completes the word one `clk67` cycle later; two `clk16` cycles of synchroniser delay put the CPU's consume after that second collect, so it takes a whole word. The extra cycles cost nothing measurable in frames, which rise from 865 to 889."
  consequence: "The read lag is fixed in the model by a change to the clock-domain boundary rather than by any edit to either state machine, which explains why entries 36, 46 and 47 all failed: each of them tried to move the consumer or the producer inside their own domains while the defect was the crossing between them. Both of TCL-027's properties hold here, accuracy at 47 of 47 and reach at `0x08000C00` against `0x080002E4`, so this is not a truncation dressed as a repair. Both predictions this record made were tested on the board and both held: the pool read at `0x080002E0` returns `0x00000403`, and the drawer's DISPCNT reaches `0x0403` with forced blank off, which ends the white field that entries 42 and 43 measured. The harness having ideal zero-delay clocks (BRD-033) no longer bounds the claim, because the confirmation is a board reading and not a model one. What the board adds beyond the model is worth stating: the model showed the delay fixing the word, and the board shows that the hazard was real on real silicon, where the unsynchronised strobe was in fact being sampled on the wrong edge."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: the `cpu_mem_ready_s` two-flop synchroniser and the `cpu_mem_ready_raw` net the controller instances now drive"
    - "Captures `/tmp/sync1.log` (1000 million steps) and `/tmp/sync3b.log` (3000 million steps), each scored with `tools/check_cartread.py`"
    - "This repository, `/tmp/base1b.log`, the unmodified tree at the same step counts"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 twice. In the Verilator model with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL`, 3000 million steps, `Total frames` confirmed before any figure was read: 47 of 47 reads correct, reach `0x08000C00`, pool word `0x00000403`. Then on the board, which is what raises this record to VERIFIED: `build/deploy_nwl11` (`-nowidelut`, `.fs` SHA-256 `b04314697d9358aabe11750c143f9a69d8056668307563288f5e9e8d1bd2239a`) flashed to the Tang Console 138K and loaded with `test_logo.gba`, and page 3 decoded from three independent captures (`/tmp/nwl/f1.png`, `f2.png`, `f8.png`) which agree field for field: the 32-bit read of `0x080002E0` returns `0x00000403` (it returned `0x0000FFE0` on `build/deploy_v7s13` before this change), `0x080002E4` returns `0x00007FFF` correctly, the `0x0403` store is issued and is the last DISPCNT write, the sticky `0xFFE0` flag never sets where it set twice before, and the drawer's own copy settles at forced_blank=0 with BG2 on, where before it sat at forced_blank=1 with BG0..BG3. The tail ring shows the CPU past the store, at `0x08000260` (the `strh` to `0x04000000`) and `0x08000264`, with 7 of 8 tail-ring fetches exact and the miss being `0x08000264` reading `0xE1CB00B0`, its predecessor -- the same one-transaction offset BRD-044 records elsewhere. The white field is gone as a consequence of the drawer reaching `0x0403`, which is the property entries 42 and 43 measured as absent."

- record_id: TCL-033
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The 4-bit object-settings line buffer is mapped to distributed RAM although its RTL asks for block RAM"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "`gpu/gba_gpu_drawer.v` instantiates `linebuffer #(4, 8) linebuf_obj_settings`, a 256-entry by 4-bit array, while every other line buffer in the drawer takes the module's defaults of 16 bits by 256 and is therefore wider. In `build/v7_base/synth.log` yosys maps them differently: `linebuf_bg0` through `linebuf_bg3` and `linebuf_obj_color` go to `$__GOWIN_DP_`, which is block RAM, and `linebuf_obj_settings` goes to `$__GOWIN_LUTRAM_` with the message `Extracted data FF from read port 0 of gbatang_top.gpu.drawer.linebuf_obj_settings.ram`, which means the read register was absorbed into the memory rather than left as a flip-flop. The RTL did not ask for this: `linebuffer.v` carries `/* synthesis syn_ramstyle = \"block_ram\" */` on the array under `` `ifdef M138K ``, and `M138K` is defined for this board at `boards/console138k.v:3`, so the intent on record is block RAM. The attribute is written in comment syntax, which this flow's `read_slang` front end does not parse, which is the same failure class the port already documented when it converted thirteen `(* keep *)` marks from comment form in `gba_memory.sv`. Four other arrays in the same netlist take the same path for the same reason: `sound.igba_sound_dmaA.fifo`, `sound.igba_sound_dmaB.fifo`, `sound.igba_sound_ch3.waveram` and `mem.loader_buf` all map to `$__GOWIN_LUTRAM_` with their read register extracted, and the netlist carries 66 `RAM16SDP4` cells in total."
  consequence: "The GPU drawer's object-settings buffer is on the same class of resource that the sibling `tang-tiny-gpu` project abandoned: its `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 records that distributed RAM built by these open tools misbehaved on hardware while passing Verilator, and that its `Makefile` therefore passes `synth_gowin -nolutram` so that small arrays become flip-flops and large ones block RAM. This project builds without that flag and has no gate on the result, so the mapping is invisible in the RTL and unchecked by any of the four existing build gates: the register-parity gate compares flip-flop counts against a baseline rather than their placement, the clock-plane and timing gates say nothing about memory cells, and the model does not reproduce the hardware behaviour of distributed RAM. This is a candidate for BRD-018, where the drawer receives lines and never asserts `pixel_out_we`, and it is not a finding about that defect: nothing here shows the object-settings buffer is wrong, only that the RTL asked for a resource and got a different one. Testing it needs the drawer instrument and a build with a real `(* ram_style *)` attribute on that array, and that is a GPU-path change. Adopting `-nolutram` wholesale would also move the four sound and loader arrays, so it is a change to be measured rather than assumed to be safe."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/v7_base/synth.log`: the `mapping memory gbatang_top.gpu.drawer.linebuf_*` lines and the `Extracted data FF from read port 0` messages"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gpu/gba_gpu_drawer.v:1711`, `linebuffer #(4, 8) linebuf_obj_settings`, against the default-width instantiations at lines 1691 and 1707"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gpu/linebuffer.v`, the `syn_ramstyle` comment attribute and its `` `ifdef M138K `` guard"
    - "Sibling project `fpt/tang-tiny-gpu`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 and `CLAUDE.md`, for the distributed-RAM finding and the `-nolutram` response, and `scripts/bsram_check.py` for the gate shape"
  verification: "Read from the synthesised netlist and its log on 2026-11-13, not inferred from the RTL: the mapping messages name each array and its template, and a cell census of `build/v7_base/tinygba.json` counts 66 `RAM16SDP4`, 66 `DPB` and 43 `DPX9B`. Not measured on hardware, and no gate exists for this class in the build."

- record_id: TCL-034
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A board result from one placement read once is not a result about the design"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "This project has been deploying a single bitstream at a single seed and reading the board once, and it has measured twice that this is not enough. BRD-047 separates two bitstreams of one netlist by a `clk67` spine and obtains a different program: `v7_s9` looped at `0x0800020C` with byte-2 increment errors in five of nine fetches, while `v6_s9` on the same board with the same cartridge ran the blink loop with byte-exact words. BRD-048 separates two by an `hclk` spine and obtains a picture against no picture at all: `v7_s18` passed every gate with zero hold violations and left the HDMI link unsynchronised, while `v7_s13`, same netlist, same cartridge, same capture path, restored sync. Both differences are placement and nothing else, and in both cases the failing image passed every gate the build has. The sibling `fpt/tang-tiny-gpu` project states the same rule from the other direction: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 lists defects that all passed in Verilator and only misbehaved on hardware, and its closing line is that if changing `nextpnr --seed` changes the hardware result then one of that list should be suspected, with completion defined as building two or more seeds and running the hardware twice for each."
  consequence: "A single placement read once cannot distinguish a property of the design from a property of the placement, and on this board the two have already differed twice in the answer they give. Board verification of any change therefore requires at least two seeds that pass every gate and at least two fresh loads per seed, with the clock plane recorded for each so a disagreement can be attributed. This is not a cosmetic tightening: BRD-047's whole content is that one placement produced a plausible and wrong reading, and entry 44 was withdrawn for exactly that reason. The practical form on this project is `tools/clock_plane_select.py` to pick candidates by measured-good pip rather than by wall-clock luck, then two seeds from that set, then two loads each, reporting reach and read accuracy per TCL-027 for every combination."
  sources:
    - "This repository, records BRD-047 and BRD-048, both of which separate two placements of one netlist that pass every build gate"
    - "This repository, core-log entries 44 and 45, which record the withdraw-then-retest sequence those two records came from"
    - "Sibling project `fpt/tang-tiny-gpu`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 for the Verilator-passes-hardware-fails list and the seed rule, and `CLAUDE.md` for the two-seed two-run completion bar"
  verification: "Measured twice on this board, 2026-11-13: the `clk67`-spine contrast in BRD-047 and the `hclk`-spine contrast in BRD-048, each an A/B on one board within one session with the same cartridge and the same capture path. Not a statistical claim: two observations of a placement-dependent difference, plus the sibling project's independent statement of the same rule."

- record_id: TCL-035
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "nextpnr's Gowin model does not time the dual-port BSRAM B-port address, and mem_bios drives it through twelve levels of logic"
  status: MEASURED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "The sibling `fpt/tang-tiny-gpu` project records, and reproduces with `tools/repro/nextpnr_gowin_timing`, that nextpnr's Gowin timing model does not check the setup path into a true dual-port BSRAM's B-port address (`ADB`): `gowin_arch_gen.py` registers that pin under a different cell class, so a path that meets timing in the report can still fail on the board, and whether it does depends on the placement. Their `scripts/bsram_check.py` therefore requires every `ADA` and `ADB` bit to come directly from a flip-flop `Q` or a constant. Running the same analysis on this project's netlist, `build/v7_base/tinygba.json`, finds 109 dual-port BSRAM cells and 994 address bits not driven directly from a flip-flop, split as `DPB.ADA` 632 bits in 54 cells, `DPX9B.ADB` 266 bits in 38 cells and `DPB.ADB` 96 bits in 8 cells. The `ADA` majority is the modelled port and is reported here for completeness rather than as a finding.
