On the unmodelled `ADB` port, the logic against depth is 38 bits at one level, 38 at two, 38 at three, 76 at five, 38 at six, 38 at seven, and 96 bits at 41 to 43 levels, and all 96 deep bits belong to one array: `mem.mem_bios`, the BIOS block RAM, across eight `DPB` slices. That array is on the instruction-fetch path the CPU reads on every boot. Tracing one of those chains names its shape: it starts at `cpu.cmd`, runs through a long `ALU` carry chain of the CPU's multiply/address datapath, and arrives at `mem.ram_addr`, which is the address `mem_bios` is read with, so this is the CPU's address generation meeting an untimed memory port rather than a peripheral's local logic."
  consequence: "This is the same shape as BRD-019 but for timing rather than clock routing: a path that no gate in this build checks, on the memories closest to the boot path, in a design whose hardware behaviour has already been shown twice to depend on the placement (BRD-047, BRD-048). It is recorded as MEASURED and not as a fault, because 41 to 43 LUT and carry levels at the 16.75 MHz this array is addressed at may still fit a 59.7 ns budget and nothing here shows the path failing; what is established is that nextpnr's Fmax report omits it, so the build's timing gate cannot vouch for it either way, and the sibling project's own note that long carry chains come out slower than reported makes a 43-level cone worth watching rather than dismissing. The specific reason to suspect it is that the four existing build gates all pass on images that then behave differently on the board, and this is a class of path that all four are blind to. The test is a gate that flags an `ADB` cone deeper than a chosen bound and reports its estimated depth, plus, if it is to be fixed rather than watched, registering the `mem_bios` address one level before the port so the deep cone feeds a flip-flop instead. Do not treat the `ADA` count as a defect: that port is modelled."
  sources:
    - "Sibling project `fpt/tang-tiny-gpu`: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10, `CLAUDE.md`, `scripts/bsram_check.py`, and `tools/repro/nextpnr_gowin_timing/` (`repro.sv`, `run.sh`), which reproduce the missing tool check by driving `ADB` with a thirty-stage combinational chain"
    - "This repository, `build/v7_base/tinygba.json`: the cell census (109 dual-port BSRAM, 66 `DPB` and 43 `DPX9B`) and the per-port address cone analysis performed on 2026-11-13"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_bios` instantiation in `gbatang_top.sv`, the array the deep cones belong to"
  verification: "Computed 2026-11-13 from the synthesised netlist: 994 logic-driven address bits across 109 cells, with the port split and the depth distribution above, and all 96 deep bits traced to `mem.mem_bios`'s eight `DPB` slices. This record was corrected on the same day after `tools/bsram_addr_check.py` was written and tested: the depth figures first recorded here were taken from an ad-hoc analysis whose traversal walked into BSRAM cells, whose outputs are registered, so it never reached a flip-flop and its depths were clipped by a cap -- which is where the `twelve levels` in the first version came from. The tool stops traversal at every non-combinational cell and measures against a fixed cap, and it reproduces 41 to 43 levels in half a second. Not measured on hardware; the tool exists but is not yet wired into the build."

- record_id: TCL-036
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Mapping wide multiplexers onto Gowin's fixed MUX2_LUT primitives triples build time, and -nowidelut fixes it while improving Fmax"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "`synth_gowin` by default maps wide multiplexers onto the device's dedicated `MUX2_LUT5` through `MUX2_LUT8` primitives. Those primitives occupy a fixed group of LUT tiles -- a `MUX2_LUT8` locks eight 4-LUT sites at one position -- so every one of them removes placement freedom. This design produced 13,295 of them on the instrumented build and 14,077 on the `v7` netlist, 9,419 of those in the CPU block alone, amounting to about 22,677 LUT-site equivalents or 16 percent of the device's 138,240 LUT sites position-locked. The consequence was not a resource problem but a routing problem: utilisation was only 38 percent LUT4 and 11 percent DFF, and yet placement of the default build took 1.5 to 2 hours per seed, at roughly 15,000 iterations per minute against 253,232 arcs. Passing `synth_gowin -nowidelut`, whose documented meaning is 'do not use muxes to implement LUTs larger than LUT4s', changes the netlist as follows with the same source and the same defines: cells 81,124 to 58,501, wide mux cells 13,295 to 0, LUT cells 46,161 to 36,800, flip-flops 14,923 to 14,923 exactly, netlist file 102 MB to 64 MB, and routing arcs 253,232 to 197,525. Synthesis time fell from 12.7 to 9 minutes. The CPU block fell from 19,895 cells to 10,595 and the memory block from 13,642 to 4,192, with no other block changing by more than 1,224. The first placement to converge did so in about 32 minutes and passed every gate: zero hold violations, `clk16` 22.871 MHz against a 16.750 constraint, `clk50` 67.732, `clk67` 32.982 under the documented GBATang four-cycle allowance, `hclk` 104.723, cross-domain paths 40.64 and 33.40 ns against 59.70, and `clk67` on both measured-good spines. Total build from synthesis to bitstream is therefore about 41 minutes against about 135, and the achieved clocks are better rather than worse: `clk16` rises from `v7_s13`'s 18.688 to 22.871 MHz and `hclk` from 86.972 to 104.723, because the fixed-position muxes were also the source of long combinational paths."
  consequence: "This is the answer to the standing question of why a design at 38 percent utilisation took longer to place than the sibling SNES core with SuperFX and its helper chips, which this project's operator put at about 20 minutes. It was not thread count, not device utilisation, and not the tool: it was the synthesiser choosing a primitive that pins logic in place, in a design whose CPU is mux-heavy. `-nowidelut` should be carried in the build rather than used as an experiment, because it is faster, smaller, better-timed and structurally identical in flip-flop count, which is the check that says no logic was lost. The reason this went unnoticed for a hundred entries is that no gate in the build measures build time or primitive mix, so a 3x cost was paid silently on every cycle. The open question it does not answer is behaviour: the first `-nowidelut` bitstream was flashed and the operator confirmed the display drawing its trace pages, so the design runs, but no decoded capture was obtained because the capture card stopped seeing signal during that session, so nothing in this record says the mapped design behaves identically to the wide-lut one beyond the observation that it boots and displays."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/sync_b/tinygba.json` (default) and `build/nwl/tinygba.json` (`-nowidelut`), synthesised from the same source and defines on 2026-11-13; cell census and per-block figures computed from both"
    - "This repository, `build/nwl_s11/`: `timing-gate.log`, `clock-plane.log`, `tinygba.fs` SHA-256 `b04314697d9358aabe11750c143f9a69d8056668307563288f5e9e8d1bd2239a`"
    - "This repository, `tools/build_tinygba.sh`: the `TINYGBA_SYNTH_EXTRA` variable added this cycle to pass extra `synth_gowin` arguments without editing the script"
    - "`yosys` `help synth_gowin`: `-nowidelut  do not use muxes to implement LUTs larger than LUT4s`"
    - "Sibling `TinySNES` at `cb4b22b`, `build/ack_s5/tinysnes.json`: 6,097 wide mux cells in the whole design against this project's 14,077"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13: both netlists synthesised from identical source and defines, census computed from the placed JSON, one placement of the `-nowidelut` netlist carried through every build gate to a packed bitstream in about 32 minutes, and that bitstream flashed with the operator observing the trace pages drawing on screen. The behavioural equivalence of the two mappings is NOT established -- no decoded capture was obtained -- and is stated here as the remaining gap."

- record_id: BRD-051
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "For the first time a cartridge draws its own picture: test_logo paints all eight checks green"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "The production image `build/deploy_prod` (`.fs` from `build/prod_s14`, `TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, the official BIOS, `TINYGBA_SYNTH_EXTRA=-nowidelut`, 11,497 flip-flops and 148 block RAM) flashed to the Tang Console 138K and loaded with `test_logo.gba` paints a solid green field with a white orientation square, and nothing else. That picture is the cartridge's own self-describing result: `test_logo` fills the background green when its failure mask is zero and red otherwise, then draws one 16-pixel yellow column per failed check, so a clean green field states that all eight checks passed -- three cartridge word reads at `0x08000400`, `0x08000800` and `0x08000C00`, word and byte-lane checks on IWRAM bottom and top, EWRAM bottom and top, and a halfword check on VRAM at `0x0600FF00`. Three independent captures agree and none contains a single yellow or red pixel: green 1,737,728 pixels, yellow 0, red 0, with the white orientation square present in two of the three and absent in the third only because it was captured during the square's blink. The operator, watching the same board, reported seeing the field. This is the first time since the project opened that a cartridge has produced a picture from its own code on this hardware, and it follows directly from BRD-050: the drawer now reaches `0x0403`, so the display is out of forced blank and the mode-3 background the cartridge fills is visible. It also closes the equivalence question entry 53 left open, because an image built with wide muxes mapped to LUTs passes the cartridge's own eight checks, which is a behavioural test of ROM fetch, IWRAM, EWRAM and VRAM rather than an inference from a boot."
  consequence: "This is the milestone the whole read-lag investigation was for, and it validates the change at the level a user cares about rather than at the level of a probe reading. Three results now stand together: the ready-strobe synchroniser fixes the one-word lag (BRD-050), the `-nowidelut` mapping is behaviourally sound as well as three times faster to build (TCL-036 plus this record), and the machine executes a cartridge to completion of that cartridge's own self-test. The reach figure is `0x08000C00`, the highest of the three `ROMCHK` addresses, against the `0x080002AC` blink loop that earlier entries measured, so reach increased rather than merely holding. What this record does not claim: it says nothing about a retail title, because BRD-045's DMA `REQ1_START` deadlock remains verified and untouched, and `test_logo` does not exercise DMA; it says nothing about sound, timers, EEPROM or flash saves, none of which were tested; and it does not establish that the picture is stable over time, only that three captures across a few seconds agreed."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/deploy_prod/` (`SHA256SUMS`, `timing-gate.log`, `clock-plane.log`, `parity.log`) and `build/prod_s14/`"
    - "Captures `build/evidence/prod_green.png` and `prod_green2.png`, plus `/tmp/prod/c1.png` to `c3.png`"
    - "This repository, `build/test/test.s` and `build/test/test_logo.gba`: the green/red background choice and the per-bit yellow column are the cartridge's own reporting, described in the ROMCHK, MEMCHK and VRAMCHK macros"
    - "This repository, records BRD-050 and TCL-036, which this result depends on"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 on the board: image flashed, `test_logo.gba` streamed over UART, three captures taken with the colour census reported above, and the operator independently confirming the field on screen. The image's gates read `Meets the contract with no relaxation needed`, `clk16` 23.959, `clk50` 86.311, `clk67` 152.462, `hclk` 95.905 MHz and zero hold violations. This is the first board image of this project to need no multicycle relaxation."

- record_id: TCL-037
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The harness frame dump, the controller model and the board disagree about the same cartridge's picture"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "Three configurations were asked what `test_logo.gba` draws and all three answered differently. On the board, `build/deploy_prod` paints a solid green field (BRD-051, confirmed by three captures and by the operator). In the harness built with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL`, the same cartridge dumps `pixel_out_*` frames that are pure black from frame 200 through frame 700. In the harness built with the behavioural array instead of the controller, it dumps pure white `252,252,252` at frames 100 to 400, while the run reports only 60 cartridge fetch cycles against millions on the controller build, so that configuration is not executing the cartridge at all. The same controller harness, given the RetroRegen diagnostic cartridge, reproduces the board exactly: a single orange text row at output rows 130 to 137 with the same smeared glyph shapes and the same frozen state across six frames. The dump path itself is sound as code -- `dump_frame()` writes the same `screenbuffer` the SDL texture is built from, at 240x160, and the buffer is written only where `pixel_out_we` is high -- so the disagreement is not an obviously broken file writer."
  consequence: "The harness is a trustworthy instrument for the diagnostic cartridge, where it matched the board, and an untrustworthy one for `test_logo`, where it did not. That kills the plan this cycle started with, which was to debug the diagnostic cartridge's smeared text inside the harness on the assumption that the harness represents the board. It represents at least one board state faithfully and at least one badly, and until the difference is explained a harness-only result about a picture cannot be carried to the board or used to rank candidate fixes. The likely reason is that `screenbuffer` is only written on `pixel_out_we` and is never cleared per frame, so a mode whose output is one flat value can read as uniform black or uniform white depending on which pixels were last written and whether the frame counter is derived from the same events; that is a hypothesis and not yet measured. Do not treat a harness frame as evidence about the board until this is settled, and do not use the array model to judge display questions at all: it did not reach the cartridge in this test."
  sources:
    - "Board: `build/evidence/prod_green.png` and BRD-051"
    - "Harness, controller: `/tmp/mtl/t_frame0200.ppm` through `t_frame0700.ppm`, all single-colour black; run log `/tmp/mtl3.log`"
    - "Harness, array: `/tmp/arr/a_frame0100.ppm` to `a_frame0400.ppm`, single-colour `252,252,252`; run log `/tmp/arr.log`, `cartridge fetch cycles: 60`"
    - "Harness, diagnostic cartridge: `/tmp/mf/g_frame0100.ppm` and five identical successors; the board's matching capture is `/tmp/d2.png`"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/sim_main.cpp`: `dump_frame()`, the `pixel_out_we` guard on `screenbuffer`, and the texture update"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 by running the same binaries against the same two cartridges and reading the dumped PPMs rather than the SDL window, with frame counts and colour histograms compared across configurations. No explanation is offered for the disagreement beyond a stated hypothesis, because none was tested."

- record_id: BRD-052
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "WITHDRAWN: the harness appeared to corrupt the 0x0403 store, but the trace attributed stores to non-store instructions"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "Running `test_logo.gba` in the harness built with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` reproduces the load correctly and corrupts the store. The cartridge's `ldr r0,=0x0403` at `0x0800025C` reads the literal at image offset `0x2E0`, and the harness's own `CARTREAD` line reports that read returning `0x00000403`. The immediately following `strh r0,[r11]` at `0x08000260` is then reported committing with data `0x00000008`, and it is the last write to `0x04000000` in the run. The consequence is visible in the same run's framebuffer: `FINALFRAME: nonzero=0 white=0 green=0 of 38400`, an entirely blank screen, because DISPCNT ends at `0x0008` (mode 0, BG0) rather than `0x0403` (mode 3, BG2). On the board the same cartridge ends with `0x0403` and paints solid green (BRD-051), and BRD-050's page-3 decode records `last data written=0x0403`. So load and store disagree inside one run in the harness, and the harness disagrees with the board on the stored value. Two other writes in the same trace carry values that are addresses rather than data -- `data=04000000` at `pc=0x08000068` -- which points at the store data path rather than at the cartridge."
  consequence: "WITHDRAWN 2026-11-13, later the same day. TCL-039 tested the `0x0008` against a scan of the cartridge for real store encodings and found that the harness attributes stores to instructions that are not stores, roughly three instructions late; the store that writes DISPCNT is at `0x260` and the trace reported it at `0x26C`. The `0x00000008` is therefore not what the cartridge's `strh` carried but whatever the datapath held three instructions later, and the `0x0403 >> 7` agreement noted above is a coincidence of that later state and not evidence of a shifter. Nothing in this record should be cited as evidence that the harness corrupts stores; what survives is only the observation it was built to explain, that the harness's final framebuffer is empty for `test_logo` while the board's is solid green, and that remains unexplained. The record is kept rather than deleted because the withdrawal, and the fact that a plausible mechanism was published for a few hours, is itself the useful part: a bus trace gated on `cpu_en` says a cycle completed, not which instruction completed on it."
  sources:
    - "Harness: `build/sim_pc2/obj_dir/Vgbatang_top` on `build/test/test_logo.gba`, 800 million steps; log lines `CARTREAD: addr=080002e0 ... data_now=00000403` and `DISPCNTWRITE: data=00000008 be=3 pc=0800026c`, and `FINALFRAME: nonzero=0 white=0 green=0 of 38400`"
    - "This repository, `build/test/test_logo.gba`: `0x25C` = `E59F007C` (`ldr r0,=pool`), `0x260` = `E1CB00B0` (`strh r0,[r11]`), `0x2E0` = `00000403`"
    - "This repository, records BRD-050 and BRD-051, the board readings of the same store and the resulting green field"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 in the harness and compared against two board records taken the same day. The harness result was re-run after correcting an unrelated tracing defect (see TCL-038) and is reproducible at 800 million steps."

- record_id: TCL-038
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The harness's DISPCNT-write trace was not gated on cpu_en, so one store was logged once per cycle it was held"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "In `tools/sim/sim_main.cpp` the cartridge-read trace is gated on `cpu_en_r`, but the write trace was not: it printed whenever `ram_cen_r && ram_wen_r && ram_addr_r == 0x04000000`. A store held on the bus for several cycles is therefore logged once per cycle, and the log showed the cartridge's single `strh r0,[r11]` as four identical `DISPCNTWRITE` lines followed by a fifth with different data. Gating the write trace on `cpu_en_r` as the read trace already was reduces the same run to one line per store and changes the picture completely: with the defect the trace showed `0x0403` written four times and then `0x0008`; corrected, it shows that the cartridge never writes `0x0403` at all and that the store carries `0x0008` (BRD-052). Three separate instrument defects were found in this cycle and all three produced plausible-looking wrong readings: a VRAM write trace sampled on `fclk` when the memory latches on `mclk`, and so reported `din=0` for every write; the same trace capped at 400 lines, which filled entirely during the BIOS's VRAM clear and never reached the cartridge; and this missing `cpu_en` gate."
  consequence: "Any conclusion drawn from the harness's `DISPCNTWRITE` lines before this correction is suspect, because the line count and the sequence both change. The board-side equivalents are unaffected: `build/deploy_prod` and the page-3 readouts in BRD-050 and BRD-051 come from the RTL recorder in `gbatang_top.sv`, not from this harness. The lesson is the one TCL-024 states in a different form: an instrument can manufacture events, and a write trace that fires on level rather than on commit will do it whenever the bus is held. The check that catches it is to gate every bus trace on the same enable as the consumer uses and to compare the line count against an independent counter."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/sim_main.cpp`: the read trace's `cpu_en_r && ram_cen_r && !ram_wen_r` condition against the write trace's original `ram_cen_r && ram_wen_r`"
    - "Harness logs `/tmp/pc.log` (defective) and `/tmp/pc2.log` (corrected) for the same cartridge and step count"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v` and `fpga/gba/src/common/sim_dpram_be.sv`, for the clock-domain mistake in the VRAM trace"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 by running the same cartridge and step count before and after the one-line change and comparing the write sequence, which changed from four identical `0x0403` lines plus a stray `0x0008` to a single `0x0008` line."

- record_id: TCL-039
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The harness attributes CPU stores to instructions that are not stores, roughly three instructions late"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "`test_logo.gba` contains store instructions at image offsets `0x06C`, `0x070`, `0x0C8`, `0x114`, `0x144`, `0x174`, `0x1A4`, `0x1D4` and `0x260`, the last being the `strh r0,[r11]` that writes DISPCNT. Sampling every instruction word in the image for the ARM `strh Rd,[Rn]` and `str Rd,[Rn]` encodings gives exactly that set. The harness's `STOREDBG` trace, gated on `cpu_en_r && ram_cen_r && ram_wen_r && ram_addr_r == 0x04000000`, reports writes from `test_logo` at `pc_rom` values `0x08000068`, `0x080000D4` and `0x0800026C`. None of those three is a store instruction: `0x068` holds `F130956F` and `0x06C` is the store, `0x0D4` is not in the set, and `0x26C` holds `E59F5070` (`ldr r5,=0x7FFF`) while the store is one instruction earlier at `0x260`. The same trace run on the RetroRegen cartridge reports its DISPCNT write at `0x0800334C` with data `0x00000403`, and that value is correct -- so the trace can produce a right answer, which is what makes the wrong ones dangerous. The consequence for BRD-052 is direct: the `0x00000008` attributed to the store is not what the cartridge's `strh` carried, it is whatever the datapath held three instructions later, and `0x0403 >> 7` equals `0x0008` only as a coincidence of that later state rather than as evidence of a shifter fault."
  consequence: "BRD-052's conclusion that the harness corrupts a store between the register file and the bus is withdrawn: what the trace showed was its own attribution error. So there is no measured evidence that the harness corrupts stores, and equally none that it does not. What remains solid in BRD-052 is the observation it was built to explain, that the harness's final framebuffer is empty for `test_logo` while the board's is solid green, and that observation still has no explanation. The lesson is narrower than TCL-038's and sharper: gating a bus trace on the consumer's enable is necessary but not sufficient, because the enable says a cycle completed, not which instruction completed on it. A store trace must be tied to the retiring instruction -- by capturing the store's own address and data at issue and carrying them through the pipeline, or by comparing its reported PC against a precomputed list of real store sites, which is cheap and would have caught this on the first run."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/test/test_logo.gba`, scanned for `strh Rd,[Rn]` (`(w & 0x0FF000F0) == 0x01C000B0`) and `str Rd,[Rn]` (`(w & 0x0F300000) == 0x05000000`), yielding the nine offsets above"
    - "Harness `build/sim_st2/obj_dir/Vgbatang_top` on both cartridges at 700 million steps; log lines `/tmp/st_diag.log` and the paired `STOREDBG` output"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/sim_main.cpp`: the `STOREDBG` block added this cycle"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13 by comparing the harness's reported store PCs against an independent scan of the cartridge image for real store encodings, and by showing the same trace producing a correct value on a different cartridge."

- record_id: BRD-053
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Three purpose-built probes pass on the board: VRAM, cartridge reads to 1 MiB, and fifteen ALU operations"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "Three new cartridges were built and run on the board, and every one of them reported success. `make_vram_probe_rom.py` writes a four-band pattern into frame memory in mode 3 -- a red ramp, a green ramp, a blue ramp and a band whose colour depends on both coordinates -- then reads every halfword back and paints a border white when all 38,400 readbacks matched and red otherwise. The board shows all four bands exactly as designed and a white border, so writing frame memory, reading it back, and the drawer's read of it are all correct; the earlier reading of a red border was a sampling error in my own capture analysis, not the machine. `make_romprobe_rom.py` stores each checkpoint's own offset at that offset for every 64 KiB from `0x10000` to `0xF0000` across a 1 MiB image, reads each one back and paints a 15x15 square, green for a match. All fifteen squares are green, so cartridge reads are correct across the whole first megabyte; nothing before this had tested a cartridge read above 4 KiB. `make_alu_probe_rom.py` scores fifteen operations a text renderer depends on -- five multiplies including `0xFFFF * 0xFFFF` and a 15-bit multiplicand, `mla`, `lsl`, `lsr`, `asr`, add with wrap and with carry, subtract with borrow, and the shift-pair forms of unsigned byte, signed byte and signed halfword extension -- and paints one square per test. All fifteen are green and the end marker is reached, so the CPU's arithmetic is correct for every form tested."
  consequence: "The three subsystems a display fault would most plausibly live in are now measured good on the board, which narrows the RetroRegen cartridge's failure rather than explaining it. In particular a wrong multiply or shift would have produced exactly the observed symptom -- glyphs at the correct position and size with wrong patterns, because a text renderer computes a glyph's address as `base + (char - 32) * bytes_per_glyph` -- and that hypothesis is now excluded by direct measurement. What is left is anything a probe of this shape does not exercise: interrupts, HALT, DMA, timers, execution of code spread across a large image, and display modes other than the mode 3 bitmap all three probes used. The probes deliberately avoid interrupts and never halt, because they were written to isolate memory and arithmetic first. A fourth probe that tested VBlank interrupt delivery and HALT was written and did not draw at all, which would mean a hang, but it is not recorded as a finding because it is not trusted: it hung on the board in three runs including after its counter was moved out of the BIOS's IRQ stack region, and an instrument that fails on the first run says nothing until its own correctness is established (TCL-024, and the eleven instrument defects recorded across entries 53 to 58)."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/make_vram_probe_rom.py`, `tools/make_romprobe_rom.py` and `tools/make_alu_probe_rom.py`, each deterministic and each printing what it built"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/vp3.png` (white border, four bands), `/tmp/rp.png` (fifteen green squares) and `/tmp/ap.png` (fifteen green squares plus the end marker)"
    - "This repository, `build/test/vramprobe.gba`, `build/test/romprobe.gba` and `build/test/aluprobe.gba`, each loaded through `tools/load_gba_rom.py` with its header verified"
  verification: "Measured on the board 2026-11-13: each cartridge was loaded over UART, its header checked by `load_gba_rom.py`, and the result read from a 1920x1080 capture by sampling each test square's centre against the two report colours. The white-border reading was re-measured after an initial mis-sample that reported red, which is noted above and in the repository history."

- record_id: TCL-040
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A probe cartridge must be checked for the result it should show before the machine is blamed for showing nothing"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-13
  statement: "Four probe cartridges were written this cycle. Three reported their results and were read successfully. The fourth, which set up a VBlank interrupt handler at `0x03007FFC`, enabled the interrupt, waited for thirty-two of them and then painted a count bar, drew nothing at all on the board in three successive runs -- and drawing nothing is exactly what a machine that never delivers an interrupt would do. It is therefore not evidence of a fault: the probe's own correctness was never established, and the same run would look identical whether the machine had failed or the cartridge had. Two mistakes were found and fixed in it while trying: it placed its counter at `0x03007FF0`, inside the region the BIOS uses for its own interrupt stack, and it used an ARM `halt` instruction that ARM7TDMI does not have before being rewritten to write `HALTCNT`. Neither fix made it draw, and no third cause was established before the work stopped."
  consequence: "The asymmetry matters: a probe that draws the success colour is informative, and a probe that draws nothing is not, because 'nothing' is the predicted output of both the fault under test and of a broken probe. So a probe needs a control -- a variant that draws unconditionally, or a run on a known-good configuration -- before its silence can be read. This is the same lesson as TCL-024 and TCL-038 arriving from a new direction, and it is worth stating separately because the failure mode here is silence rather than a wrong number: a wrong number invites a second look, and a blank screen invites a conclusion. The three probes that did work all paint an explicit success colour and a distinct failure colour, which is why their results can be read."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/make_vram_probe_rom.py` and `tools/make_alu_probe_rom.py`, which paint green for pass and red for fail and can therefore be read"
    - "The deleted fourth probe, whose two defects were a counter at `0x03007FF0` in the BIOS IRQ stack area and an ARM `halt` instruction absent from ARM7TDMI"
    - "This repository, records TCL-024 and TCL-038, the same class from different directions"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-13: three probes produced readable results and one produced a blank screen across three runs, and the blank one was never shown to be correct in a configuration where it should pass."

- record_id: BRD-054
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "mGBA renders the diagnostic cartridge perfectly, and the board draws different glyph data rather than corrupted glyphs"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "mGBA 0.10.5 on this machine renders the RetroRegen cartridge correctly: the title screen shows `RETRO REGEN HANDHELD HARDWARE TEST SUITE v1.5` over an illustrated background, and pressing Start reaches a text menu with `QUICK TEST`, `SYSTEM STATUS`, `MEMORY TEST`, `SCREEN TEST`, `GPU STRESS TEST`, `BUTTON TEST`, `AUDIO TEST`, `BURN-IN TEST`, `ABOUT / HELP`, a dashed rule, `MODEL: GBA FAMILY (AGB BIOS)` in orange and `GUIDED FULL CHECKOUT WITH A PASS/FAIL SUMMARY` beneath it. The cartridge is therefore sound, and the board's failure to run it is the board's. Comparing the two at native resolution changes the diagnosis. The board draws exactly one text row, at native rows 128 to 147 including descenders, in orange -- the same vertical region and the same colour as mGBA's `MODEL: GBA FAMILY` line. But a per-pixel comparison of that row between the board and mGBA gives 55.3 percent agreement, which is what two unrelated patterns give: the board is not drawing that line with corrupted bits, it is drawing different glyph data. The two have similar ink density, 607 lit pixels on the board against 537 in mGBA, so the board is producing text-shaped output of the right size at the right place in the right colour, from the wrong glyphs."
  consequence: "This reframes the fault from corruption to addressing. A corrupted copy of the right glyphs would agree well with the reference and differ in a few bits; 55 percent agreement means the source data is different, so something upstream of the pixel is selecting the wrong glyph. The cartridge sets DISPCNT to `0x0403`, which is mode 3 -- bitmap -- so it renders its text in software rather than through a tile map, which means it copies glyph bitmaps into frame memory itself. Cartridge reads are correct to 1 MiB (BRD-053) and frame memory write, read and display are correct (BRD-053), so a software copy should work. What sits between a ROM read and a frame-memory write and has not been measured on the board is DMA, and BRD-045 records a verified DMA defect. That is now the leading hypothesis and the next test. Two things are excluded by this record: the cartridge, which runs under a known-good emulator, and the idea that individual bits are being lost somewhere in the display path, since losing bits would not produce an unrelated pattern."
  sources:
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 (`/usr/games/mgba-qt`) on this machine, window captures `/tmp/mg3.png` (title) and `/tmp/mg5.png` (menu)"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/d2.png` and `/tmp/bd1.png`, the same cartridge on the Tang Console 138K with `build/deploy_prod`"
    - "Per-pixel comparison at native 240x160 resolution: 55.3 percent agreement over rows 128 to 135, 607 lit on the board against 537 in mGBA"
    - "This repository, record BRD-053, for the three probes that pass, and BRD-045 for the DMA defect"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-14: mGBA rendering observed directly from its window, the board frame read from a capture of the same cartridge, and the two compared pixel by pixel after locating each image's active area and scale (board 6.76x from a 1622-pixel-wide active area, mGBA exactly 3x from a 720-pixel window whose game viewport starts at y=22)."

- record_id: TCL-041
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Running a probe cartridge under mGBA is a control, and it caught two probe bugs before the board was blamed"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "A DMA probe cartridge was written to test the one path between cartridge ROM and frame memory that no board measurement covers. Its first run on the board reported five failures out of five, which would mean DMA is completely dead on this hardware. Running the identical cartridge under mGBA 0.10.5 -- a known-good GBA implementation on the same machine -- reported the same five failures, so the probe was wrong and the board was innocent. Two defects were then found in the probe. It verified the DMA result with 32-bit reads from frame memory, and VRAM does not support 32-bit access, so those reads return garbage on real hardware and in the emulator alike. And it loaded its DMA source address with `=DMASRC`, where `as` resolves the symbol to an offset within `.text` and `objcopy` emits `.text` at zero, so the transfer read from `0x00000xxx` in the BIOS region instead of `0x0800xxxx` in the cartridge; every such reference needed `0x08000000` added by hand. Both were fixed and the control still reported five failures, so the probe remains invalid and a third fault is unfound. No conclusion about the machine's DMA is drawn."
  consequence: "The board is expensive to test and its failures are ambiguous -- a red square means either the machine is broken or the probe is. mGBA resolves that ambiguity in about a minute, needs no hardware, and is already installed. Any probe cartridge should therefore be run under mGBA first and required to report success there before its board result is read at all. This is the operational form of TCL-040: a probe that draws nothing cannot be interpreted, and one that draws the failure colour is no better until a known-good implementation has been shown to draw the success colour with the same binary. The two defects this control caught would each have produced a confident and wrong finding about the machine, and the second in particular -- a link-time address used as a runtime address -- is the kind of mistake that reads as correct in the source."
  sources:
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 (`/usr/games/mgba-qt`) on this machine, showing five red squares for `build/test/dmappobe.gba` both before and after the two fixes"
    - "The board showing the same five red squares for the same binary"
    - "This repository, `tools/make_dma_probe_rom.py`, and records TCL-040 and BRD-054"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-14: the same cartridge image run on the board and in mGBA, both reporting five failures, which is what established that the probe rather than the machine was at fault."

- record_id: BRD-056
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Byte loads, unaligned byte loads and the VBlank flag all work on the board, each validated in mGBA first"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Two more probes, each run in mGBA before its board result was read, come back clean. `build/test/byteprobe.gba` performs eleven byte loads (`ldrb`) from cartridge ROM at consecutive offsets and four more from a base deliberately not word aligned, comparing each against the value the image holds, and paints one square per test. All fifteen squares are green in mGBA and all fifteen are green on the board. That matters because byte loads are how a font decoder reads glyph bitmaps, no earlier probe exercised them, and BRD-034 once confined a read defect to the byte path. `build/test/irqprobe.gba` polls `DISPSTAT`'s VBlank flag for up to 200,000 iterations and paints green if it ever rises. That square is green in mGBA and green on the board. Two of that probe's other checks, which use `HALT` to wait for the interrupt, are red in mGBA as well, so they are the probe's defect and are not read as results about the machine."
  consequence: "The list of subsystems measured good on this board is now: frame memory write, read-back and display; cartridge reads at every 64 KiB checkpoint to 1 MiB; fifteen CPU arithmetic operations including multiply, shift and sign extension; DMA3 from cartridge ROM to frame memory with an incrementing source; byte loads including unaligned ones; and the VBlank flag in DISPSTAT rising. Everything the RetroRegen cartridge needs in order to copy a glyph and store it has been tested and works. The machine draws exactly one text row of that cartridge, at the correct position, size and colour, from the wrong glyph data (BRD-054), and then stops. Since every individual operation the cartridge performs is measured good, the remaining explanations are about execution rather than about any single operation: the cartridge may use a feature not implemented here, may execute from a region not covered by a 4 KiB probe, or may hang at a point whose cause is not in these paths. HALT itself is not among the measured-good items, because the probe that tests it is still wrong."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/test/byteprobe.gba` and `build/test/irqprobe.gba`, with their assembly sources preserved"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 results `/tmp/bp.png` (fifteen green) and `/tmp/irqm2.png` (square 0 green, squares 1 and 2 red)"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/bpbd.png` (fifteen green) and `/tmp/irqbd.png` (square 0 green, `(18,255,0)`)"
    - "This repository, records BRD-053, BRD-055 and TCL-041"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-14: each cartridge run in mGBA first and required to pass there, then loaded on the board over UART and read from a 1920x1080 capture, with the board's active area located and the sample coordinates derived from its scale rather than assumed."

- record_id: BRD-055
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "WITHDRAWN: DMA3 from cartridge ROM to frame memory works on the board -- the probe expected zero, and zero is what a broken transfer writes"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "A minimal DMA cartridge (`build/test/minidma.gba`, built from an assembly source of about a hundred lines) clears frame memory to `0x001F` with CPU stores, starts a DMA3 transfer of 64 words from cartridge ROM at `0x080001C0` to `0x06000000` with the control word `0x8400003F` (enable, 32-bit, source increment, destination increment, 64 units), and then reads the first destination halfword back and paints the whole screen one of three ways: green when it reads `0x0000`, which is the low half of the first source word `0x12340000`; red when it still reads `0x001F`, the value the clear left; blue for anything else. Under mGBA 0.10.5 the screen is green. On the board the screen is also green: 1,730,160 pixels of `(18,255,0)` against 311,040 of the `(43,43,43)` pillarbox, with no red or blue anywhere, and the operator independently reported the screen filled completely green. The transfer therefore ran, used an incrementing source address in cartridge ROM, wrote frame memory, and the destination held the expected value afterwards."
  consequence: "WITHDRAWN 2026-11-14. The probe's expected value was `0x0000`, which is the low half of the source word `0x12340000` and is equally what a transfer that writes zeroes leaves behind, so this green certified a broken path rather than a working one: `tools/roms/dmawrote.s` later measured the destination changed, not equal to the pattern, and exactly zero on the board (BRD-062), and BRD-064 then showed the netlist was mis-synthesised. Nothing here should be cited as evidence that DMA works. What survives is the probe's own validation discipline and the fact that the capture path was checked against the operator's eyes. Original text follows.

This closes the leading hypothesis for the RetroRegen cartridge's failure. BRD-054 established that the board draws that cartridge's text from the wrong glyph data, and that the cartridge renders text in software in mode 3 by copying glyph bitmaps from ROM, so the copy crosses the DMA path. DMA is now measured working on the board, using the same shape of transfer such a copy would use, so DMA is not the cause. Two further notes matter for what comes next. The probe was validated in mGBA before its board result was read, which is the discipline TCL-041 records, and the validation was not a formality: the earlier multi-check DMA probe reported five failures on both the board and the emulator, and only the emulator run revealed that the probe was wrong. And the capture path was checked against the operator's own eyes on the same frame, which is worth doing because this session has produced several wrong readings from instruments rather than from the machine. What remains untested on the board and still plausibly explains a cartridge that draws one text row and stops: byte loads (`ldrb`), which no probe has exercised and which is how font bitmaps are read; the mode 0 tile path, which every probe so far has avoided by drawing in mode 3; interrupts and HALT; timers; and execution of code spread across a large image rather than a 4 KiB one."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/test/minidma.gba`, and the assembly it was assembled from, which is reproduced in the cycle's log entry"
    - "Board capture `/tmp/chk.png`: 1,730,160 pixels of `(18,255,0)`, 311,040 of `(43,43,43)`, 20 colours total, green at every sampled row"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 on the same binary, green, `/tmp/mini5.png`"
    - "This repository, records BRD-054 and TCL-041"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-14: the same image passed in mGBA and on the board, and the board's capture was confirmed against the operator's direct observation of the screen."

- record_id: BRD-057
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Real interrupt dispatch works on the board: the cartridge handler is entered, acknowledges IF, counts, and returns"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "`tools/roms/irq3.s`, built by `tools/make_probe_rom.py`, was required to report success under mGBA 0.10.5 with the retail BIOS before its board result was read, per TCL-041. Both agree exactly. The board capture reads: square 0 green (the VBlank flag in `DISPSTAT` rises while polling), square 1 green (the BIOS hands the cartridge `IE` and `IME` both clear), square 2 green (`[0x03007FFC]` holds the stored pointer), square 3 blue (the main flow survives `IME = 1` and reaches the next instruction), square 4 green (a dispatch completes), a white bar 8 pixels long (exactly one interrupt), and three white markers in the top right corner painted by the handler itself at entry, after the IF acknowledge, and after the count increment. The handler is therefore entered, acknowledges the source, increments its counter, and returns to the interrupted code. This is the first measurement of the cartridge interrupt path on this hardware; every earlier interrupt probe was a broken instrument."
  consequence: "Interrupt dispatch is removed from the list of things that could explain a cartridge drawing one text row and stopping. The remaining untested execution features are the BIOS SWI calls a cartridge makes every frame -- principally `VBlankIntrWait` (SWI `0x05`) and `IntrWait` (SWI `0x04`) -- and the hardware the cartridges drive that the probes never touch. That matters because the observed behaviour, a cartridge that runs briefly and then stops, is the natural signature of a frame wait that never returns, and a probe that calls `VBlankIntrWait` directly is the cheapest way to separate a broken SWI from a broken interrupt. Three probe defects were found and recorded on the way here (TCL-042, TCL-043, TCL-044); each of them would have produced a confident finding about the machine that was false."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/irq3.s` and `build/test/irq3.gba`"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 with `/tmp/official_bios.bin`: squares 0/1/2 green, 3 blue, 4 green, bar 8 px, markers 236-238 white"
    - "Board capture `/tmp/board_irq3.png`: squares 0/1/2 green, 3 blue, 4 green, bar 8 px, markers 236-238 white"
    - "This repository, the retail BIOS disassembly at 0x128 in `/tmp/official_bios.bin`: `push {r0-r3,ip,lr}`, `mov r0,#0x04000000`, `add lr,pc,#0`, `ldr pc,[r0,#-4]`"
- record_id: TCL-042
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "`adr` already yields the runtime address; adding the cartridge base on top of it jumps into unmapped memory"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "To install a cartridge interrupt handler, a probe must write the *runtime* address of its handler to `0x03007FFC`. An earlier probe used `ldr r1, =0x08000000 + _irq` and needed the base because `as` resolves `_irq` to an offset inside `.text` which `objcopy` re-bases to zero. The replacement used `adr r1, _irq` and kept the base addition, reasoning that the same correction still applied. It does not: `adr` is PC-relative, so at runtime `pc` already holds the executing address and the sum came out as `0x1000033C` -- `0x33C` plus the base twice -- which is unmapped. Every interrupt jumped into open bus and the machine spun there. A GDB session against mGBA's stub showed `pc = 0x1000031c` with `[0x03007ffc] = 0x1000031c`, which named the defect in one reading."
  consequence: "An absolute `ldr =sym` needs the cartridge base added by hand; an `adr` never does. The two forms look interchangeable in source and are not, and the mistake is invisible in review because both spellings contain the symbol. The corresponding discipline is to read the pointer back from `0x03007FFC` at runtime rather than to reason about the expression, which is what BRD-057's square 2 does."
  sources:
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 gdb stub: `pc = 0x1000031c`, `[0x03007ffc] = 0x1000031c`"
    - "This repository, commit history of `tools/roms/irq3.s`"
- record_id: TCL-043
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "IF is at 0x04000202; acknowledging through IE leaves the source pending and starves the main flow"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "The interrupt acknowledge register `IF` is a halfword at `0x04000202`. `IE`, the enable mask, is the halfword at `0x04000200` directly below it. A hand-written probe acknowledged VBlank with a store to `0x04000200`, which sets the enable bit and leaves the source pending. Because the VBlank flag stayed asserted, the interrupt was re-taken before the interrupted instruction could retire: the handler ran continuously, the main flow made no progress at all, and the screen showed the handler's own progress markers while every square after the enable stayed black. This reads exactly like 'the handler never returns' and was mistaken for an `lr` convention problem first; a `sub lr, lr, #4` was added on that theory and made no difference, because the return path was never broken."
  consequence: "A level-triggered interrupt that is not acknowledged produces a machine that looks hung after the handler has visibly run, which is indistinguishable on screen from a broken return. Two consequences follow. The disassembly of the shipped dispatcher settles the `lr` question without experiment: `0x128 push {r0-r3,ip,lr}`, `0x12c mov r0,#0x04000000`, `0x130 add lr,pc,#0`, `0x134 ldr pc,[r0,#-4]` gives the handler `lr = 0x138`, the address of the dispatcher's own `pop`, so a correct handler ends in a plain `bx lr` and must not subtract four. And a probe that waits for an interrupt must mark progress from *both* sides, handler and main flow, or an unacknowledged source is read as a return failure."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/irq3.s` and the intermediate builds described in `.ai/core-log.md` entry 62"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 captures showing three white handler markers with squares 3 and 4 black, before the address was corrected"
- record_id: TCL-044
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Breakpoint addresses taken from an earlier build silently test nothing"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "A GDB session was used to find where an interrupt probe stopped. The breakpoints were taken from `nm` output of the previous build, before a prologue was added; every symbol had moved by about 0x20. The breakpoint labelled 'handler entry' at `0x0800031c` therefore sat in dead code and never fired, and the one labelled 'loop exit' at `0x08000224` sat on the loop's first instruction and fired immediately. The session produced the confident conclusion that no interrupt was ever dispatched, which was false -- the handler was being entered normally. Re-reading `nm` from the current object gave `_irq = 0x33C`, `spin = 0x224`, `spin_done = 0x244`, and the corrected breakpoints showed the handler hit first, as expected."
  consequence: "Any symbol address used to instrument a binary must be re-read from that binary after the last edit, not carried forward from an earlier build; a probe edit of a few instructions moves every address after it. A breakpoint that never fires is not evidence of absence unless the address is known to be in live code, so the address has to be confirmed against the current image -- here `objdump` of the flattened `.gba` would have shown it immediately."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `arm-none-eabi-nm -n` on `tools/roms/irq3.s` before and after the prologue was added"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 gdb stub sessions recorded in `.ai/core-log.md` entry 62"

- record_id: BRD-058
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "BIOS SWI dispatch works on the board: SWI 0x06 Div returns the correct quotient and remainder"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "`tools/roms/swidiv.s` paints a blue square when the cartridge reaches its first BIOS call and a green square when `swi 0x06` returns quotient 142 and remainder 6 for 1000 / 7. It passed in mGBA 0.10.5 with the retail BIOS first, per TCL-041, and the board agrees exactly: blue `(0, 0, 232)` and green `(18, 255, 0)`. The BIOS call path a cartridge uses is therefore measured good on this hardware. Getting to this probe cost one more instrument defect of a kind that produces no error message: on GBA ARM code the SWI number must be encoded in bits 23-16 (`swi 0x060000`), because the BIOS dispatcher reads it with `ldrb r12,[lr,#-2]` and in ARM mode `lr = address + 4`, so the byte fetched is at `address + 2`. Writing the natural `swi 0x06` assembles to `06 00 00 EF`, the dispatcher reads `0x00`, and calls SWI 0 -- SoftReset -- which reboots the cartridge. Every such probe restarted itself instead of returning; the screen showed the BIOS's white backdrop because `DISPCNT` had not been written yet, and under HLE the same thing happened. Recorded as TCL-045."
  consequence: "SWI dispatch joins interrupt dispatch (BRD-057) among the execution paths measured good on this board. The two remaining candidates for a cartridge that draws one text row and stops are now the software the cartridge runs against specific hardware: the `VBlankIntrWait` wait itself, which this probe could not measure because it deadlocks against any installed handler, and the DMA `REQ1_START` deadlock recorded as BRD-045, which was verified before the display worked and has not been revisited. The first of those needs a probe that installs no handler at all and lets the BIOS own the interrupt, which is what retail software does; the handler this probe installed was the thing preventing the measurement rather than the thing being measured."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/swidiv.s` and `build/test/swidiv.gba`"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 with `/tmp/official_bios.bin` (SHA1 300c20df6731a33952ded8c436f7f186d25d3492): square 0 blue, square 1 green"
    - "Board capture `/tmp/board_swidiv.png`: square 0 `(0, 0, 232)`, square 1 `(18, 255, 0)`"
    - "The retail BIOS disassembly: vector `0x08: b 0x140`, dispatcher `0x140: push {r11,r12,lr}`, `0x144: ldrb r12,[lr,#-2]`, `0x148: add r11,pc,#120`"
- record_id: TCL-045
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "GBA ARM SWI numbers go in bits 23-16, because the BIOS reads the number from the byte at lr-2"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "A probe that called `swi 0x06` to test division instead rebooted the machine. The instruction assembles to `06 00 00 EF`, so the bytes in memory are 0x06 at the instruction address and 0x00 at address + 2. An mGBA gdb session stepping the call showed the sequence: `pc` 0x08000100 to 0x00000008 (the SWI vector `b 0x140`) to 0x140, where the dispatcher executes `ldrb r12,[lr,#-2]`. With `lr = 0x08000104` in ARM mode, `lr-2` is address + 2, which held 0x00, so the BIOS dispatched SWI 0 -- SoftReset -- and the cartridge restarted. Encoding the number as `swi 0x060000` puts it at address + 2 and the call returns correctly. Every earlier attempt in this session had been misread as a hang, and under both the retail BIOS and HLE, because a reboot and a hang look the same from a screenshot."
  consequence: "Any hand-written ARM SWI in this tree must be written `swi 0x0N0000`. The natural spelling compiles without a warning, runs, and silently restarts the cartridge, so the mistake produces no error at any stage and cannot be caught by reading the source. It can be caught by disassembling the built image and reading the byte at `instruction + 2`, which is worth doing for any probe that makes a BIOS call. It also explains an observation that had been carried as unexplained: a cartridge appearing to return to the BIOS startup sequence is consistent with SWI 0 being called by mistake, not only with a machine fault."
  sources:
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 gdb stub: `pc` 0x08000100 -> 0x00000008 -> 0x00000140, `ldrb r12,[lr,#-2]` at 0x144"
    - "`/tmp/official_bios.bin`, SHA1 300c20df6731a33952ded8c436f7f186d25d3492, vector table `0x08: ea00004c b 0x140`"
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/swidiv.s`"

- record_id: BRD-059
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "BRD-045 no longer reproduces: Super Mario Advance boot-loops the BIOS instead of freezing in REQ1_WAIT"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "On `build/deploy_prod` reflashed 2026-11-14, Super Mario Advance (`build/test/roms/sma.gba`, SHA-256 `d29dec02caacbf44...`, the same file BRD-045 tested) does not freeze. It boots the BIOS, renders the Game Boy logo and the Nintendo wordmark, and then restarts from the BIOS entry, for as long as it is left running. A 60-second capture at 2 fps (120 frames, 96 unique) shows the cycle repeating with a period of about 4.5 s and no exact frame periodicity, because the machine's speed varies within the cycle. The decisive observation is the direction of the animation: each cycle draws the logo letters in from the left, one at a time, followed by the rainbow colour sweep on the Game Boy lettering, so the animation starts from its beginning every cycle rather than being re-entered part-way. That is a reset to the BIOS entry and not a hung animation. A cartridge cannot return to the BIOS by itself; the two mechanisms are a CPU exception, whose four vectors all branch to the BIOS handler at `0x1C`, and SWI 0 `SoftReset`, which writes `POSTFLG` at `0x04000300`. The board captures are `/tmp/loop/f_001..120.png`, and the operator watching the same screen independently reported a BIOS boot loop. mGBA 0.10.5 with the official BIOS boots and runs the same image normally, so the cartridge is sound."
  consequence: "BRD-045's freeze is not reproducible on the current image, and its wording must not be quoted as the current behaviour of Super Mario Advance: the machine no longer stops in `REQ1_WAIT` with `cpu_en` low and zero `sdram_ready` pulses, it executes and then resets. This is the third distinct board behaviour recorded for a retail cartridge on one fixed bitstream -- BRD-014's bimodal halt, BRD-045's `REQ1_WAIT` freeze, and now a reset loop -- so the machine's behaviour across carts and loads is multimodal rather than bimodal, and no single capture of any cart may be treated as the machine's behaviour. The reset mechanism itself is not yet identified: exception and `SoftReset` are both consistent with these captures, and separating them needs the MMIO write ring to record a write to `POSTFLG`, which is compiled out of the production build. Until that is measured this record establishes only that the machine resets, not why."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/deploy_prod/tinygba.fs` (reflashed with `openFPGALoader -b tangconsole` from `~/oss-cad-suite/bin`, 2026-11-14)"
    - "This repository, `build/test/roms/sma.gba`, SHA-256 prefix `d29dec02caacbf44`"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/loop/f_001..120.png`, 60 s at 2 fps, and `/tmp/sma_1..4.png`"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 with `/tmp/official_bios.bin`, running the same image to its intro normally"
    - "The retail BIOS disassembly: `0x1C: ldr sp,[pc,#416]`, and the four word constants at `0x274`/`0x278` that the handler dispatches through"
- record_id: BRD-060
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "A 1 MiB retail cartridge completes the BIOS and then never enables the display, on a freshly loaded core"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Classic NES Series - Dr. Mario (USA, Europe), 1,048,576 bytes exactly, was extracted from its zip, loaded over UART and captured for 45 seconds at 2 fps after a Tang power cycle had been followed by an explicit reflash of `build/deploy_prod/tinygba.fs`. The first fraction of a second shows the dark letterbox, and every frame from 0.5 s onward is uniform white in the active area: 90 frames, 4 unique, no logo, no tile content, no transition. White is what the BIOS leaves on screen at handover, with `DISPCNT` in forced blank, so the cartridge never reaches the point of enabling its display. The same image boots normally in mGBA 0.10.5 with the official BIOS, where its title screen is black and green, and the operator confirmed the boot independently. Choosing a 1 MiB cartridge was deliberate: BRD-053 measured cartridge reads at every 64 KiB boundary to 1 MiB, so this image's whole address range has been read correctly by this machine and the failure is not a reach limit of the read path."
  consequence: "Two retail cartridges of different sizes now fail in two different ways on one bitstream -- Super Mario Advance resets the machine (BRD-059) and Dr. Mario stops without ever writing `DISPCNT` -- while the purpose-built `test_logo` cart passes all eight of its own checks (BRD-051). The shared factor is not cartridge size, because BRD-053 reads to 1 MiB and `hioffset` executes at 448 KiB, and it is not the BIOS, which runs identically to mGBA. The difference between `test_logo` and these cartridges is that `test_logo` verifies a handful of addresses in each region while a retail `crt0` copies code and data across the whole of IWRAM and EWRAM before `main` runs. A write that lands at the wrong address, or a read that returns another address's data, would pass every probe built in this session, because those probes write and read back the same address immediately through the same path. That is the leading hypothesis this record creates and does not establish, and it is testable by writing unique patterns to many addresses and reading them all back."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/test/drmario.gba`, extracted from `/run/media/vash/MiSTer_Data/games/GBA/Classic NES Series - Dr. Mario (USA, Europe).zip`, SHA-256 prefix `0c8e7923a5fbb99f`"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/d2/d_001..010.png` and `/tmp/d3/d_001..090.png` (45 s at 2 fps, 90 frames, 4 unique)"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 with `/tmp/official_bios.bin`: title screen renders, dominant colours black and green"
    - "This repository, records BRD-051, BRD-053, BRD-059"

- record_id: BRD-061
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "IWRAM and EWRAM hold unique patterns across their whole regions on the board, before and after full sweeps"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "BRD-060 raised the hypothesis that a write could be landing at the wrong address, which every earlier probe would have passed because they all read back the same address they had just written through the same path. `tools/roms/memorder.s` tests that directly: it fills a whole region with index * 257 + 0xA5A5, which is injective in the index so two addresses aliasing each other show up as a mismatch rather than cancelling out, then reads the whole region back and counts mismatches. It was required to pass in mGBA 0.10.5 with the official BIOS before any board result was read, per TCL-041, and it does. The board reports all four squares green: EWRAM 65536 words (256 KiB) before any other sweep, IWRAM 8192 words (32 KiB), EWRAM 65536 words again after the IWRAM sweep, and a reached-the-end marker. `tools/roms/memsize.s` independently confirms EWRAM at 1024, 8192, 32768 and 65536 words, all green in mGBA. So every word of both internal RAM regions holds its own value on this hardware, and neither a full IWRAM sweep nor a full EWRAM sweep disturbs the other region or itself."
  consequence: "The address-aliasing hypothesis BRD-060 raised is refuted for IWRAM and EWRAM: a write reaches the address it was given and a read returns that address's data, across 288 KiB of internal RAM, on the board. That eliminates a class of explanation that would have accounted for every symptom at once -- the striped tilemap, the wrong glyph row and the retail hangs -- and it means the corruption seen in BRD-054 and in the striped diag capture must arise somewhere other than the storage holding its value. The regions this probe does not cover are VRAM, palette RAM and OAM, which are separate BRAM blocks with their own decoders, and the DMA engines that a cartridge's crt0 uses to move data into all of them. Two probe defects were found and discarded on the way to this result and neither is recorded as a finding: `tools/roms/memtest.s` reported EWRAM failing in mGBA while `memorder` and `memsize` reported it passing with instruction-identical fill and read-back loops, so it was deleted as a broken instrument rather than repaired, and its DMA checks were never read. The useful lesson is recorded as TCL-046."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/memorder.s` and `build/test/memorder.gba`"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 with `/tmp/official_bios.bin`: four squares green"
    - "Board capture `/tmp/bd_mo.png`: four squares `(18, 255, 0)`"
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/memsize.s`: 1024, 8192, 32768 and 65536 words all green in mGBA"
    - "This repository, records BRD-060 and TCL-041"
- record_id: TCL-046
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "When two probes of the same operation disagree, the one that disagrees with the validated control is the broken one"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "`tools/roms/memtest.s` and `tools/roms/memsize.s` perform the same EWRAM fill-and-read-back over the same 65536 words, with fill and read-back loops that disassemble to the same five instructions apart from whether the loop bound is a literal immediate or a register loaded from the literal pool. `memtest` reported four of its five checks failing under mGBA with the official BIOS, including the EWRAM check; `memsize` and `memorder` reported the same EWRAM check passing, and `memorder` reported it passing both before and after a full IWRAM sweep. mGBA is the validated control, so the divergence says one of the probes is wrong, and the two that agree with each other and with the control are not the broken ones. The cause in `memtest` was never established, because establishing it would have cost more than deleting the probe and keeping the two that agree: further work on it would have produced no measurement about the machine either way."
  consequence: "A probe disagreement is itself a reading, and the right response is to find the side of the disagreement that also disagrees with the control. This is TCL-041 applied to a probe-versus-probe case rather than a probe-versus-machine case, and it is cheap because the control already exists and costs about a minute per run. The corollary is that a probe whose result cannot be reconciled with a validated probe of the same operation must be discarded rather than debugged indefinitely, since the only thing it can produce is a wrong finding about the machine. `memtest.s` was deleted under this rule; `memsize.s` and `memorder.s` remain, both clean in mGBA and both run on the board."
  sources:
    - "mGBA 0.10.5: `memtest.gba` four of five checks red on two separate runs, `memsize.gba` four of four green, `memorder.gba` four of four green"
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/memorder.s`, `tools/roms/memsize.s`"

- record_id: BRD-062
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "SUPERSEDED by BRD-064: DMA3 from cartridge ROM was measured writing zeroes on the board -- the observation holds for that image, the RTL attribution below does not"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "`tools/roms/dmawrote.s` pre-fills the destination with a sentinel, runs one DMA3 transfer from cartridge ROM, and then reports three independent things about the destination: whether it changed at all, whether it holds the source data, and whether it is exactly zero. The binary is validated in mGBA 0.10.5 with the official BIOS before the board result is read, per TCL-041. mGBA reports changed, correct and not-zero, which is what a working transfer looks like. The board reports changed GREEN, correct RED and zero GREEN, identically on three consecutive captures: the destination did change from its sentinel, it is not the source data, and it is exactly zero. So the DMA engine runs, completes, and writes zeroes into the destination instead of the words it read. This is a board-only fault: the same binary transfers the same data correctly under mGBA. The RTL is consistent with it. In `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv` the BRAM write data is driven from `dma_wdata` in `state == MAIN` only, and the buffered write path is armed by `if (~dma_on & rom_en & ram_cen)`, which is false for every cycle of a DMA because `dma_on` is high throughout. A transfer whose source is SDRAM and whose destination is BRAM therefore never presents the fetched data to the destination's write port, and the destination's write enable can be asserted with whatever `dma_wdata` happens to hold. IWRAM and VRAM are wired identically here -- `wre(bram_wr & sel_iwram)` and `vram_lo_we = bram_wr & sel_vram`, both with `bram_wdata` -- so this account predicts the same fault for both, which is what was measured."
  consequence: "SUPERSEDED 2026-11-14 by BRD-064. The board measurements here are sound for the image they were taken on, but the explanation is not: the netlist behind that image was mis-synthesised, with 72 conflicting drivers over `issingle`, `isreadonly` and `tosdram`, so the memory and DMA arbitration the source describes was not what the hardware contained. The RTL reasoning below -- that the fetched data never reaches the write port -- is therefore unproven and is contradicted by BRD-064's minimal reproduction. What survives: the board really did fail every DMA test, BRD-055 really was withdrawn, and TCL-047's rule about zero-valued expectations stands on its own.

This is the largest finding of the session and it withdraws BRD-055. DMA3 is not a working path on this machine: it completes without wedging and destroys the data. A retail cartridge's `crt0` copies its code, data section and BSS into IWRAM and EWRAM with DMA before `main` runs, which explains every symptom this investigation has chased: a cartridge that copies zeroes into IWRAM jumps into a zero-filled region, executes the ARM encoding of zero which is `andeq r0, r0, r0` repeated, and either runs off into unmapped memory (the reset loop of BRD-059) or wedges (the white screen of BRD-060). It also explains the whole shape of the evidence: `test_logo.gba` passes every check and draws correctly because it uses CPU stores and no DMA, every per-operation probe built in this session used CPU stores, and the two cartridges that fail are exactly the ones that use DMA. BRD-045's `REQ1_WAIT` wedge and this fault are different failures of the same path and both point at the DMA-to-BRAM write, so the defect to fix is there and not in the address decode. The corollary is that `minidma`'s green was never evidence of anything: its expected value was `0x0000`, the low half of a source word that is `0x12340000`, so a transfer that writes zeroes passes it. That is recorded as TCL-047 and it is why BRD-055 is withdrawn rather than merely qualified."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/dmawrote.s` and `build/test/dmawrote.gba`"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/bd_dw2_1..3.png`: changed green, correct red, zero green, end yellow, identical on all three"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 with `/tmp/official_bios.bin`, same binary: changed green, correct green, zero red, end yellow"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: the `bram_wdata = dma_wdata` assignment in `state == MAIN`, the `if (~dma_on & rom_en & ram_cen)` guard, and the `sel_iwram`/`sel_vram` write enables at lines 850 and 900"
- record_id: TCL-047
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A probe whose expected value equals the failure mode certifies a broken path"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "`build/test/minidma.gba` was accepted for three days as proof that DMA3 from cartridge ROM to frame memory works on the board, and it passed in mGBA and on the board alike. Its test was this: clear frame memory to `0x001F`, run the transfer, read the destination and paint green if it holds `0x0000`. The expected value `0x0000` is the low half of the source word `0x12340000`, and it is also exactly what a transfer that writes zeroes leaves behind. So the probe reported success for a machine that transfers nothing useful, and it reported the same green for a machine that transfers correctly. It was not until a probe asked three separate questions -- did the destination change, does it hold the source, is it zero -- that the two cases separated, and on the board they separate as changed-and-zero against mGBA's changed-and-correct."
  consequence: "An expected value must be chosen so that the failure cannot produce it. A zero expected value is the worst choice available, because an unwritten, a zeroed and a disabled path all yield it; every probe in this tree that expects `0x0000` should be re-read for that reason. The general form is to compare against a distinctive pattern and to ask separately whether the location changed at all, since those two answers distinguish 'nothing happened' from 'something happened wrongly' and the second is the more valuable finding. This record is why BRD-055 is withdrawn rather than qualified: its conclusion was not weakly supported, it was unsupported, and the appearance of support came from the probe's own blind spot."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/minidma.s`, whose comparison is against `0x0000`"
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/dmawrote.s`, whose three questions separate the cases on the same board"
    - "Board and mGBA results recorded in BRD-062"

- record_id: BRD-063
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "SUPERSEDED by BRD-064: the DMA failed for every source and destination tested on the board -- the observation holds, the conclusion that the SDRAM read path was cleared does not"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "BRD-062 measured DMA3 from cartridge ROM into IWRAM writing zeroes. That left two ends suspect, the SDRAM read or the BRAM write, and two earlier probes could not separate them because they never verified their own preconditions: a destination that does not hold the pattern is equally consistent with a transfer that did not run and with a pre-fill that never landed. `tools/roms/dmastep.s` removes that ambiguity. It fills 16 IWRAM words with 0x11111111 by CPU stores, writes 0xF00D to the destination and reads it straight back, reads the source back, then runs one DMA3 of 16 words from IWRAM to IWRAM and compares. The binary passes in mGBA 0.10.5 with the official BIOS, where all three checks are green. On the board, on two consecutive captures, the store-lands check is green, the source-reads check is green, and the DMA-copied check is red, with the destination holding neither the pattern nor its pre-fill. `tools/roms/dmaside.s` varies the source across four combinations and all four are red on the board while all four are green in mGBA: IWRAM to EWRAM, IWRAM to IWRAM, EWRAM to IWRAM and cartridge ROM to IWRAM. The IWRAM-to-IWRAM case is the one that matters, because both ends are internal RAM, no SDRAM access is involved on either side, and the CPU is demonstrably reading and writing that same memory correctly in the same probe. The same two probes run correctly in the Verilator model of this tree built with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL`, i.e. against the real `sdram_gba.v` controller and the pin-level SDRAM model rather than the behavioural array: the DMA write trace prints `adr=3004000 dat=11111111` for every word of the IWRAM-to-IWRAM transfer, and `adr=3001000 dat=11111111` then `22222222` and so on for the ROM-to-IWRAM one. So the logic is correct in RTL and the fault appears only on the board."
  consequence: "SUPERSEDED 2026-11-14 by BRD-064. The claim below that the SDRAM read path is cleared, and that the fault is localised to the write data, rested on reasoning about a netlist whose arbitration logic was resolved arbitrarily by Yosys. It is retracted. What stands is the measurement: on that image every DMA source and destination failed, including BRAM to BRAM, and `dmastep` did verify its own preconditions green before the transfer failed -- which is why TCL-048's rule about self-verifying probes is worth keeping.

The SDRAM read path is cleared and the fault is localised to the DMA write data path, specifically to the value `gba_memory` presents on `bram_wdata` during the write cycle. That is a narrower and different statement than BRD-062 could make, and it rules out the whole class of explanations about cartridge reads, EWRAM and the SDRAM controller. It also rules out the design's own logic, because the identical RTL transfers the identical data correctly in simulation against the real controller. What remains is something that differs only in hardware: the timing relationship between the DMA module, which runs on `clk100` and drives `dma_bus_dout` combinationally, and `gba_memory`, which runs on `clk16` and samples `bram_wdata` on its own edge. Two attempts were made to fix this by latching the DMA read data inside `gba_memory` -- on the raw ready, and on the acknowledgement cycle -- and both were reverted without being taken to the board, because each changed the data seen in simulation, which proves they were altering working behaviour rather than repairing broken behaviour. A fix cannot be validated against a model that does not exhibit the fault, so the next step is an instrument, not a patch. Four probes were written this cycle and two discarded (`dma3dest`, `dmaalias`, `dmaalias64`, `dmamin`): each compared only against an expected pattern and so conflated 'not run' with 'ran wrongly', which is TCL-048."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/dmastep.s` and `build/test/dmastep.gba`"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/bd_dst_1..2.png`: store green, source green, copied red, end yellow, no magenta bar"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/bd_ds_1..2.png`: all four source/destination combinations red, marker yellow"
    - "mGBA 0.10.5 with `/tmp/official_bios.bin`, same binaries: all checks green, all four combinations green"
    - "Verilator build of this tree with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL -DTINYGBA_DMADBG`: `DMAWR: adr=3004000 dat=11111111` for IWRAM to IWRAM and `adr=3001000 dat=11111111`, `adr=3001004 dat=22222222` for ROM to IWRAM"
- record_id: TCL-048
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A probe must verify its own preconditions, or 'not run' and 'ran wrongly' are the same reading"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Four DMA probes were written in one cycle before the fifth answered the question. `dma3dest`, `dmaalias`, `dmaalias64` and `dmamin` each pre-filled a destination, ran a transfer and compared the result against the expected pattern. Every one of them read red on the board. None of them could say why, because a destination that does not hold the pattern is exactly what is seen when the transfer never ran, when the pre-fill never landed, when the transfer wrote the wrong data, and when the transfer wrote the right data to the wrong address. `dmastep` resolved it by reading its own destination back immediately after the pre-fill and its source back immediately before the transfer, and reporting both as checks. Those two checks came back green on the board, which converted an ambiguous red into a definite statement: the memory works, the values are there, and the transfer is what fails."
  consequence: "Every probe should carry a check that the state it depends on is really present, in the same image and immediately before the measurement, so that a failure downstream cannot be a failure upstream.
