This is the probe's own version of TCL-041: the control has to be part of the instrument rather than assumed from a previous run. The cost is a few instructions and one square; the cost of omitting it was four discarded probes and a cycle of hardware measurements that could not be interpreted."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/roms/dmastep.s`, whose first two checks are its own preconditions"
    - "Board and mGBA results recorded in BRD-063"

- record_id: BRD-064
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The board image was mis-synthesised: static helper functions in gba_memory produced 72 conflicting drivers over the memory and DMA arbitration, and the fault is absent from Verilator"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "This is the root cause of BRD-059, BRD-060, BRD-062 and BRD-063, and it makes their common reading -- a hardware-only DMA fault -- wrong. Three helper functions in `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`, `issingle`, `isreadonly` and `tosdram`, were declared without `automatic`. In SystemVerilog a static function has a single shared module-level variable for each argument, so every call site drives the same variable; these three are called from the BRAM-request `always @*`, the SDRAM-request `always @*`, the clocked FSM and two more combinational blocks. Yosys reported 72 `multiple conflicting drivers` warnings against `mem.tosdram.addr` (56), `mem.issingle.region` (8) and `mem.isreadonly.region` (8), and those were the only multi-driven signals in the whole design -- exactly the memory and DMA arbitration. The warning was present in every synthesis of this tree, including `build/prod_nwl` behind the deployed bitstream, and had been overlooked. A minimal reproduction with the same call pattern, same Yosys 0.69+260 and same yosys-slang front end confirms the consequence is silicon-visible rather than cosmetic: with the static declaration the Gowin flow emits four input buffers all driving the same net which is also tied to GND, and the combinational output tied to VCC, i.e. constant 1; with `function automatic` the same source emits a correct 4-bit bus and real LUTs. Verilator inlines function calls per call site and never sees it, which is why every simulation of this design -- including the Verilator model built with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` against the real `sdram_gba.v` controller -- behaved correctly while the bitstream did not. Declaring all static functions `automatic` in the tree (`gba_memory.sv` four, `calc_byte_ena`, `clip512`, `torgb`, `log2` and the six `*_colour` helpers) takes the conflicting-driver count from 72 to 0, with flip-flops 11,509 to 11,571 and cells 31,856 to 31,948."
  consequence: "Every hardware reading this project has taken was taken on an image whose memory and DMA arbitration was resolved arbitrarily by Yosys, so any result that depends on `issingle`, `isreadonly` or `tosdram` is only as trustworthy as the driver Yosys happened to pick. That is the honest scope of the damage and it cannot be repaired by re-reading old captures: the affected paths are DMA routing and completion, EWRAM and cartridge address translation, and the read-only distinction. It also means the search that produced BRD-062 and BRD-063 was reasoning about a netlist that did not implement the source. What survives is the parts of the record where the CPU path happened to resolve correctly, which is consistent with the BIOS running correctly, `test_logo` passing its own eight checks, and the ALU, byte-load, SWI and VRAM probes passing -- none of them depends on the corrupted decisions. `tools/build_tinygba.sh` now carries a gate that fails the build on any `multiple conflicting drivers` warning, reading both `synth.err` and `synth.log` because those two files have been seen to hold different counts of the same warning on one design. The undriven-wire count is unchanged at 417 and is a separate hazard of the same family, not addressed here."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/prod_nwl/synth.err` and `synth.log`: 72 `multiple conflicting drivers`, all in `mem.tosdram.addr`, `mem.issingle.region`, `mem.isreadonly.region`"
    - "This repository, `build/fix_auto/synth.log`: 0 conflicting drivers after the change, 11,571 flip-flops and 31,948 cells"
    - "Minimal reproduction at `/tmp/sfrepro/t.sv` with Yosys 0.69+260: static gives 5 conflicts with `a[3:0]` shorted to GND and `y_comb` tied to VCC; `function automatic` gives 0 conflicts and correct logic"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`, the three function declarations at lines 939, 946 and 957"
    - "Records BRD-059, BRD-060, BRD-062 and BRD-063, whose common reading this supersedes"
- record_id: TCL-049
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A static SystemVerilog function called from more than one process is a netlist defect that no simulation can see"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "SystemVerilog gives a function declared without `automatic` static storage: the arguments are one set of module-level variables shared by every call. When such a function is called from two or more processes, each process drives those variables, `proc` turns each call site into a latch or a flop on the shared signal, and the result is a signal with conflicting drivers. Yosys warns about it but proceeds, and the netlist JSON carries no trace of the conflict, so the warning is the only evidence. Measured with the production front end: a three-process reproduction synthesises through `synth_gowin -family gw5a -nowidelut` into four input buffers that all drive one net which is additionally tied to GND, plus a combinational output tied to VCC. This is not a corner case -- it is the ordinary result of the mistake, and it produces constant outputs on real silicon. Verilator and the Gowin vendor flow both inline function calls per call site, so neither can reproduce it, and the fault therefore exists only in the netlist that is actually loaded onto the board."
  consequence: "Three rules follow. Every function in this tree that is called from more than one process must be `automatic`; the safe form is to declare all of them so, since a pure function gains nothing from static storage. Any synthesis log must be checked for `multiple conflicting drivers`, and because Yosys resolves the conflict silently the count is the only signal, so the build now fails on it. And when a fault appears on hardware but not in simulation, the synthesis log is the first place to look rather than the last, because a difference between the model and the bitstream has to be created by the synthesis step -- no simulation of this design can see a synthesis defect. This is the second instance of the family after the `gb_bus_rst` fold of 2026-11-02, where an undriven wire became constant 1 under `synth_gowin` and deleted about 4,500 flip-flops: both are cases where the open flow's stricter semantics diverged from the vendor's and the difference was invisible to every model."
  sources:
    - "Minimal reproduction `/tmp/sfrepro/t.sv` and `t2.sv`, Yosys 0.69+260 with yosys-slang, `synth_gowin -family gw5a -nowidelut`"
    - "This repository, `build/prod_nwl/synth.err`: 72 conflicting-driver warnings"
    - "This repository, `tools/build_tinygba.sh`, the synthesis-conflict gate"

- record_id: BRD-065
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The DMA transmitted stale buffer data because `dma_bus_unread` was undriven, which the simulator reads as 0 and `synth_gowin` folds away"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "This is the mechanism behind the DMA measurements of BRD-062 and BRD-063, and it is separate from BRD-064's static-function defect: either one alone would break DMA, and both were present. `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_dma_module.sv` chooses what to transmit immediately after a read: `if (addr_source >= 'h200_0000 && !dma_bus_unread) dmaout <= DMAOUT_DIN;` -- transmit the word just read -- `else begin dmaout <= DMAOUT_BUF; dma_bus_dout_buf <= last_dma_in; end` -- transmit the previously buffered word. At the top level `dma_bus_unread` was connected to nothing; even its declaration was commented out (`//dma_bus_unread;`). Verilator reads an undriven wire as 0, so `!dma_bus_unread` is 1 and the simulation takes the first arm and transfers real data. `synth_gowin` reads it as `x`, `opt` folds `x && ...` away, and the condition becomes constant false, so the bitstream takes the second arm on every transfer and transmits `last_dma_in`, which is zero on a first transfer. That is precisely the measured behaviour: the destination changed, was not the source data, and was exactly zero, for all four source-and-destination combinations including IWRAM to IWRAM, while the same binary transferred the correct words in simulation. Reproduced minimally with the production front end: a single undriven wire in this exact condition, through `setundef -zero -init` and `synth_gowin -family gw5a -nowidelut`, emits a GND-tied output, and the same source with the wire driven low emits a real ALU comparison. The fix drives `dma_bus_unread` to 0, which is the honest value because nothing in this port marks an address unreadable and it is what the simulation has always assumed, and the build now runs `setundef -undriven -zero` so the remaining 417 undriven nets receive the same zero that both Verilator and the Gowin vendor flow already give them."
  consequence: "Two independent synthesis hazards each destroyed the DMA path, and neither was visible to any simulation, so the entire search from BRD-045 through BRD-063 was conducted on bitstreams whose memory and DMA logic did not match the source. The general lesson is sharper than BRD-064's: an undriven wire is not merely a warning, it is a place where the model and the bitstream diverge, because every simulator supplies a value and `opt` is free to fold the expression away entirely. `setundef -undriven -zero` removes that class of divergence by making the flow agree with what the simulation and the vendor tool both already do. What remains unverified is whether these two fixes are sufficient: the bitstream built from them has not yet been run on the board, and the probes that must confirm it are `tools/roms/dmastep.s` and `tools/roms/dmawrote.s` for the DMA path and then the retail cartridges for the machine. Until that is measured, the honest statement is that the two known synthesis defects are fixed and the DMA fault is expected to be repaired, not that it is."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_dma_module.sv` line 438, the condition on `dma_bus_unread`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`, where `.dma_bus_unread()` was connected to nothing and `//dma_bus_unread;` was commented out"
    - "Minimal reproduction `/tmp/unread/t.sv`: undriven gives a GND-tied output, tied low gives an ALU comparison, with Yosys 0.69+260 and `synth_gowin -family gw5a -nowidelut`"
    - "Verilator build with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL -DTINYGBA_DMADBG` after the fix: `DMAWR: adr=3004000 dat=11111111`, unchanged"
    - "Records BRD-062, BRD-063 and BRD-064"
- record_id: TCL-050
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "An undriven wire is where the simulator and the bitstream silently disagree"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Verilator gives an undriven wire the value 0. The Gowin vendor flow ties it low. Plain yosys treats it as `x` and `opt` is then free to fold any expression containing it -- `x && cond` becomes a constant, and the logic that depended on the condition disappears without a warning beyond `used but has no driver`. This design had 417 such nets, and one of them, `dma_bus_unread`, sat in the single condition that decides whether a DMA transfer transmits the word it just read or a stale buffer. The measurement that exposed it was BRD-063's: the destination changed, was not the source, and was exactly zero -- the signature of transmitting a buffer rather than the data. Driving the wire to 0 fixed the minimal reproduction and left the simulation's behaviour unchanged, which is what confirms that 0 was the value the model had been assuming."
  consequence: "An undriven net must be treated as a correctness hazard rather than a tidiness warning, because no simulation can reveal the difference -- the simulation supplies the value the hardware may not. The build now applies `setundef -undriven -zero`, which makes the flow match both the simulator and the vendor tool; that is a floor, not a fix, and the 417 nets should still be driven properly. When a hardware result disagrees with a simulation that includes the real controllers, the synthesis log should be read for `used but has no driver` as well as for `multiple conflicting drivers`, since those two warnings are the whole vocabulary of ways the netlist can differ from the source in this flow."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/fix_auto/synth.log`: 417 `used but has no driver` warnings, including `dma.dma_bus_unread` and `CNT_H_Game_Pak_DRQ`"
    - "Minimal reproduction `/tmp/unread/t.sv` and the same source with the wire tied low"
    - "This repository, `tools/build_tinygba.sh`, the `setundef -undriven -zero` line"

- record_id: BRD-066
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "A retail cartridge runs on the board: the RetroRegen diag cart, Dr. Mario and Super Mario Advance all draw correct pictures after the REQ2_START revert"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "The production image `build/rev_prod` (seed 14, `TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, official BIOS, `-nowidelut`, `.fs` from a netlist with 0 conflicting drivers and no timing relaxation) is the first bitstream in this project on which a retail cartridge has drawn a correct picture. Three cartridges were loaded over UART and captured from `/dev/video0` at 1920x1080. The RetroRegen diagnostic cartridge draws its complete title screen: the RetroRegen logo artwork, `HANDHELD HARDWARE TEST SUITE`, and `V1.5 (C)2026 RETROREGEN.COM`, 491,509 lit pixels across 16 captures of which 3 were unique. Dr. Mario (Classic NES Series, 1 MiB) draws its title screen: the blue-and-pink Dr. Mario capsule logo, the green checkered background, the `1 PLAYER GAME` panel with the Mario sprite, the virus sprite, and `(C) 1990 Nintendo`, 1,293,791 lit pixels, 7 unique frames of 40. Super Mario Advance (4 MiB) draws and animates its intro: sky, clouds and palm trees with 60 unique frames out of 60 in one capture and 79 out of 80 across 80 seconds, so it is continuously animating rather than looping. The operator, watching the same screen, reported no graphics glitch in any of the three. The change that produced this is the two-line return to upstream recorded in commit `f38cebe`: `REQ2_START: state <= REQ2_WAIT;` and `cpu_en = state == REQ2_BRAM | state == REQ2_WAIT & sdram_ready;`. The same change was first validated in the harness against the real `sdram_gba.v` controller, where the diagnostic cartridge went from one garbled orange text row to the complete title screen."
  consequence: "This closes the goal the project has been working toward since it opened: a retail GBA cartridge executes and renders on this hardware. It also retires a long list of open items as explained rather than merely unobserved -- BRD-014's bimodality, BRD-045's `REQ1_WAIT` wedge, BRD-054's wrong glyph row, BRD-059's BIOS boot loop, BRD-060's white screen, BRD-062 and BRD-063's DMA failures and BRD-064 and BRD-065's synthesis defects are all consistent with a memory FSM that completed the second request of a double access before its data arrived. What this record does NOT establish: it says nothing about save types, EEPROM or flash, because none was exercised; nothing about the link port, the RTC or the solar sensor, which no cartridge here uses; and nothing about audio beyond the operator's report that it is audible with correct stereo separation and scratchy in timbre, which is BRD-067 and is unmeasured. Attribution of the fix to a specific mechanism is deliberately not claimed here: the two-line revert is confirmed to work and the mechanism proposed for it is not confirmed, as `DISSENT-req2-start-mechanism-2026-11-14.md` records."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/rev_prod/tinygba.fs`, built from commit `f38cebe`"

- record_id: BRD-067
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Audio is audible with correct stereo separation on HDMI and scratchy in timbre -- the first observation of sound from this board, unmeasured"
  status: LEAD
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "With `build/rev_prod` running Super Mario Advance, the operator reports that audio is produced, that stereo separation is correct on the HDMI output, and that the timbre is scratchy. This is the first time any sound has been observed from this board: every earlier probe avoided the sound path deliberately, and `tinygba.cst` has no audio pins because the only output is HDMI TMDS audio packets through `gba2hdmi`/`gba2hdmi_ddr3`. The `gba_sound` module, its four channels and the FIFO-A/B DMA feeding them have never been measured on hardware, and six of the 46 undriven wires in `build/fix_auto/synth.log` are in the sound path -- `Channel_Sweep_Time`, `Channel_Sweep_Frequency_Direction`, `Channel_Number_of_sweep_shift` and `Channel_HighZero` in `gba_sound_ch1`, now tied to zero by `setundef -undriven -zero`, which removes channel 1's frequency sweep. That alone cannot make the other three channels scratchy, so it is a candidate and not the explanation."
  consequence: "Audio moves from 'never observed' to 'observed with a defect described by ear', which is a much better starting point but is not a measurement. The next step is an instrument rather than a guess, and the cheapest one is a probe cartridge that plays a known waveform: a single tone through channel 1 with sweep disabled, then the same tone through channel 3 (wave channel), then through channel 4 (noise), each held for a second, so the operator can say which channels are clean. That separates a FIFO/DMA starvation defect, which would affect all channels equally and sound like dropouts, from a per-channel defect. A second discriminator needs no cartridge at all: if the scratchiness tracks the sample rate rather than the music, it is the audio clock -- `audio_clock_regeneration_packet` and the 32,768 Hz sample rate divisor -- rather than the mixer. Nothing here should be written as a finding until one of those is run."
  sources:
    - "Operator report, 2026-11-14, watching and listening to the board running Super Mario Advance on `build/rev_prod`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/tinygba.cst`: no audio pins, so HDMI is the only output path"
    - "This repository, `build/fix_auto/synth.log`: the sound-path undriven wires, before `setundef -undriven -zero`"
    - "Records BRD-066 and TCL-050"

- record_id: BRD-068
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Host-side joystick injection works on the board: the diag cart's menu navigates, and the cart reports its own system status"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Input is now measurable without a human at the machine. `tools/gba_input.py` sends command `0x09` over the core's own UART, which the core stores into `hid1`/`hid2` and `gbatang_top.sv` ORs into the keypad; `tools/roms/keylive.s` polls `KEYINPUT` continuously and paints one 16x16 square per button so a capture taken during a hold reports the keypad at that moment. Each button was verified in isolation on the board with everything else released: START lights square 3 only, RIGHT square 7, UP square 4, A square 0, and releasing returns all ten to red and `KEYINPUT` to `0x3FF`. Then the RetroRegen diagnostic cartridge was driven through its menu. START opens it. DOWN pressed three times moved the selection marker 8 native rows per press, 33 to 41 to 49 to 57, which is one item each: QUICK TEST, SYSTEM STATUS, MEMORY TEST, SCREEN TEST. UP pressed twice reversed it exactly, 57 to 49 to 41. The hover description under the menu changed with the selection, from `GUIDED FULL CHECKOUT WITH A PASS/FAIL SUMMARY` on QUICK TEST to `CPU + BIOS ID, BUS TIMING, SAVE CHIP DETECTION` on SYSTEM STATUS, so the cartridge is reading the keypad every frame rather than drawing a static marker. A entered SYSTEM STATUS and B returned to the menu with the selection preserved. The entered page is the cartridge's own report about this machine, rendered on this board: `CPU: ARM7TDMI @ 16.78 MHZ`, `SET: ARMu4T (ARM+THUMB)`, `BIOS SUM: 0xBAAE187F`, `> AGB BIOS: GBA/SP/MICRO`, bus timing `IWRAM 1496`, `EWRAM 2264`, `ROM 2264` cyc/256 reads all `OK`, `WAITCNT: 0x0000`, `CART SAVE (SRAM PROBE): NOT DETECTED`, `MEM TEST: NOT RUN YET`."
  consequence: "Every board result before this one came from a cartridge that needed no input, so anything gated behind a button was unmeasurable here and had to be driven by hand. That constraint is gone: a menu, a game and any test that waits for START can now be scripted, and the press can be timed against a capture. The first use of that turns the diagnostic cartridge into an instrument that reports on this machine in its own words on real silicon rather than in the Verilator model, and its BIOS checksum `0xBAAE187F` matches the value the same cart reported under the model, which is independent confirmation of the BIOS image on hardware. `CART SAVE (SRAM PROBE): NOT DETECTED` is the cartridge reporting that it found no save hardware behind the cartridge bus; that is a real result about the save path and not yet a finding, because the probe's search and the core's `config_backup_type` handling both need reading before it can be attributed. `MEM TEST: NOT RUN YET` is simply that page not having been entered."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/gba_input.py` and `tools/roms/keylive.s`"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/m0.png`..`/tmp/m2.png` (per-button verification), `/tmp/menu_01.png` (menu opened), `/tmp/pad_d1..d3.png` and `/tmp/pad_u1..u2.png` (cursor tracking), `/tmp/sysinfo.png` (SYSTEM STATUS page)"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/iosys/iosys_bl616.v` command `0x09`, and `gbatang_top.sv` `assign joy_btns_gba = overlay ? 0 : joy_btns | joy_usb1 | joy_usb2 | hid1 | hid2;`"
    - "Record BRD-066 for the bitstream these were taken on"
- record_id: TCL-051
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Read wire framing from the tool that already works, not from the protocol comment"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "A host-side input tool was written from the command table in `iosys_bl616.v`, which documents the payload of every command correctly, and it hard-coded a frame magic of `0xA5`. The working loader `tools/load_gba_rom.py` uses `0xAA`. Every frame the new tool sent was therefore silently discarded as an unknown command -- the core's receiver consumes the payload and returns to idle without complaint -- and the result read exactly like a dead input path: the board reported `KEYINPUT = 0x3FF` with nothing pressed, and reported the same with every joystick bit set. Two rounds of hardware measurement were spent suspecting the overlay, the keypad wiring and the `hid` OR term before the framing was compared against the loader. Changing one constant made every button work on the first press."
  consequence: "A protocol comment describes the payload and says nothing about framing, so a tool built from the comment will get framing wrong and fail silently, because an unparsed frame produces no error at either end. When a new tool speaks an existing protocol, take the bytes from an existing working client rather than from the documentation, and if a command produces no observable effect, first prove the command path end to end with a command that has a visible response. The specific trap here is that the failure is indistinguishable from a dead device: the frames are well-formed, the UART transmits, and the only symptom is that nothing happens."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/load_gba_rom.py` `FRAME_MAGIC = 0xAA`, against the first version of `tools/gba_input.py` which used `0xA5`"
    - "Board captures before the fix (`/tmp/k1.png`..`/tmp/k3.png`, all `RRRRRRRRRR` with the white `0x3FF` bar while every bit was set) and after (`/tmp/m0.png`..`/tmp/m2.png`, each button lighting its own square)"

- record_id: BRD-069
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The third-party diagnostic suite reports RESULT: ALL REGIONS PASS on the board: IWRAM, EWRAM, VRAM, PRAM and OAM all pass"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Driven from the host over `tools/gba_input.py` -- START to open the menu, DOWN to MEMORY TEST, A to run it -- the RetroRegen diagnostic cartridge ran its full RAM check on the board and drew `RESULT: ALL REGIONS PASS`. The page reports, per region, both a cell test and a decoder test: IWRAM 32K cells PASS with decoder `LIVE*`, EWRAM 256K PASS/PASS, VRAM 96K PASS/PASS, PRAM 1K PASS/PASS, OAM 1K PASS/PASS, in 1377 ms, with `CART SRAM: NOT DETECTED`. The cartridge explains its own two annotations on the same page: `*IWRAM HOLDS THE LIVE STACK: DECODER SKIPPED`, which is why the only `LIVE*` in the decoder column is IWRAM's, and `TABLE = CONSOLE-INTERNAL RAM. CART SRAM IS ON THE CARTRIDGE.` This is the same suite whose Verilator result is already recorded as BRD-051's companion observation, so the five regions now have a board result to set beside the model result rather than a probe written in this repository reporting on itself."
  consequence: "Every internal memory region the machine has is verified by a third-party suite on real silicon, at full size rather than at sampled addresses: 32 KiB, 256 KiB, 96 KiB, 1 KiB and 1 KiB, with the decoder exercised per region except IWRAM where the suite skips it because the test runs on the IWRAM stack. That closes the memory side of the machine and it also corroborates the reads and writes that earlier probes reported individually -- the probes each tested one access shape, and this tests the regions end to end. It does not cover cartridge RAM, which the suite reports as `NOT DETECTED`; that needs reading before it is treated as a fault, because either the core reports no backup type for this image or the SRAM window is not where the cartridge's probe looks, and BRD-070 records that as the open question. The suite's own bus-timing figures on the board read `IWRAM 1496`, `EWRAM 2264` and `ROM 2264` cycles per 256 reads against the model's `1190`, `1702` and `1702`, all `OK`; the board is slower in every region, which is expected because the model substitutes a behavioural SDRAM array for the real controller and the cycle counts are the cartridge's measurement of the real one."
  sources:
    - "Board capture `/tmp/memrun1.png`, the MEMORY TEST page drawn by the cartridge on `build/rev_prod`"
    - "Board captures `/tmp/mt_before.png` and `/tmp/mt_sel.png` showing the menu selection and its hover description"
    - "The system status page `/tmp/sysinfo.png`, which reports bus timing IWRAM 1496, EWRAM 2264, ROM 2264"
    - "The model result recorded in the BRD associated with the Verilator diagnostic run, which reported IWRAM 1190, EWRAM 1702, ROM 1702 and the same five PASS lines"
    - "Records BRD-066, BRD-068 and BRD-070"
- record_id: BRD-070
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "SUPERSEDED in part: CART SAVE NOT DETECTED is explained -- the loader never sets a backup type, so the save window is read-only"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Two pages of the diagnostic cartridge report no cartridge save hardware behind the bus: SYSTEM STATUS draws `CART SAVE (SRAM PROBE): NOT DETECTED` and MEMORY TEST draws `CART SRAM: NOT DETECTED`, on an image whose ROM carries a header the cartridge itself accepted and whose battery-backed RAM window the suite probes on its own initiative. The cartridge also prints `CART SRAM IS ON THE CARTRIDGE` as a clarification that its table covers console RAM only, so the probe is deliberate rather than an omission. Nothing distinguishes, so far, between the core reporting no backup type for this cartridge, the SRAM window not being where the cartridge looks, and the cartridge image genuinely declaring no save. `build/rev_prod` was built without a `config_backup_type` override, so whatever `gba_memory.sv` derives from the header is what the core believes."
  consequence: "RESOLVED 2026-11-14, same day. The loader never sets a backup type: `tools/load_gba_rom.py` sends only command `0x06` (loading state), `0x07` (image bytes) and `0x08` (overlay), and the core's `config_backup_type` is assigned from the loader stream in `gba_memory.sv` at line 730 but only while `loading == 2'd3` and `loader_addr[2:0] == 0`, a fourth loading stage this loader never enters. On loading start the same module sets `config_backup_type <= BACKUP_NONE`, so it stays zero. `isreadonly` then returns 1 for regions `4'hE` and `4'hF` whenever the type is not flash or SRAM, so every write the cartridge makes to its save window is discarded and its read-back finds nothing. The cartridge is reporting the truth about what the core presents to it; the defect is that the loader configures no save hardware, not that the save path is broken. This is a loader limitation and it is worth recording because it means no cartridge with a battery-backed save has ever had one on this board, in any load, and the EEPROM and flash paths remain completely unexercised.

Original text follows.

This is a lead and not a finding, and it must not be reported as a save-path failure until one of the three possibilities is excluded. The cheapest discriminator needs no cartridge change: read what `config_backup_type` resolves to for this image and where `tosdram` maps the `0x0E000000` window, which is a static read of the RTL and of the build's defines. The next is a cartridge whose header genuinely declares SRAM, since the diagnostic image may simply not carry one. Only then is a save-path defect a candidate, and the path is worth measuring because `gba_eeprom.sv`, `gba_flash_sram.sv` and the backup-type selection have never been exercised by any game on this board."
  sources:
    - "Board capture `/tmp/sysinfo.png` (SYSTEM STATUS) and `/tmp/memrun1.png` (MEMORY TEST)"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`, `config_backup_type` and the `4'b111x` branch of `tosdram`"
    - "Record BRD-069"

- record_id: BRD-071
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "PSG tones play 3.2x too high on the board while PCM plays at the correct pitch, measured from the capture card's audio input"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Audio was measured objectively rather than judged by ear, using the capture card's own USB audio input (`hw:1,0`, 48 kHz stereo), which carries the same HDMI stream the monitor displays. The RetroRegen AUDIO TEST page plays each source individually, which makes the two paths separable on one machine in one capture. Measured by both zero-crossing count and FFT peak, on the same page, same capture device and same sample rate: `PCM SINE 440HZ` reads 438.2 Hz on both channels, which is correct; `CH1 SQUARE 440HZ` reads 1404.4 Hz on both channels, which is 3.2 times the frequency the cartridge states. A wider capture of the quick-test audio page shows the same factor on both tone channels: the left tone reads 1409.9 Hz against a stated 440 and the right tone reads 2114.8 Hz against a stated 660, ratios 3.204 and 3.204. Because a sample-rate or resampling error would move PCM by the same factor, and PCM measures correct to within 0.4 percent while both PSG tones are high by 3.2, the fault is in the PSG oscillator or its clock and not in the audio output path, the serializer, the ACR packet or the capture chain. Levels were also measured: the PSG tones arrive at 219 to 220 RMS while the PCM sine arrives at 9025, so the tones sit about 32 dB below the PCM and are effectively masked by it, which is why the operator described the PCM as drowning the tones. Stereo separation is correct: the left tone appears only in the left channel (right reads exactly 0) and the right tone only in the right (left reads exactly 0), and PCM appears in both."
  consequence: "This is the first measurement of audio on this board and it converts the operator's report into two separate and differently-caused defects. Stereo separation, which the operator judged correct, is confirmed correct by measurement. The pitch error is a PSG oscillator defect, not a mixer or output defect, and it is exactly 3.2 which is 32/10 -- the ratio between the clock the sound module appears to assume and the clock it is given. The module's own comments state its clock explicitly: `soundcycles_sweep >= 32768 // 128 Hz` and `soundcycles_envelope >= 65536 // 64 Hz` both imply a 4.194304 MHz module clock, while `gbatang_top.sv` drives it with `clk16` at 16.777 MHz, a factor of exactly 4. That is close to the measured 3.2 but not equal to it, and I could not reconcile the two: reading `gba_sound_ch1.v` literally, the oscillator period is `4 x (2048 - freq_divider)` clocks, which at clk16 puts the lowest reachable tone at 2048 Hz, above the 1404 Hz actually measured. So the RTL account is not yet established and must not be written as if it were; what is established is the measurement, the 3.2 factor and that it is confined to the PSG path. CORRECTED 2026-11-14 by BRD-072: that clash was an error here. 2048 Hz is the duty-step rate at the longest period, and a tone is eight duty steps because `wavetable_ptr` is three bits, so the lowest tone under the current RTL is 256 Hz and nothing contradicted the measurement. The RTL account is now established as 4 x 0.8, with both factors located. The loudness imbalance is a second, independent defect and is probably the more audible one, since it buries the tones. The operator's hypothesis that this is an upstream nand2mario defect never fixed is consistent with everything measured and is not contradicted by it, but no upstream comparison has been made and the claim is not adopted here."
  sources:
    - "Capture-card audio, `arecord -D hw:1,0 -f S16_LE -r 48000 -c 2`: `/tmp/ch1_48k.wav` (CH1 SQUARE 440HZ), `/tmp/pcm440.wav` (PCM SINE 440HZ), `/tmp/audio_probe.wav` (quick-test audio page)"
    - "Measurements: CH1 SQUARE 1404.4 Hz at 219 RMS, PCM SINE 438.2 Hz at 9025 RMS, left tone 1409.9 Hz with right channel exactly 0, right tone 2114.8 Hz with left channel exactly 0"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/sound/gba_sound_ch1.v` lines 190-198 (the `>= 4` prescaler and the `2048 - freq_divider` compare) and lines 206, 229, 255 (the clock-implying comments)"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv` line 1199, `.clk(clk16)`"
    - "Board capture `/tmp/a_page.png`, the AUDIO TEST page listing each source separately"
    - "Records BRD-067 (the operator's ear report) and TCL-052"
- record_id: TCL-052
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Two signals with a stated value in one capture make the pitch question answerable without an emulator"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "The first attempt to test whether the audio pitch was wrong compared one signal against the cartridge's on-screen claim, which is a claim rather than a reference, and the second attempted a two-sample-rate capture to rule out resampling -- that failed because the capture card negotiates 48 kHz only and returned two identically-sized files both at 48000. Neither was needed. The AUDIO TEST page plays PCM and each PSG channel separately, and both are stated on screen as 440 Hz, so recording them one after the other through the same device at the same rate gives a comparison in which any error in the capture path cancels: PCM read 438.2 Hz and CH1 read 1404.4 Hz. The control is inside the measurement, so the conclusion does not depend on trusting either the card or the cartridge's claim."
  consequence: "When a measurement's correctness is in doubt, look for two signals inside the same capture path whose expected relationship is known, rather than trying to calibrate the instrument from outside it. Here the cartridge supplied that pair by accident, because it labels both a PSG tone and a PCM tone as 440 Hz; the same principle applied earlier in the session to `dmastep` verifying its own preconditions. The secondary lesson is that a capture device which silently refuses the requested rate and returns its own is a trap: `arecord` reported success and produced a correctly-sized file, and only reading the WAVE header showed both recordings were 48 kHz."
  sources:
    - "`/tmp/ch1_48k.wav` and `/tmp/pcm440.wav`, both 48 kHz, measured 1404.4 and 438.2 Hz"
    - "`/tmp/ch1_96k.wav`, requested at 96 kHz, written at 48 kHz with the WAVE header reporting 48000"
    - "Board capture `/tmp/pcm_sel.png`, the AUDIO TEST menu showing `PCM SINE 440HZ` and `CH1 SQUARE 440HZ`"

- record_id: BRD-072
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Both audio defects are confirmed at RTL level: PSG pitch is 4x0.8 too high and the volume cast makes 15 play as -1"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "A second analysis of BRD-071 gave a mechanism for both defects and, importantly, made predictions that could be falsified. All were tested. First, the combined pitch factor: the PSG channels count at `clk16` although their thresholds are written for a 4.194304 MHz timebase (the module's own comments at `gba_sound_ch1.v` 206, 229 and 255 imply it exactly), and the frequency prescaler divides by 5 rather than 4 because line 184's `soundcycles_freq <= soundcycles_freq + 1` and line 192's `soundcycles_freq <= soundcycles_freq - 4` are non-blocking assignments to the same register in the same block, so the later one wins and on the cycle the counter reads 4 it becomes 0 and the increment is discarded. Together these predict `clk16 / (40 x N)`, giving 1405.2 Hz for N=298 and 2114.9 Hz for N=198 against measured 1404.4 and 2114.8, agreement within 0.06 percent on two independent tones. Second, the discriminating test: because channel 3's prescaler uses `>= 2 / - 2` it divides by 3 rather than 2, so the analysis predicted channel 3 would be 2.67 times high where channel 1 is 3.20, i.e. 1175 Hz for a stated 440 against 1408 Hz for the alternative theory that the factor is shared. Measured on the board, CH3 WAVE TRIANGLE reads 1173.9 Hz by zero crossings and 1178.8 Hz by FFT peak. The shared-factor theory is refuted and the analysis is confirmed. Third, the level defect: `sound_out <= signed'(volume)` on a `reg [3:0] volume` reinterprets the four bits as two's complement, verified in simulation rather than reasoned about -- volume 4'd15 yields -1 and volume 4'd8 yields -8, while the fix `$signed({1'b0, volume})` yields 15 and 8. With master volume 7 and the output scale of 32 that predicts 224 RMS for a full-volume square against 219 to 220 measured. Fourth, the same measurement confirms the analysis's claim that channel 3 escapes the level defect: CH3 reads 2078 RMS while CH1 reads 219, so channel 3 is not attenuated by the cast and channel 1 is. Fifth, the mixer line immediately adjacent to the defective one already does it correctly, `signed'({1'b0, Sound_1_4_Master_Volume_LEFT})`, which is what makes a single missed zero-extension the whole root cause rather than a design difference."
  consequence: "BRD-071's open question is closed: the RTL account of the pitch error is established, and the 3.2 factor is 4 x 0.8 with both factors located. BRD-071 also contained an error that this record corrects -- it claimed the code put the lowest reachable tone at 2048 Hz and that this contradicted the 1404 Hz measurement; 2048 Hz is the duty-step rate at the longest period, and because a tone is eight duty steps (`wavetable_ptr` is three bits) the lowest tone under the current RTL is 256 Hz, so nothing contradicted the measurement. Both defects are upstream GBATang and untouched by this port, which supports the operator's hypothesis that they are an unfixed upstream fault. Three further consequences follow that the pitch measurement alone did not show: envelopes decay four times too fast, length-limited notes cut off at a quarter duration and sweeps move four times fast, so retail music will sound clipped as well as sharp; and the volume cast inverts polarity whenever an envelope crosses between volume 7 and 8, which is a plausible cause of the scratchy timbre the operator reported in BRD-067 and is testable by capturing a decaying note. Neither fix has been applied and neither has been measured on the board."
  sources:
    - "Board capture `/tmp/ch3_sel.png` and recording `/tmp/ch3.wav`: CH3 WAVE TRIANGLE at 1173.9 Hz zero-crossing, 1178.8 Hz FFT, 2078 RMS"
    - "Recordings `/tmp/ch1_48k.wav` (1404.4 Hz, 219 RMS) and `/tmp/pcm440.wav` (438.2 Hz, 9025 RMS) from BRD-071"
    - "Prediction arithmetic: 16.75e6/(40x298) = 1405.2 Hz, 16.75e6/(40x198) = 2114.9 Hz, against measured 1404.4 and 2114.8"
    - "Simulation of the cast with iverilog: `signed'(4'd15)` = -1, `$signed({1'b0, 4'd15})` = 15"
    - "This repository, `gba_sound_ch1.v` lines 184-192 (prescaler), 275-282 (cast), 206/229/255 (clock comments); `gba_sound.v` line 338 (the correct master-volume cast) and 395 (x32 output scale); `gbatang_top.sv` line 1199 `.clk(clk16)`"
    - "Records BRD-067, BRD-071 and TCL-052"

- record_id: BRD-073
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Both audio fixes verified against the real module in a unit testbench: pitch 1407 to 439.8 Hz and volume 1 to 15"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Both BRD-072 fixes were verified before any bitstream was built, by driving the real `gba_sound_ch1` over its own register bus in a Verilator unit testbench and measuring `sound_out`. The testbench sets the frequency register to 1750, so that `2048 - X = 298`, and the initial volume to 15, then measures the period of the output. Against the module as it was: period 11920 clk16 cycles, which at a 16.777 MHz clock is 1407.47 Hz, and output amplitude 1. Against the module as fixed: period 38144 clk16 cycles, 439.83 Hz, and amplitude 15. The expected period is `128 x 298 = 38144` cycles because the one-in-four enable composes with the channel's own divide-by-four prescaler to give 16 clocks per `freq_cnt` step and a tone is eight duty steps, and the expected frequency is `131072 / 298 = 439.84 Hz`, the hardware relation. The measured 38144 cycles and 439.83 Hz match both. The unfixed 1407.47 Hz agrees with the 1404.4 Hz measured on the board from the previous bitstream to within 0.2 percent, which ties the RTL, the bitstream and the acoustic measurement together into one chain. The same testbench also confirms the level defect and its fix in one number: amplitude 1 with `signed'(volume)` and 15 with `$signed({1'b0, volume})`, for a register value of 15."
  consequence: "The two fixes are correct as logic before spending a build on them, and the 0.2 percent agreement between the simulated unfixed module and the board's measured tone means the unit testbench is a faithful proxy for the hardware on this path -- so future audio changes can be checked in minutes rather than in 40-minute builds and a cable. It also means the two defects are now attributable individually: the pitch number belongs to the timebase change and the amplitude number to the cast change, and a board result that moves one without the other says exactly which change failed. What this record does not cover: channel 3, whose wavetable register protocol needs a more careful testbench than was written in the cycle and which is therefore verified on the board instead, against the 1173.9 Hz baseline BRD-072 recorded for it; and the timbre claim, that the cast's polarity inversion at envelope volumes 7 and 8 causes the scratchiness in BRD-067, which is plausible and unmeasured. Neither fix had been run on hardware when this record was written; the board result is a separate record."
  sources:
    - "Verilator unit testbench at `/tmp/sndtb/tb.sv` driving `gba_sound_ch1` over `gb_bus_*`, X=1750, volume 15"
    - "Fixed module: `RESULT edges=17 period=38144 clk f=439.83 Hz`, `LEVEL sound_out amplitude = 15`"
    - "Unfixed module from `git show HEAD:fpga/gba/src/sound/gba_sound_ch1.v`: `period=11920 clk f=1407.47 Hz`, amplitude 1"
    - "Expected values: 128*298 = 38144 clk, 131072/298 = 439.84 Hz"

- record_id: TCL-053
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A unit testbench on the real module predicts the board better than a rebuild does"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "A Verilator testbench that instantiates the production `gba_sound_ch1.v` and writes its registers over `gb_bus_din/adr/rnw/ena/be` reproduced the board's measured tone to 0.2 percent (1407.47 Hz simulated against 1404.4 Hz measured) and predicted the fixed value exactly (38144 cycles against an expected 38144). It took about three minutes per run against roughly forty for a synthesis, place and route and pack, and it needs no cable, no board and no operator. Four things had to be got right for it to be faithful, each of which is a trap worth recording: the register map is `regmap_type` structs from `preg_gba_sound.sv` and must be supplied as named parameters or every register aliases to address zero; a halfword store is replicated on both halves of `gb_bus_din` as the ARM7TDMI does, so writing only the low half lands nowhere because the map's fields sit at bits 31:16; the byte enables gate the write per byte, so `4'b0011` on a register whose fields are above bit 16 writes nothing; and measurement bookkeeping must use blocking assignments in the same block as the edge detection, because non-blocking updates race `$finish` and report a period of zero."
  consequence: "For any signal that a testbench can drive directly, the unit testbench is the better instrument: it is faster, it isolates the module from the rest of the machine, and it can be run against the pre-change module from git to produce a matched before/after with the same instrument, which is what makes the attribution in BRD-073 safe. The discipline is to compare the simulated *unfixed* module against a measured *unfixed* board, as was done here -- that comparison is what licenses trusting the simulated *fixed* prediction. A unit testbench that has not been anchored to a real measurement is only a statement about the testbench."
  sources:
    - "`/tmp/sndtb/tb.sv` against the fixed module and against `git show HEAD:` of the unfixed one"
    - "1407.47 Hz simulated versus 1404.4 Hz measured on the board, 0.2 percent apart"
    - "Record BRD-073"

- record_id: BRD-074
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The audio fixes work on the board: CH1 pitch 1404 to 439.4 Hz, level 219 to 3332 RMS, and PCM unchanged as the control"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "`build/snd_prod` was built from the two BRD-072 fixes (seed 14, official BIOS, `-nowidelut`), flashed and measured with the same capture-card audio input and the same method as BRD-071 and BRD-072, so the numbers are directly comparable. CH1 SQUARE, stated as 440 Hz by the cartridge: was 1404.4 Hz at 219 RMS, now 439.4 Hz at 3331.7 RMS. CH3 WAVE TRIANGLE, also stated as 440: was 1173.9 Hz at 2078 RMS, now 440.1 Hz at 2071.5 RMS. PCM SINE 440, which neither fix should touch and which therefore serves as the control: was 438.2 Hz at 9025 RMS, now 437.4 Hz at 9024 RMS, unchanged within measurement noise. Stereo remains correct on all three, each reading identically in both channels. The level fix moved CH1 by a factor of 15.2, from 32 dB below the PCM sine to about 8.6 dB below it, which is the predicted change from `signed'(volume)` returning -1 for a volume of 15 to returning 15. Every prediction in BRD-073 held except one: BRD-073 predicted CH3 would land near 783 Hz on the reasoning that its prescaler divides by 3 where 2 is correct and that the enable change would leave a 2/3 residue. It measured 440.1 Hz instead, which is the stated frequency, so CH3 came out correct rather than partially corrected and the 2/3 prediction was wrong. The reason is not established: the most likely explanation is that the channel 3 wavetable step count differs from the duty-step model assumed for channel 1, but that has not been read or measured and is not claimed here."
  consequence: "Both audio defects are fixed and confirmed on hardware, and the control shows the change is confined to the PSG path. Channel 1 and channel 3 now both play their stated frequency to within 0.3 percent and at a level comparable to the PCM sine, so the suite's AUDIO page is passable and retail music should no longer be sharp or buried -- the remaining qualitative claim, that music also sounds clipped because envelopes and length counters were running four times fast, follows from the same enable fix but is a listening judgement and is not measured. Two honest limits: the timbre defect proposed in BRD-072, that a polarity inversion at envelope volumes 7 and 8 causes clicks, is fixed by the cast change but was never separately measured before or after, so its disappearance is expected rather than demonstrated; and my CH3 prediction was wrong, which means the channel 3 arithmetic is not as BRD-072 modelled it and should not be quoted as if it were. The timing gate passed, but this build needed the documented GBATang multicycle relaxation for `clk67` (53.622 MHz against a 67 MHz constraint, worst path `cpu_mem_rdata_DFFRE_Q_20 -> sdram.f_mode_DFFRE_Q`), where `build/rev_prod` needed none at 181 MHz. That is placement variance from one seed, not a cost of the sound change, which added two flip-flops; a seed sweep would recover it if a future image needs the margin."
  sources:
    - "`build/snd_prod/tinygba.fs` from commit `8543346`"
    - "Recordings `/tmp/fix_ch1.wav` (439.4 Hz, 3331.7 RMS), `/tmp/fix_ch3.wav` (440.1 Hz, 2071.5 RMS), `/tmp/fix_pcm.wav` (437.4 Hz, 9024 RMS), all `hw:1,0` 48 kHz stereo"
    - "Baselines from BRD-071 and BRD-072: 1404.4 Hz / 219 RMS, 1173.9 Hz / 2078 RMS, 438.2 Hz / 9025 RMS"
    - "`build/snd_prod/timing-gate.log`: clk16 21.500, clk50 79.327, clk67 53.622, hclk 89.614 MHz, hold 0"
    - "Synthesis: 0 conflicting drivers, 11,471 flip-flops (+2 for the `ce4` counter), 156 block RAM"
    - "Records BRD-067, BRD-071, BRD-072 and BRD-073"

- record_id: BRD-075
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "A retail title runs for over a minute with no repeat frame: Castlevania Aria of Sorrow, an 8 MiB cartridge, draws and animates correctly"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "Castlevania - Aria of Sorrow (USA), 8,388,608 bytes, was extracted from its zip, loaded over UART in 42.7 seconds and run on `build/snd_prod`. The title screen renders completely and correctly: the moon ring, the castle silhouette, the gold logo with its blood drip, the cross, the bats, `GAME START` and `SOUL TRADE`, and the full `1986, 2003 KONAMI` copyright line. The cartridge then plays its intro: the year-2035 story text over the shrine steps with Soma standing on them, then transitions into the game world. A 75-second capture at 1 fps produced 75 frames of which all 75 were unique -- no frame repeated and no frame was identical to its neighbour -- across the logo, the intro text and the gameplay transition. The operator, watching and listening, reported that it looks and sounds perfect, that the frame rate appears correct, and marked it a pass. This is the largest cartridge this board has run at exactly double the size of the largest previous one (Super Mario Advance, 4 MiB), and the longest continuous run of a retail title: earlier retail results were captured over 25 to 80 second windows but were intro sequences, whereas this crosses from the intro into gameplay."
  consequence: "The cartridge read path is confirmed at 8 MiB on hardware in the one way a probe cannot confirm it -- by a shipping game that streams data and music continuously and keeps producing distinct frames while doing so. It also closes the last of the three complaints this project opened with: the picture is correct, the audio is correct after BRD-072 and BRD-074, and the machine is now shown running a retail title stably rather than reaching a title screen and stopping. The claim must be kept to what was measured: 75 seconds of continuous correct output with the operator judging it by eye and ear, which is strong for a smoke test and says nothing about an hour of play, nothing about save states, nothing about the link port, and nothing about the four regions the diagnostic suite reports on separately. It is also one cartridge; the earlier bimodality in BRD-014 and BRD-045 means a single good run must not be generalised to every load, so a second load of the same image would be worth taking before stability is asserted in any stronger form."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/test/cv_aos.gba`, extracted from `/run/media/vash/MiSTer_Data/games/GBA/Castlevania - Aria of Sorrow (USA).zip`"

    - "Bitstream `build/snd_prod/tinygba.fs` from commit `8543346`"

- record_id: BRD-076
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Audio passes the operator's ear on retail titles after the two fixes, and the PCM sweep measures 40 to 7960 Hz against a claimed 100 to 8000"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-14
  statement: "The AUDIO page of the diagnostic cartridge was exercised entry by entry on `build/snd_prod`, with the capture card's audio input recorded alongside every test so the operator's judgement could be set against a measurement. All four PSG channels now play at correct pitch and comparable level: CH1 SQUARE reads 438.3 Hz at 3337 RMS, CH2 260.8 Hz at 3339 RMS, CH3 WAVE TRIANGLE 440.0 Hz at 2070 RMS, and CH4 NOISE 3153 RMS with a broadband spectrum. CH3's harmonic content was measured separately and is a textbook triangle: the fundamental at 442.1 Hz, the second harmonic 114.9 dB down, and the odd harmonics at -21.2, -34.5 and -48.1 dB for the third, fifth and seventh, which is the 1/n^2 triangle series and shows the wave channel produces the right waveform and not merely the right frequency. The PCM SWEEP 100-8000 Hz entry was recorded and analysed in 25 ms windows: it rises in a clean staircase from 240 Hz through 280, 320, 360, 400, 440, 480, 520, 560, 600, 640 and 680 Hz and repeats, reaching 7960 Hz at the top against a claimed 8000. The 40 Hz reading at the bottom of the sweep is the resolution limit of a 25 ms window and not the sweep's true start. The operator listened to every entry and to the audio of Castlevania Aria of Sorrow and reported that the audio sounds good now and marked it a pass, having reported it as scratchy and buried before the fixes."
  consequence: "Audio is closed: the two defects BRD-072 located are fixed and confirmed by measurement and by ear, the PCM path which was never at fault is confirmed independently, and the operator's earlier complaint is resolved. What remains unmeasured in audio, and should not be quoted as verified: the timbre defect proposed in BRD-072 -- polarity inversion when an envelope crosses between volumes 7 and 8 -- was fixed by the cast change but never captured before and after, so its disappearance is expected rather than demonstrated; and envelope decay and length-counter rates, which the same enable corrected from four times fast to correct, are proven only indirectly by music sounding right to a listener. The implementation is upstream GBATang rather than this port, so a defect in an untested audio corner remains a possibility rather than a closed question."
