The two flash1M bytes that differ sit at `0x2AAA` and `0x5555` and hold `0x55` and `0xF0` -- a flash unlock command and a reset command -- which is pre-existing behaviour of the flash model and not of the save path: `sdram_gba.v` line 206 sets `flash_cmd_en = 0` whenever `f_mode == MODE_WRITE`, so in byte-write mode every write, command bytes included, lands in the array as data. Two consecutive dumps of the live flash1M array were byte-identical to each other, so the array is stable and the read path is exact at the full 128 KB. The loader and dumper respectively reported `save restored, <size> bytes of <type>` and `gbasave: <size> bytes of <type>` for each cartridge."
  consequence: "The save engine, the framing in `iosys_bl616` and the firmware in TinyTang now agree with each other on hardware for every save type the core claims to support, which is the first time that has been true. What this does NOT establish is persistence, and the distinction matters because it is easy to mistake one for the other. Both save stores survive a `gbaload` with the power on and no save path at all: EEPROM lives in `mem_eeprom`, a block RAM that the loader never touches, and SRAM and flash live in the SDRAM cart-RAM window, which `gba_memory` writes only in loader stage 2 (`loader_addr <= {loading == 1 ? 4'h8 : 4'hE, 24'b0}` seeds stage 1 to the ROM base) and which `gbaload` never reaches because it sends stages 1, 3 and then 0. This was observed directly: an 8 KB pattern was loaded into a fresh EEPROM, the `.sav` was then deleted from the card, and the cartridge reloaded with no restore possible -- and the pattern was still in the array afterwards, so the reload had preserved it rather than the save path. A reload therefore cannot distinguish 'the save persisted' from 'the save was never lost', and only a power cycle tests the card. Stating the round trip as persistence would have been wrong by exactly the margin this paragraph describes. Two smaller results are worth keeping: the byte-exactness at 128 KB shows the flat `rv_addr[17:1]` mapping through the adapter reaches a 1 Mbit flash's second bank, and the flash-command-bytes-in-array behaviour means a dumped flash image can contain two bytes the cartridge never stored as save data, which a restore would write back -- harmless for the model, but it is why a flash1M round trip is reported as 131,070 of 131,072 rather than as a failure."
  sources:
    - "Board console at `/dev/ttyACM1`, 2026-11-15: `gbaload: save restored, 32768 bytes of SRAM`, `gbasave: 32768 bytes of SRAM`; the same pair for 65536 flash512K, 131072 flash1M and 8192 EEPROM"
    - "This repository, `tools/save/sav_pattern.py`, whose pattern is `(offset * 31 + 7) & 0xFF`, the same one `tools/save/tb_save.sv` uses"
    - "Round-trip digests: SRAM `611253a4531dea3d840789b4f11a1ad9c4329fbbf85ee1634f2ae601e6da6db0`, EEPROM and flash512K compared identical by `cmp`, flash1M `131070/131072`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v` line 206 for the flash write-mode behaviour; `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv` line 756 for the loader's address seeding"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_eeprom` instantiated by `gba_eeprom.sv` as a block RAM the loader never addresses"
    - "Records TCL-060, BRD-089"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-15 on the board, each type loaded and dumped through the real UART and the returned file compared against the sent one by SHA256 or `cmp`. The EEPROM-persists-across-reload observation was a direct A/B: the pattern was present after a reload whose restore could not run because its `.sav` had been deleted. Not measured: survival across a power cycle, which is the test that establishes persistence and is not claimed here."

- record_id: BRD-093
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Both save stores survive a bitstream reload untouched, so a reload cannot test persistence and only power loss can"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "A cartridge's save memory survives `tangload` and a following `gbaload` with no save file present at all, for both stores the core uses, measured with a controlled marker rather than inferred from the design. The EEPROM test: 8192 bytes of `0xC3` were restored into `mem_eeprom` through the save path, the `.sav` was then renamed so no restore could possibly run, `tangload` reprogrammed the bitstream, the cartridge was loaded, and the dump returned 8192 bytes of `0xC3`, byte for byte. The SRAM test: 32768 bytes of `0x5A` were restored into the SDRAM cart-RAM window, the `.sav` was renamed, the bitstream was reprogrammed, the cartridge was loaded, and the dump returned 32768 bytes of `0x5A`. Neither store was disturbed. This is consistent with the source: `gba_memory` writes the cartridge window only in loader stage 2 (`loader_addr <= {loading == 1 ? 4'h8 : 4'hE, 24'b0}`), and `gbaload` sends stages 1, 3 and 0 and never stage 2, so nothing addresses the cart-RAM window; the EEPROM is a block RAM the loader never writes, and a `tangload` reprograms the FPGA without clearing it. The consequence for testing is direct and it invalidates a test this project was about to rely on: a save that survives a reload proves nothing, because it would survive even if the save path did not exist. Only removing power tests the card, and even then the claim needs the negative control -- dump after the power cycle with the `.sav` absent and confirm the memory is blank -- or it is assuming the thing it is trying to prove."
  consequence: "This record exists because the distinction was got wrong first. The save-persistence work was begun and described as making a save 'survive a core reload', which was already true of the machine before any of it was written. What the work actually adds is survival across power loss, and a way to move a save on and off the card at all. The evidence that corrected it was accidental: an 8 KB pattern was loaded into a fresh EEPROM, the `.sav` was deleted, the cartridge was reloaded with no restore possible, and the pattern was still there -- which read as a fault until the same behaviour was reproduced deliberately with a marker and traced to the loader's stage table. Two habits follow. A persistence claim must name the event that would have destroyed the data, because 'survived X' is vacuous when X does not clear the resource. And where a test's negative control is cheap, run it first: the stashed-`.sav` dump is what turned an ambiguous pass into a measurement. Related, and still open: the power-cycle test never completed, because the BL616 console stopped answering during the first `gbasave` afterwards, so survival across power loss is NOT established by this record."
  sources:
    - "Board console at `/dev/ttyACM1`, 2026-11-15: `gbaload: save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM` then, with the `.sav` renamed and after `tangload`, `gbasave: 8192 bytes of EEPROM` returning 8192 bytes of `0xC3`"
    - "The same for SRAM: `save restored, 32768 bytes of SRAM`, `.sav` renamed, `tangload`, `gbasave` returning 32768 bytes of `0x5A`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv` line 756 for the loader's stage table and `fpga/gba/src/iosys/iosys_bl616.v` for the firmware's stage sequence 1, 3, 0"
    - "Record BRD-092, whose persistence wording this record corrects"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-15 on the board with controlled markers and a stashed save file, twice, once per store; both dumps were single-valued over their whole length, so the result is unambiguous. Not measured: whether either store survives removal of power, which is the test that would establish persistence and which the console wedged before completing."

- record_id: TCL-061
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A full frame queue hides a core reply from the reader, reproduced on a host rather than guessed at"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "The firmware's reply-frame decoder keeps a queue of sixteen frames and its feed loop, `rx_decode` in `tang_fpga_uart.c`, stops as soon as `fpga_frames_space()` is false: `while (!s_pipe && fpga_frames_space() && rx_pop(&byte)) fpga_frames_push(byte);`. Once the queue holds sixteen unprompted joypad reports (`0x03`), no further bytes leave the ring, so the reply a caller is waiting for is never decoded and `tang_fpga_wait` times out on it however long it waits. `fpga_frames_take(type)` removes only the type asked for, so it does not relieve the pressure; the pressure is relieved only by `fpga_frames_joypad()`, which the desk task calls on its own poll and which purges queued joypad copies. That makes the fault conditional on the desk task not running, which is why it is intermittent. This was reproduced on a host, with no board involved: `tools/tests/tb_frames.c` case 11 formats a byte stream of `FPGA_FRAMES_MAX` joypad frames followed by one save-data frame and feeds it through the same space-gated loop the firmware uses, then asks for the save-data frame forty times. It returns `FPGA_FRAMES_NONE` every time. Calling the new `fpga_frames_flush_queue()` first and repeating the same forty asks returns the frame with its body intact. The test therefore encodes the hazard as an assertion (`a reply behind a full queue of joypads is NOT reachable`) rather than leaving it as a suspicion, and encodes the fix beside it."
  consequence: "This started as a hypothesis offered for a wedged shell on hardware and it was nearly stated as fact before being tested, which would have been wrong in an instructive way: the first reading was that the queue simply overflows, but `push_frame` evicts the oldest entry and `tb_frames.c` case 9 already asserted that the newest frame survives, so an overflowing queue was never the mechanism. The mechanism is that the decode loop refuses to run at all when the queue is full, which is a different fault with the same symptom, and only the space-gated loop model exposes it. Two rules follow. A queue that stops its producer needs its consumer to be shown to make progress, and `take()` for one type is not that: the fix is an explicit purge, which the save dump and restore now call before every exchange. And a wedging fault in a task that also owns a UART, a mutex and a card is not diagnosable by watching it wedge -- the shell printed nothing until it finished, so three separate observations of it were consistent with several causes and could not separate them. Instrumentation was added for exactly that reason (`gbasave: dumping N bytes of TYPE`, progress every 64 chunks, and a bounded `tang_fpga_lock_within(5000)` in place of `portMAX_DELAY`, whose unbounded wait is still the one path that can hang the shell with no output at all). The firmware test suite was run after these changes and 46 of 46 tests pass; the rebuilt image is 703,120 bytes, sha256 `682876ad57459a865ca18073f8b0cb4b2b1748eab3ff6b153065f8242ab2b266`, and it is NOT yet on the board."
  sources:
    - "TinyTang `ports/bl616/tang_fpga_uart.c`: `rx_decode`'s space-gated loop, and `tang_fpga_wait`'s refusal-is-final path"
    - "TinyTang `ports/bl616/fpga_frames.c`: `push_frame` evicting the oldest, `fpga_frames_take` removing one type only, `fpga_frames_joypad` purging queued joypad copies"
    - "TinyTang `tools/tests/tb_frames.c` case 11, run by `tools/tests/test_frames.sh`, which passes 27 checks"
    - "TinyTang `tools/tests/run_all.sh`: 46 of 46 pass after the change"
    - "Board console at `/dev/ttyACM1`, 2026-11-15: `gbasave` returning no output at all, then a 45-second read producing zero bytes while the USB device stayed enumerated"
    - "Records TCL-060, BRD-093"
  verification: "Reproduced on a host with no hardware: the starvation case fails as asserted and the flushed case passes, in one run of `tools/tests/test_frames.sh`. The full firmware suite passes 46 of 46. Not established: that this mechanism is what wedged the shell on the board, because the board ran a firmware that printed nothing during a dump and the instrumented image has not been flashed; every observation of the wedge is consistent with this cause but does not single it out."

- record_id: TCL-062
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "LEAD: the queue flush added for the save dump is called outside a critical section, unlike every other task-context caller"
  status: LEAD
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`fpga_frames_flush_queue()`, added so a save dump's reply cannot be hidden behind a full queue of joypad reports (TCL-061), is called from `gba_save_read` and `gba_save_write` in `tang_fpga_uart.c` with no protection. Every other caller into `fpga_frames` from task context takes one: `tang_fpga_joypad` runs `rx_decode()` and then wraps `fpga_frames_joypad()` in `taskENTER_CRITICAL()`/`taskEXIT_CRITICAL()`, because the receive path can push into the same queue from another context. The flush should follow that pattern. The effect is bounded: `s_q_count` is a plain `unsigned`, so on RV32 the `s_q_count = 0` store is a single aligned word write and is atomic; what the missing section allows is a frame pushed by the other context between the store and its neighbour, which would be discarded. Since the only frames that can be in the queue at that moment are unprompted joypad reports -- the flush exists precisely because those are worthless here, and joypad *state* is cached separately by `fpga_frames_joypad` -- nothing a caller is waiting for can be lost."
  consequence: "Recorded rather than fixed at the time of writing, deliberately and with the reason stated: the board's flash already holds the firmware containing this code, and editing the source without reflashing would leave the tree disagreeing with the image under test, which is worse than a known minor imperfection. It should be fixed in the next firmware build, and the fix is to add a `tang_fpga_flush()` wrapper in `tang_fpga_uart.c` that does `rx_decode(); taskENTER_CRITICAL(); fpga_frames_flush_queue(); taskEXIT_CRITICAL();` -- the exact shape of `tang_fpga_joypad` two functions above it. The general point is the one this project keeps relearning: a function that mutates shared decoder state has one correct calling discipline, and a new caller must be checked against it rather than assumed to be safe because the mutation looks small. The audit that found this was a comparison of the new call sites against the existing ones in the same file, which is cheap and should be part of adding any function that touches `fpga_frames`."
  sources:
    - "TinyTang `ports/bl616/tang_fpga_uart.c`: `fpga_frames_flush_queue()` at lines 1189 and 1209, and `tang_fpga_joypad` at line 203 for the protected pattern"
    - "TinyTang `ports/bl616/fpga_frames.c`: `fpga_frames_flush_queue` and the `s_q_count` declaration"
    - "Record TCL-061"
  verification: "Found by reading the two call sites against the neighbouring protected caller; not measured, and the analysis of its severity is reasoning from the types and the cache rather than from an experiment. No failure has been observed from it."

- record_id: TCL-063
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The clk67 relaxation is a property of one netlist and not of the audio fix: the save-path netlist needs none"
  status: MEASURED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "TCL-056 measured ten placements of the `build/hyg_s1` netlist at `clk67` between 48.633 and 57.172 MHz, every one of them passing only through the documented GBATang multicycle relaxation, and attributed that cost to the two-flip-flop audio fix. The attribution holds for the netlist it measured and does not survive the next one. `build/save_s14`, built after `build/hyg_s1` and carrying both the audio fix and the save path, passes the timing gate at `clk67` 173.641 MHz with the gate reading `Meets the contract with no relaxation needed`, and its `synth.log` shows `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_save.v` in the `read_slang` file list, so the fast netlist is not one that lacks the save code. The tree therefore holds four measured netlists that divide evenly: `build/rev_prod` at 181.061 MHz and `build/save_s14` at 173.641 MHz need no allowance, while `build/snd_prod` at 53.622 MHz and `build/hyg_s1` at 51.967 MHz do. The audio fix is present in three of the four and slow in two of them, so it neither predicts nor precludes the loss. The fast save netlist is also the one that ran: its `.bin` sha256 `338f35faf7ef6c0ddedd16ea6e32fed1d76ba4f836cda063622b61733d473694` is the digest entry 77 records as deployed, and the save round trip on hardware was taken on it, so this is not a synthesised-but-unshipped configuration."
  consequence: "Do not quote TCL-056 as a rule about the audio fix, and do not carry its 'every build carrying the audio fix needs the allowance' phrasing forward; it is true of `build/hyg_s1` and of the ten placements measured there, and false of the next netlist built. What is true and operationally sufficient is that the `clk67` margin must be read from the build in hand rather than predicted from what changed, because ABC is choosing a different optimisation boundary for reasons not localised here. The practical consequence cuts both ways and is the reason this record exists: an agent should neither assume a new netlist will need the relaxation and record a passing build as marginal, nor assume it will not and read a 50 MHz result as a regression to be chased. Read `tools/timing_gate.py`'s verdict, which says which it was, and state that in any timing claim. The underlying question of why these netlists differ by 3.4x remains unidentified; this record removes a wrong answer without supplying the right one, and the shortening of the `sdram.f_mode` comparison path that TCL-056 proposed remains the untried candidate."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/save_s14/timing-gate.log`: `clk67 : constraint 67.002 MHz, achieved 173.641 MHz pass` and `Meets the contract with no relaxation needed`"
    - "This repository, `build/save_s14/synth.log`: the `read_slang` invocation, which lists `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_save.v` and `-DTINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`"
    - "This repository, `build/snd_prod/timing-gate.log`: `clk67` 53.622 MHz, passing only through the documented multicycle relaxation; and `build/hyg_s1/timing-gate.log`: 51.967 MHz, likewise"
    - "This repository, `build/rev_prod/timing-gate.log`: `clk67` 181.061 MHz, `Meets the contract with no relaxation needed`"
    - "Record TCL-056, and core-log entry 77 for the deployment of `338f35fa...`"
  verification: "Read from the timing-gate logs and the synthesis logs of four build directories in this tree, and the save image's `.bin` digest was recomputed here and matches the one entry 77 records as deployed. The placement seed is not recoverable from these build products -- it is carried only by the directory name -- so this is not a fixed-seed comparison the way TCL-056's sweep was, and it must not be quoted as one. It is enough to refute a rule and not enough to identify the cause."

- record_id: BRD-094
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The EEPROM block RAM keeps its contents across a power cycle, decaying 360 of 8192 bytes, so a blank store cannot be produced and persistence cannot be tested"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "A pattern was written into the EEPROM save window and read back byte-identical (`3faac63d...`), and then the board was power-cycled twice. Each cycle was confirmed to be a real power loss rather than a reset: before `tangload` the FPGA answered `fpga: core 84`, the board's own default core, and only after `tangload /root/cores/console138k/tinygba.bin` did it answer `core 3`, so the fabric had been reconfigured from scratch both times. After the first cycle the EEPROM read back as sha256 `65047658e4d7d4e082295f0422f379a5f199c0a7b1cf9d74253ce93f1cb53e2e`, and after the second cycle, with no `.sav` present so that no restore could run, it read back as the same `65047658...` byte for byte. That content is the pattern with 360 of 8192 bytes differing (367 of 65,536 bits), and the differing bits are spread evenly over all eight bit positions rather than clustered on one lane or one address range, which is the shape of decay rather than of a lost write. The decay happened once, between the write and the first power cycle; the content has been bit-stable across every read since, including two dumps that bracketed the second power cycle. The second dump was taken over `fpgapipe` with the core held in reset by loader stage 3, so no cartridge ran and no SD write occurred between the two readings. `mem_eeprom` declares no `INIT_` parameters at all and is built from Gowin `DPB` block RAM, so a freshly configured fabric has no defined content and nothing in the design restores this array."
  consequence: "The negative control that entry 77 left as the next agent's starting point cannot be made to succeed, and the reason is now measured rather than guessed. That control required the store to read blank after a cold boot with no `.sav` present; the store reads 95.6 per cent of its previous contents instead, and it does so however the board is power-cycled. Entry 77's own observation -- 8121 of 8192 bytes non-blank and 2775 differing from the pattern last written -- is the same phenomenon, and this record explains it: the array does not clear, it partially decays. Two conclusions follow and they must not be blurred. First, save persistence is not testable on this board as it stands, because the only event that could destroy the store does not destroy it, so nothing here can distinguish 'the save persisted' from 'the memory was never lost'. Second, and more useful: a save written to this store is not reliably preserved either. A 4.4 per cent corruption rate per power cycle means the store is neither volatile enough to be a clean test subject nor non-volatile enough to be trusted with a save, and any future claim that a save 'survives power loss' on this hardware has to account for 360 changing bytes. The work's demonstrated value is therefore reduced to what BRD-092 already proves -- moving a save on and off the card -- and that should be stated plainly rather than dressed as persistence. Whether the FPGA rail is genuinely dropped on these cycles is the one thing this record does not establish, since the operator removed power and the fabric returned to its default core, but a shorter retention would require the block RAM to be powered from something the cycle does not interrupt."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `/tmp/gbatest/backup/smw_pattern.bak`, sha256 `3faac63d133ee546e983a131136bc44c9d3c0910d1c6b143d60509ef90a386e7`, produced by `tools/save/sav_pattern.py make --size 8k` and written to the store over the FT2232 at `/dev/ttyUSB1`, 2 Mbaud, then read back byte-identical"
    - "Board console: `fpga: core 84 answering on UART1 at 2000000 baud` before `tangload` and `fpga: core 3` after, on both cycles"
    - "`gbasave /root/media/gba/t_sma2.gba` and the fetched `t_sma2.gba.sav`, sha256 `65047658e4d7d4e082295f0422f379a5f199c0a7b1cf9d74253ce93f1cb53e2e`, twice ten seconds apart after the first cycle"
    - "`fpgapipe` dump of 64 chunks from `0x700000` with `config_backup_type` set to EEPROM, sha256 `65047658...`, after the second cycle"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15. Both power cycles were verified to be real by the FPGA reporting its default core 84 before `tangload`; the no-restore condition was verified by `gbaload` printing no `restoring` line while a `.sav` was absent; and the second reading was taken without any SD write in between. The one assumption this record rests on and cannot check from the host is that the operator's power cycle removed power from the FPGA core rail."

- record_id: BRD-095
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Super Mario Advance 2 running on the board does not write the EEPROM, so the game is not what disturbs a save test"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "With the EEPROM save window filled with `0xC3` through the shipped restore path, the cartridge `Super Mario Advance 2 - Super Mario World` was loaded and allowed to run, and then the save window was dumped twice with `gbasave` about fifteen seconds apart. Both dumps were sha256 `4ab596140ada097ffb0ae8f6a701fc349be23f448f746b5543d0d9d454ee8d0a`, byte-identical to the `0xC3` fill that had been restored (`marker.bak`, the same digest), and both were taken with the cartridge live rather than held in reset. A third dump taken at the same time over a host-side path agreed. The game therefore neither cleared nor rewrote the store over the window measured, which settles the fork that entry 77 left open: that entry could not tell whether the store was stable and had never been cleared, or whether the running cartridge was writing it, and proposed exactly this two-dump comparison to separate them."
  consequence: "The 'the game rewrote it' explanation for the failed persistence control is retired. That leaves the store itself as the cause, which BRD-094 then measured directly: the array retains 95.6 per cent of its contents across a power cycle instead of clearing. The practical consequence for any future save work on this board is that the cartridge does not have to be held in reset to take a stable reading, so a dump taken mid-play is valid evidence, and the cheaper of the two experiments entry 77 proposed is the one that answers. It is worth saying what this record does not claim: two dumps fifteen seconds apart cannot exclude a game that writes its save only on a save-screen event, and Super Mario Advance 2 does not save during ordinary play, so a title that autosaves could still disturb the window. The measurement answers the question that was asked -- whether an unattended running cartridge keeps the store stable -- and not a broader one."
  sources:
    - "Board console: `gbaload: save hardware = EEPROM` and `gbaload: save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM` before the run, then two `gbasave: 8192 bytes of EEPROM -> ...` runs 15 seconds apart at 07:31:16 and 07:31:31"
    - "This repository, `/tmp/gbatest/backup/marker.bak`, sha256 `4ab596140ada097ffb0ae8f6a701fc349be23f448f746b5543d0d9d454ee8d0a`, and the two dumps `dumpA.bin` and `dumpB.bin` with the same digest"
    - "Record BRD-092 for the restore path, and core-log entry 77 for the question this answers"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, with the cartridge running and not held in reset. Both dumps agree with each other and with the fill that was restored, and a third independent dump over the host path agreed as well. The fifteen-second window is the one entry 77 specified."

- record_id: TCL-064
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The console dies on large SD transfers, and the save window can be read over fpgapipe instead"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Across one session the BL616 console stopped answering three times, and every time the command in flight was moving a file: `gbasave` twice and `tangput` once. Commands that stream a cartridge to the core never failed once -- `tangload` ran eight times and `gbaload` five, at 4 MiB and 16 MiB, all completing in about 7 and 24 seconds. The failures were not USB disconnections. The kernel logged no disconnect after 07:35 while the CDC node stayed enumerated, and a single carriage return then timed out on write, so the firmware had stopped servicing the console with the device still attached. One of the three, `tangput`, left the shell blocked inside its byte loops waiting for a length it never received, and feeding it bytes only produced further write timeouts. Recovery needs power removal: `cdc_acm/unbind` and the device's `authorized` node are both non-writable to an unprivileged user and `sudo` is unavailable under the `no new privileges` flag. The way past it, found once the console could not be used at all, is `fpgapipe`: it takes the console as a raw byte pipe and moves those bytes to and from UART1 through `uart_write` and `rx_pop` directly, skipping both the SD and the sixteen-frame decoder that TCL-061 identified as the place a reply gets hidden. Over that pipe, 64 save-read frames of 128 bytes each were requested and answered without fault, which is a full 8 KiB EEPROM dump taken with no SD write and no `gbasave` involvement."
  consequence: "Two rules follow. First, the console is the fragile part of this board and it is fragile in proportion to how much data it moves through the SD path, so a save dump is the least reliable command here and repeating one after a failure is not a retry but a way to spend the board's only recovery method. Second, and more useful, the save window does not need the console's file commands at all: `fpgapipe` plus the two documented frame types -- `0x0e` read and `0x0f` write, three address bytes plus 128 data bytes from a base of `0x700000` -- gives a host a complete read and write path that leaves the SD and the frame decoder out of the circuit. That is what made this cycle's measurements possible after the console had died twice. The remaining hazard is not in `gbasave`, whose lock handling is bounded on every path, but in `cmd_fpga`, which takes `tang_fpga_lock()` -- `portMAX_DELAY` -- and so blocks the shell forever if the transmit lock is held. That is why the console goes silent even for an innocent `fpga` once a transfer has failed, and it is the one path TCL-061 already flagged as able to hang the shell with no output at all."
  sources:
    - "Board console: `gbasave` returning `gbasave: the FPGA link is held by another task; try again` and then the console accepting no bytes; `tangput` answering `tangput did not answer` and wedging afterwards"
    - "`journalctl -k`: no `USB disconnect` for `1-13` after 07:35 while `cdc_acm 1-13:1.0: ttyACM0` stayed registered; the device re-enumerated as new device numbers 015 and 016 only when the firmware reset itself"
    - "TinyTang `ports/bl616/tang_fpga_uart.c`: `cmd_fpgapipe`, `cmd_gbasave`, `cmd_gbaload`, `cmd_fpga`, `tang_fpga_lock` and `tang_fpga_lock_within`"
    - "TinyTang `ports/bl616/tdsh_tang_flash.c`: `cmd_tangput`, whose receive loop blocks on `console_getc()` until its announced length arrives"
    - "Record TCL-061 for the frame-queue starvation the pipe route bypasses"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15. The three console deaths and the eight `tangload` runs are counted from the session's own transcripts, and the kernel log establishes that the device stayed attached. The `fpgapipe` route was exercised end to end for a 8192-byte read. The claim about which lock `cmd_fpga` takes is read from source, not timed on hardware."

- record_id: TCL-065
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Sixteen files in the build tree are Gowin IP-generator output under all-rights-reserved headers, and GBATang's GPL does not cover them"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Sixteen files under `fpga/gba/src/` carry `Copyright (C)20xx Gowin Semiconductor Corporation. All rights reserved.` in their header and state no grant, no SPDX tag and no licence text. Nine of them are in the shipped build: the six memory wrappers `memory/mem_eeprom.v`, `memory/mem_iwram.v`, `gpu/vram_lo.v`, `gpu/vram_hi.v`, `iosys/gowin_dpb_menu.v` and `fb.v`, and the PLL wrappers `pll/pll_12.v`, `pll/pll_27.v` and `pll/pll_33.v`. The remaining seven are not built: six are duplicates in an unused `plla/` directory and `pll/pll_74.v` is superseded by the hand-written `pll/gowin_pll_hdmi.v`. The generated cells are real and measured, not vestigial: the routed netlist of `build/save_s14` contains 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP` and 43 `DPX9B` instances, of which 53 `DPB` are declared in the four memory wrappers (`mem_eeprom` 4, `vram_lo` 32, `vram_hi` 16, `gowin_dpb_menu` 1), all 16 `SP` in `mem_iwram`, and 38 of the `DPX9B` in `fb.v`. The files build in the open flow because they are flat Verilog that instantiates blackboxed primitives -- `DPB`, `SP`, `DPX9B` and `PLL` are passed to `read_slang` as `--blackboxed-module` with behaviour from oss-cad-suite's `cells_sim_nospecify.v` and `cells_xtra_gw5a.v`, and nextpnr places them against apicula's `GW5AST-138C` database. No Gowin EDA is involved in the build and none is needed; the build never reads the `.ipc` or `.mod` descriptors beside them. They are therefore not vendor HDL that would fail in yosys, which is the intuition that makes their presence look impossible."
  consequence: "These files cannot be relicensed by this project, and the licence it chooses makes no difference. GBATang is GPL-3.0 and these arrived inside GBATang's tree, but a licensor can only license what it owns, so GBATang's grant does not extend to Gowin's generated output and neither this project's MIT, GPL or Apache-2.0 terms can. The correct description is that they are carried as build inputs rather than as licensed source, which is what THIRD_PARTY.md now says. Whether redistribution is permitted at all depends on Gowin's EULA, which is NOT established here and must not be asserted: no Gowin EDA is installed on this machine, no EULA text is in the tree, and reading terms that are not available would be exactly the kind of claim this store exists to prevent. The precedent inside this project's own family is that the ambiguity has been reduced rather than resolved -- TinyNES carries no Gowin-generated files at all, while TinySNES replaced its PLL wrappers with hand-written GPL-3.0 ones and still ships Gowin-headed RAM wrappers. The way out for the memories is that they contain no logic: `mem_eeprom` is four `DPB` instantiation lines and a port list, so this project's own RTL can express the same memory and let yosys infer the primitive. The flow demonstrably does infer, since 43 `DPX9B` appear in the netlist against 38 declared in `fb.v`, so the five extra came from behavioural arrays such as `linebuffer.v`'s."
  sources:
    - "This repository: `grep -rl 'Copyright (C)20.*Gowin Semiconductor' fpga` returns the sixteen files; per-file `defparam` and `DPB`/`SP`/`DPX9B` instantiation counts read directly from them"
    - "This repository, `build/save_s14/tinygba.json`: `DPB` 66, `SP` 16, `DPX9B` 43, `PLL` 3, counted by cell type over all 234 modules"
    - "This repository, `build/save_s14/synth.log`: the `read_slang` invocation listing each of the nine built files, and the `--blackboxed-module` list carrying `DPB`, `SP`, `DPX9B` and `PLL`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/*.ipc`: `type=ram_dpb` / `ram_sp` and the `DEPTH_*`/`WIDTH_*` geometry per wrapper"
    - "oss-cad-suite `share/yosys/gowin/brams_map_gw5a.v` and `cells_sim.v`: the `DPB` definition and the techmap that supplies `INIT_RAM_00`..`INIT_RAM_3F`"
  verification: "Counted from the tree and from one routed netlist on 2026-11-15. The per-file instantiation counts and the `ipc` geometries are exact. Attribution of the remaining 13 `DPB` to nand2mario's `dpram_block`/`dpram32_block` wrappers or to inference is by elimination -- those seven files are the only other `DPB` sources in the tree -- and is not separately measured. The EULA question is explicitly unresolved, and no redistribution permission is claimed anywhere in this record."

- record_id: TCL-066
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "TinyGBA is GPL-3.0-only, and the Gowin IP-generator files are build inputs rather than licensed source"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "The repository now carries a root `LICENSE` holding the unmodified GPL-3.0 text, byte-identical to `/usr/share/common-licenses/GPL-3` and to TinySNES's copy at sha256 `3972dc9744f6499f0f9b2dbf76696f2ae7ad8af9b23dde66d6af86c9dfb36986`, 674 lines. `THIRD_PARTY.md` gained a `## This repository's own licence` section stating GPL-3.0-only and why it is forced rather than chosen: `fpga/gba/src/` is GBATang, which is GPL-3.0, vendored in-tree and compiled into the same bitstream, so the result is a single work and GPL-3.0 is the only licence it can carry; `GPL-3.0-only` rather than `-or-later` matches the absence of an \"or later\" clause in the GBATang notices. That section replaces the earlier paragraph that recorded the question as undecided, which had been left standing underneath a carve-out added in `9d958f1` saying some of that tree is not GPL -- a contradiction inside one file, now removed. Every source file this project wrote carries `SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-only`, 51 files in total, with one deliberate exception: `tools/fs_to_bin.py` keeps `SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT` because it was carried in from TinyTang, which GPL-3.0 permits. Seven files gained a header in this cycle -- `tools/check_cartread.py`, `tools/clock_edge_check.py`, `tools/load_gba_rom.py`, `tools/score_phases.py`, `tools/save/tb_save.sv`, `tools/save/tb_iosys.sv` and `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_save.v`, the last being the only file this project added under `fpga/gba/src/`. The carve-out paragraph was also rewritten to read as an ordinary third-party notice: sixteen files are Gowin IP-generator output, nine are in the shipped build, they are used as build inputs and are not covered by this repository's licence, and the sentence asserting that the project cannot relicense them is gone in favour of what is actually true. The one open question is stated as open rather than answered: whether Gowin's own terms permit redistributing IP-generator output, which nobody working here has read because no Gowin EDA is installed and no EULA text is in the tree."
  consequence: "Two things are settled and one is deliberately not. Settled: the repository has a licence, so a downstream user is no longer looking at a tree whose terms are unstated, and the licence choice is recorded with its reason so a future agent does not relitigate it. Settled also: the header-editing is provably inert, because a pre-change and post-change synthesis of the same configuration produced netlists that are identical once the 406 `gba_save.v` source-line annotations are normalised -- every one shifted by exactly one line, which is all an inserted comment line can do -- with both netlists at 234 modules and 52,453 cells and identical cell-type counts. Not settled, and not to be asserted: whether redistribution of Gowin's generated output is permitted. That is a licence question whose answer lives in a document nobody here has, and the correct handling is the one now in THIRD_PARTY.md, which names the gap and tells anyone distributing commercially to satisfy themselves. The general lesson worth keeping is the one this cycle cost: the first version of the carve-out claimed the files could not be relicensed, which is true and narrow, and was reported as though it settled whether the repository could ship under GPL, which is a different question with the opposite answer. A correct statement used to answer the wrong question is still a wrong answer."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `LICENSE`: sha256 `3972dc9744f6499f0f9b2dbf76696f2ae7ad8af9b23dde66d6af86c9dfb36986`, byte-identical to `/usr/share/common-licenses/GPL-3` and to `TinySNES/LICENSE`"
    - "This repository, `THIRD_PARTY.md`: the `## This repository's own licence` section and the rewritten Gowin paragraph under the GBATang entry"
    - "`grep -rho 'SPDX-License-Identifier: .*'` over the tree: 51 `GPL-3.0-only` and 1 `MIT` (`tools/fs_to_bin.py`)"
    - "This repository, `build/lic/tinygba.json` and `build/lic_pre/tinygba.json`: post-change and pre-change synthesis at `TINYGBA_STAGE=synth` with `-DTINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, compared after normalising `gba_save.v` source-line references"
    - "`tools/save/run_save_tb.sh` and `tools/save/run_iosys_tb.sh`, both run after the header edits and both passing with their must-fail regressions still rejected"
    - "Record TCL-065 for the file inventory, and core-log entry 80 for the carve-out this corrects"
  verification: "Verified on 2026-11-15. The licence text was compared byte-for-byte against two independent copies before being used. SPDX coverage was checked by enumerating `git ls-files` over `tools/` and `fpga/gba/diag/` plus every file added under `fpga/gba/src/`, and the claim of inertness rests on an actual pre/post synthesis pair rather than on the assertion that comments cannot change logic. The netlist comparison was done on the `modules` object with only the `creator` field excluded, so it covers cells, connections and attributes."

- record_id: TCL-067
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The OSD text page is this project's own RTL now, and a DPB parameter list over ~70 entries must be written with defparam, not #()"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`fpga/gba/src/iosys/gowin_dpb_menu.v` -- 2048 x 8, dual-port, one `DPB`, generated by Gowin EDA 1.9.9 -- was replaced by `fpga/gba/src/iosys/menu_ram.v`, written here, and `textdisp.v` and `tools/build_tinygba.sh` were pointed at it. The memory is not just the char buffer: three things share the block at addresses the renderer relies on, a 32x28 character buffer at 0x000-0x37F, a 14x9 logo bitmap at 0x380-0x3FF and the 8x8 font from 0x400, and the font and logo are never written at run time -- they are the block's initial contents. The original carried them in 28 non-zero `INIT_RAM_*` words, so they were extracted mechanically by `tools/gen_mem_wrappers.py` rather than retyped, and the replacement synthesises with the same 28 non-zero words at `BIT_WIDTH_0=8`. Equivalence was established two ways: a side-by-side module containing both wrappers synthesises to 2 `DPB` and nothing else, and the full build's primitive census is unchanged at 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM. One toolchain fact cost two builds: a parameter list of this size must be applied with `defparam`, not with `DPB #(...)`. The `#()` form works on a three-parameter toy module and fails on this instance with `ERROR: missing '(' in parameter list` followed by twenty more, because slang then parses `DPB` as a user-defined primitive. `fpga/gba/src/common/dpram_block.v` uses `#()` with one parameter and is unaffected; Gowin's own generated wrappers use `defparam` throughout, which is why they compile."
  consequence: "This is the pattern for replacing the other five Gowin memories, and it also marks the limit of it. What made this one tractable is that it has no logic around the memories: one `DPB`, no bank decoder, no registered output select. The other five are a different problem -- `mem_eeprom` alone has 27 `MUX2` and 4 `DFFRE` around its four blocks, and `vram_hi` has 448 and 6 -- so a generator that emits a port list and a parameter block is the whole job here and a fraction of it there. The initial-contents extraction is reusable, though: any future replacement of a Gowin memory must carry its `INIT_RAM_*` words across mechanically, because a wrapper emitted without them builds cleanly and then behaves differently, and for the OSD that difference is a blank text page rather than an error. The `defparam` finding applies to every remaining wrapper, all of which have 9 parameters or more."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/iosys/menu_ram.v` and `fpga/gba/src/iosys/gowin_dpb_menu.v`: the replacement and what it replaced"
    - "This repository, `tools/gen_mem_wrappers.py --summary` on each wrapper: parameter counts, non-zero `INIT_RAM` counts and `BIT_WIDTH`/`BLK_SEL` settings"
    - "This repository, `build/m1/tinygba.json` and `build/save_s14/tinygba.json`: primitive census before and after, both 66 `DPB` / 16 `SP` / 43 `DPX9B`"
    - "`tools/memory_census.py` against both netlists: 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM in each"
    - "Record TCL-065 for the file inventory this changes"
  verification: "Verified on 2026-11-15 by synthesis. The INIT survival was checked inside the emitted netlist, not assumed from the source. The claim that the two wrappers synthesise identically rests on the isolated two-instance module, which contains nothing but the two memories and their ports. Not verified on hardware: the text page has not been drawn since the change, so Deployment and User Test are NOT RUN."

- record_id: TCL-068
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "LUT count is not an equivalence test for a memory wrapper, and no DPB model exists to simulate one against"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Replacing the second Gowin memory wrapper, `mem_eeprom`, was attempted four times and abandoned, and the reason is a property of the method rather than of that file. The wrapper is four `DPB` blocks with 27 `MUX2` and 4 `DFFRE` around them: the blocks are banks selected by the top address bits, and because block RAM presents data the cycle after its address arrives, the bank select feeding the output multiplexer is registered one cycle ahead. Three restatements were built and measured against the tree without it. Tying `BLKSELA` to the instance index and registering the select statically gave 52,379 cells against 52,540; adding the original's `~wrea` gating gave 52,262; driving `BLKSELA` dynamically from `ada[15:14]` while pinning each instance with `BLK_SEL_n`, which is the structure Gowin actually emits, gave 52,350. `DFFRE` fell by 2 and `DFF` rose by 2 in the last, so the flip-flop count did not even agree in kind. None of these is evidence either way: the same tree varies by several hundred LUTs between builds with no source change at all, because ABC picks a different optimisation boundary -- TCL-056 recorded exactly this for the audio fix, where the identical netlist-class moved between 48 and 181 MHz for `clk67`. Against a noise floor of that size, a discrepancy of 190 cells cannot distinguish a real structural error from arithmetic noise, and a match would have been luck rather than proof. The intended check, simulation, is not available either: `tools/sim/gowin_cells_sim.v` declares `DPB`, `SP` and `DPX9B` as port lists with empty bodies, and oss-cad-suite's own `cells_sim.v` carries no `DPB` module at all, so there is no behavioural model anywhere in this toolchain to simulate a memory against. The attempt was reverted rather than shipped."
  consequence: "The remaining five memory wrappers -- `mem_eeprom`, `mem_iwram`, `vram_hi`, `vram_lo` and `fb.v`, which declare 52 `DPB` between them (4, 16 and 32), all 16 `SP` and 38 `DPX9B` -- should not be converted until they can be checked by simulation, because the only alternatives are guessing at gate structure against a noise floor or shipping unverified changes into the memory path of a machine that currently runs all twelve cartridge titles. The work needed is a behavioural model for `DPB`, `SP` and `DPX9B` written into `tools/sim/gowin_cells_sim.v`, covering asynchronous and synchronous reset, the `BIT_WIDTH`/`READ_MODE`/`WRITE_MODE` settings the wrappers use, the `INIT_RAM_*` initial contents, and the `BLKSEL` input, which Gowin drives dynamically as a bank select while yosys's own techmap always ties it to zero. That last point is the one that makes the model non-trivial and the one no existing open model addresses: the vendor's banking and the synthesis inference use the same primitive port differently. With a model in place, every conversion becomes a cycle-by-cycle output comparison of old against new under random stimulus, which catches the failures a census cannot see -- an off-by-one read latency, a wrong enable, a transposed port. The lesson generalises past this project: a cell-count census proves a memory still occupies the same resources, and nothing at all about whether it computes the same function."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `build/m1`, `build/m2`, `build/m3` and `build/m4` `tinygba.json`: four syntheses of the same tree with successive `eeprom_ram` restatements, and their cell-type censuses"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_eeprom.v`: 4 `DPB`, 27 `MUX2`, 4 `DFFRE`, the dynamic `BLKSELA({gw_gnd,ada[15],ada[14]})` and the per-instance `BLK_SEL_0 = 3'b000`..`3'b011`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gpu/vram_lo.v`: `lut_inst_0` as a `LUT4` with `INIT=16'h0001` on `ada[10]`..`ada[13]`, decoding an all-zero bank, feeding `BLKSELA` bit 0 of every instance"
    - "`tools/sim/gowin_cells_sim.v` and oss-cad-suite `share/yosys/gowin/cells_sim.v`: the empty `DPB` declarations"
    - "oss-cad-suite `share/yosys/gowin/brams_map_gw5a.v`: every inferred block ties `.BLKSELA(3'b000)`"
    - "Record TCL-056 for the ABC optimisation-boundary effect this depends on"
  verification: "Measured on 2026-11-15 by four synthesis runs. The ABC-noise claim is read from this project's own TCL-056 sweep rather than re-measured here, and the 'no behavioural model' claim was checked in both cell libraries by reading the module bodies. The structural reading of `BLKSEL` as a bank select is inferred from the vendor's wiring and the decoder LUTs; it was not confirmed against Gowin documentation, which is not available on this machine, and it is precisely the point a simulation model would have to settle."

- record_id: TCL-069
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The EEPROM memory is this project's own RTL, and a memory wrapper can be checked by simulation once the primitive is modelled"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_eeprom.v` -- four `DPB` blocks with 27 `MUX2` and 4 `DFFRE` around them, generated by Gowin EDA 1.9.10.02 -- was replaced by `fpga/gba/src/memory/eeprom_ram.v`, written here, and the `.ipc` and `.mod` descriptors beside it were removed with it, leaving fourteen Gowin-headed files in the tree of which seven are in the build. The restatement follows the vendor's structure rather than inventing one: four blocks in parallel, each told its own identity by `BLK_SEL_n = i` and all of them told which bank the current request belongs to by `BLKSEL` driven from `{ada[15],ada[14]}` on port A and `{adb[12],adb[11]}` on port B; port A is 1 bit wide over 65536 addresses and port B 8 bits over 8192, the same storage from two sides, and a block's address field is shifted to make a word index at each width. The output multiplexer's select is registered one cycle ahead of the data, because a block presents its word the cycle after its address arrives, and that register is gated by the read enables (`cea & ~wrea`) so a write cycle leaves the select where it was and the output holds instead of redirecting at the bytes being written. Substitution was checked by simulation rather than by counting cells: `tools/sim/tb_eeprom_equiv.sv` instantiates both wrappers side by side, drives 12000 random cycles on both ports plus a directed pass writing and reading every bank, and compares both outputs on every clock edge -- 24018 samples, 48036 comparisons, no mismatch. The testbench is only evidence because two deliberately broken variants are rejected: gating the bank register on `cea` alone instead of `cea & ~wrea` gives 1603 mismatches, and selecting the mux from the live address instead of the registered one gives 1872. `tools/sim/run_mem_equiv_tb.sh` runs all three and exits non-zero unless the shipped pair passes and both defects fail. The build's primitive census is unchanged at 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM, and the save path's own testbenches still pass with their must-fail regressions still rejected."
  consequence: "This is the method for the remaining four wrappers, and it is the method TCL-068 said was missing rather than a better version of the one that failed. The two things it needs are both now in the tree: `tools/sim/gowin_bram_sim.v` models `DPB`, `SP`, `SPX9` and `DPX9B` with the width/depth relation, byte strobes taken from the address field, one-cycle read latency, `OCE` gating, `RESET_MODE`, the 64 `INIT_RAM_*` words and `BLKSEL` compared against the per-instance `BLK_SEL`. What a pass means must not be overstated and the testbench says so in its header: it establishes that the two wrappers compute the same function under one shared set of semantics, not that those semantics match the device. Two facts about the modelling are worth carrying forward. The `BLKSEL`/`BLK_SEL` comparison is the one that makes the vendor's banking expressible, and it is a reading of the generated wrappers rather than of any Gowin document available here -- `mem_iwram` drives `BLKSEL` from `{ad[12],ad[11],ad[10]}` while pinning its instances to `BLK_SEL` 0 through 15, so two instances necessarily share each `BLK_SEL` value and the model's enable-by-match cannot be exactly right for that wrapper; it does not matter for the EEPROM, whose four blocks use distinct values, but the `mem_iwram` conversion will need this settled first. And the model treats a 16-bit port's address bits [1:0] as byte strobes, which is what `vram_lo` and `vram_hi` do by driving `{ada[9:0],2'b00,byte_ena[1:0]}`, but the `fb` wrapper is 18 bits wide and needs its own check."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/eeprom_ram.v` and the deleted `mem_eeprom.v` (still in git history at the previous commit)"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/gowin_bram_sim.v`: the `DPB`/`SP`/`SPX9`/`DPX9B` models and the `MUX2`/`DFFRE` glue cells"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/tb_eeprom_equiv.sv` and `tools/sim/run_mem_equiv_tb.sh`: the equivalence test and its two required failures"
    - "This repository, `build/m5/tinygba.json`: the full build with the replacement, census identical to `build/save_s14/tinygba.json`"
    - "`tools/memory_census.py` on both netlists: 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM in each"
    - "Records TCL-065, TCL-067 and TCL-068 for the licensing context, the first conversion and the method this replaces"
  verification: "Verified on 2026-11-15 by simulation and synthesis. The simulation result stands only because both injected defects are rejected in the same run, which the runner enforces. Not verified on hardware: nothing was built for the board and no cartridges were run, so the EEPROM round trip recorded in BRD-092 has not been repeated against this wrapper."

- record_id: BRD-096
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "With both replaced memory wrappers in the bitstream a retail cartridge runs and the EEPROM save round-trips byte for byte"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`build/hw2` was built from the tree carrying `menu_ram` (the OSD text page, this project's own RTL) and `eeprom_ram` (the EEPROM window, likewise), with `-DTINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, all gates passing: `clk67` 195.886 MHz against 67.002 with no relaxation, `clk16` 21.543, `clk50` 83.153, `hclk` 99.741, 0 hold violations, and a primitive census identical to the last deployed image at 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP` and 43 `DPX9B`. Its `.bin` sha256 `5e592cfb8a71949fa8e6d417830a884b465d00a2308d52fdda8c30d5c29d48c8` was uploaded to the card as `/root/cores/console138k/tinygba_hw2.bin` and read back with `tangget` to the same digest before use, leaving the previous image untouched. On that image Super Mario Advance 2 -- Super Mario World runs with a correct picture: the intro and world map render in full colour with no corruption, at 1920x1080 through the capture card in the project's usual pillarboxed frame. The EEPROM path was then exercised end to end on hardware: the canonical `sav_pattern.py` vector was placed as `t_sma2.gba.sav`, `gbaload` reported `restoring 8192 bytes of EEPROM` and `save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM`, which drives the write path through `eeprom_ram`, and `gbasave` then reported `8192 bytes of EEPROM` in 2.9 s and the file fetched back had sha256 `3faac63d133ee546e983a131136bc44c9d3c0910d1c6b143d60509ef90a386e7`, byte-identical to the pattern that went in. Both directions of the save window therefore work through the replacement wrapper, not merely under simulation."
  consequence: "The EEPROM wrapper is verified on hardware, and the machine is not disturbed by either replacement: a retail cartridge boots, draws and plays with both in the bitstream. That closes, for `eeprom_ram`, the gap TCL-069 recorded when it said the substitution had been proved only under a simulation model -- BRD-092's round trip now has a match under the new RTL. Two limits must be attached. First, this image was built with the overlay suppressed, so `menu_ram` is present but untested: the OSD text page is deliberately disabled in that configuration (a production build keeps it, and a bench build takes it out so the empty menu's black does not hide the machine), so nothing here says the replaced text page draws. Second, the round trip proves the save window reads and writes what is expected through the engine; it does not re-open the persistence question, which BRD-094 settled as unmeasurable on this board because the store decays rather than clears."
  sources:
    - "`build/hw2/tinygba.bin`, sha256 `5e592cfb8a71949fa8e6d417830a884b465d00a2308d52fdda8c30d5c29d48c8`; card copy `/root/cores/console138k/tinygba_hw2.bin` read back with `tangget` to the same digest"
    - "`build/hw2/timing-gate.log`: `clk67` 195.886 MHz, `0 violation(s)`, `Meets the contract with no relaxation needed`"
    - "Board console: `tangload` then `fpga: core 3 answering on UART1 at 2000000 baud`, and `gbaload: save hardware = EEPROM`, `gbaload: save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM`"
    - "Capture `/tmp/frame1.png` at 1920x1080: the world map of Super Mario Advance 2, full colour, no corruption"
    - "`gbasave` reporting `8192 bytes of EEPROM -> ...` in 2.9 s, and the fetched file at sha256 `3faac63d133ee546e983a131136bc44c9d3c0910d1c6b143d60509ef90a386e7`, equal to `/tmp/gbatest/backup/smw_pattern.bak`"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, with the image's digest confirmed on the card before it was loaded. The save comparison is a byte-for-byte match against a vector whose every byte is a function of its offset, so a dropped byte, a swapped lane or a 64 KB alias would have shown as a named offset. The OSD was deliberately not claimed: the configuration under test disables it."

- record_id: TCL-070
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The parity and memory-census gates carry stale or partial baselines, so a memory change in iosys is invisible to both"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Two gates guard memory placement in this build and each has a gap that let a real change through unnoticed. The memory-placement gate compares every block-RAM cell's path and primitive against `tools/memory_baseline.json`, but that baseline holds 123 paths drawn only from four top-level prefixes -- `gpu` 60, `mem` 22, `sound` 3 and `video` 38 -- and none from `iosys`. The `menu_ram` substitution replaced the OSD text page's single `DPB` in `iosys`, and the gate reported no difference at all, because no path under that prefix was ever recorded; the change was only caught later by comparing routed netlists by hand. The register-parity gate compares flip-flop and block-RAM totals against a stored figure, and its stored figure is stale: it reported `baseline 'TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF': 11471 flops, 156 BRAM` against a netlist of 12417 flops and 148 block RAM, a `+946 flops (+8.2%)` change that passed only because the budget is 10 per cent. Measured against the image actually deployed before this cycle, `build/save_s14`, the same netlist differs by 2 flip-flops and no block RAM at all -- 2934 `DFF` to 2936 and 9302 `DFFRE` to 9300 -- so the 946-flop figure is an artefact of the baseline, not a property of the change. The memory-placement gate did fire correctly for the second substitution, reporting the four EEPROM blocks moving from `dpb_inst_0..3` to `bank[0..3].u_bank` with the count unchanged at four, which is the rename a generate loop produces and which was recorded deliberately with `tools/memory_census.py --update-baseline`."
  consequence: "CORRECTED BY TCL-074, which found the real cause and showed part of this reading to be wrong: the census tool does cover `iosys` and finds `iosys.disp.menu_mem.u_menu` in a production netlist, and the reason no `iosys` path appeared here is that the baseline being compared against had no `default` (production) entry at all. The gap was the missing baseline, not the coverage, and the `menu_ram` change was invisible because no production baseline existed rather than because `iosys` is unrecorded. The paragraph below is kept as written so the correction is legible. Neither gate should be trusted as coverage of a memory change until its baseline is complete, and a pass from either is currently weak evidence. The memory-placement gate is the more serious of the two because its gap is structural rather than stale: an entire top-level prefix is unrecorded, so any memory change under `iosys` -- which is where the OSD text page, the menu and the desktop layer live -- cannot be detected by it however large the change. Recording the current netlist's full path set would close that, at the cost of a larger baseline, and is the recommended next step before the remaining wrappers are converted. The parity gate's stale numbers are cheaper to fix and less important to fix immediately, since the budget is wide enough that the gate passes either way; but a baseline that is 946 flops out of date cannot detect a real 900-flop regression. The general point is the one entry 83 recorded for documentation and this extends to gates: a count stored in a file and a netlist measured today are two facts that drift, and only recounting from the tree catches it. What caught the `menu_ram` change here was not a gate but a side-by-side synthesis of the old and new wrappers and a manual comparison of routed netlists by cell type."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/memory_baseline.json`: 123 paths under `TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, none with an `iosys` prefix"
    - "This repository, `build/hw2/parity.log`: `baseline 'TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF': 11471 flops, 156 BRAM`, `change +946 flops (+8.2%)`"
    - "This repository, `build/save_s14/tinygba.json` and `build/hw2/tinygba.json`: cell-type census of the last deployed image and this cycle's, differing by `DFF` 2934 to 2936 and `DFFRE` 9302 to 9300 with `DPB` 66, `SP` 16 and `DPX9B` 43 unchanged in both"
    - "This repository, `build/hw2/memory-census.log`: the gate's own DIFFERS report naming `mem.eeprom.m_eeprom.bank[0..3].u_bank` and the four `dpb_inst_N` paths"
  verification: "Read from the gate outputs and the two routed netlists on 2026-11-15. The 946-flop discrepancy was diagnosed by recounting both netlists by cell type rather than by inspecting the baseline file's history, so the claim is that the stored figure disagrees with the measured netlist and not that a particular commit made it stale."

- record_id: BRD-097
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The replaced OSD text page draws its full font and logo on the board, and a production image runs the cartridge with both replacements in place"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`build/prod3` is a production-configuration image -- no `-DTINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, so the OSD is live -- built at placement seed 3 and packaged from that seed's routed netlist. Its `.bin` sha256 `b375e05911b26df18151cf51cee56119a66313653fd9add867ca6a3e98c9b006` was uploaded to the card as `/root/cores/console138k/tinygba_prod3.bin` and read back with `tangget` to the same digest before being loaded, and the FPGA answered `core 3` afterwards. The routed netlist carries 110 `DP` cells against the 109 of the `OVERLAY_OFF` image `build/hw2`, the difference being the OSD text page, which only exists in the overlay-enabled configuration -- so the replaced memory is demonstrably in this bitstream rather than optimised away. On the board, `osd on` plus writes through `osd at` drew three lines of text: `MENURAM REPLACES GOWIN DPB`, the alphabet in upper and lower case, and the digits. Both the font and the GBATang logo render correctly, and both live in the block's `INIT_RAM_*` initial contents rather than being written at run time, so this exercises exactly the data a wrapper emitted without those words would have lost. The same image then ran Super Mario Advance 2: `gbaload` reported `save hardware = EEPROM` and `save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM`, the BIOS booted, the cartridge's own intro drew correctly in full colour, and hiding the overlay with `osd off` returned the running game undistorted."
  consequence: "Both replaced memory wrappers are now verified on hardware, in one image, with the machine running a retail cartridge: `menu_ram` by the font and logo it draws, `eeprom_ram` by the save restore it performs and by BRD-096's byte-exact round trip. That closes the gap entry 84 recorded, where the EEPROM had been proved only under simulation and the OSD not at all. One behaviour needs stating because it looks like a fault and is not: turning the overlay on after a cartridge has started composites the text page over the running machine, which presents as the game being replaced by a black page with yellow text. The source records that production keeps the OSD because the menu is a real feature and the BL616 closes it in normal use, and that a bench build with the overlay suppressed avoids the empty menu hiding the machine. On this bench path nothing drives the BL616 menu, so the overlay stays up until it is commanded off, and `osd off` restores the picture. That is the designed behaviour rather than a regression, and anyone testing a production image here should expect it."
  sources:
    - "`build/prod3/tinygba.bin`, sha256 `b375e05911b26df18151cf51cee56119a66313653fd9add867ca6a3e98c9b006`; card copy read back with `tangget` to the same digest before loading"
    - "`build/prod3/tinygba-routed.json`: 110 `DP` cells, against `build/hw2/tinygba-routed.json` at 109 and `SP` 16 in both"
    - "Capture `/tmp/osd3.png` and `/tmp/cap_a.png`: the text `MENURAM REPLACES GOWIN DPB`, `ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ`, `abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz`, `0123456789` and the GBATang logo, all legible"
    - "Capture `/tmp/cap_b.png`: the running cartridge restored after `osd off`, full colour, no distortion"
    - "Board console: `gbaload: save hardware = EEPROM`, `gbaload: save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM`, and `osd: shown` / `osd: hidden`"
    - "Records TCL-067, TCL-069 and BRD-096 for the two conversions and the first hardware test"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, with the image digest confirmed on the card before loading. The font and logo were read from captures rather than inferred from a successful command. The `DP` count comparison establishes that the replaced memory is present in the production bitstream; it does not by itself prove the cell is the OSD page, which rests on the page being the only memory that exists in the overlay-enabled configuration and not otherwise."

- record_id: TCL-071
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A seed sweep must gate on the clock-plane report as well as hold, and must run detached"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`tools/sweep_tinygba_seeds.sh` places a netlist across a seed range and reports hold violations, which its own header says is not the whole deploy criterion. On this device it is not: `build/hw3`, a production-configuration build at seed 14, failed with `NO PLANE PIP: 'clk67' crosses no bridge pip, so it is on general routing`, and the same placement carries 5 hold violations when the timing gate is run against it by hand, so a hold-only sweep would have reported the second and missed the first, which is the one that stops a deploy. `tools/sweep_build_seeds.py` was written to close that: it runs the same two gates the build runs, `tools/clock_plane_report.py` in its non-strict setting and `tools/timing_gate.py`, on each seed's routed netlist, and reports per seed whether the placement is deployable and if not, why. Twelve seeds of the production netlist were launched, eight at a time; six reached place and route before the run was stopped, and five of those six were deployable -- seeds 1, 3, 4, 6 and 8 -- with seed 11 failing place and route outright. All five put `clk67` on a measured-good spine, at 170.097, 195.733, 177.085, 201.735 and 172.384 MHz against a 67.002 MHz constraint, with zero hold violations and no multicycle relaxation. Seed 3 was taken as the best on two counts: the highest total margin across all four clocks at 7.27 of constraint, and the cleanest plane report, `SPINE25/TOP1=good` with no unmeasured entry beside it, where seeds 1 and 8 both carried an unmeasured `SPINE0/TOP0` and seed 6 the best `clk67` but the weakest `clk50` and `hclk`."
  consequence: "The production configuration is not systematically harder to place than the bench one, and `build/hw3`'s failure was seed luck rather than a property of enabling the OSD -- which is the question the sweep existed to answer, and which could not have been answered from a single failed build without risking a false conclusion about the overlay path. Eight of the twelve seeds remain unresolved because the run was stopped once five deployable placements existed, so this records five working seeds and not a failure rate; the surviving six were censored at the point the run ended and must not be read as mostly-failing. Two tooling facts are worth carrying. A sweep must gate on the clock-plane report, because hold-clean and pass-Fmax is exactly the combination TinySNES's BRD-005 shows is insufficient, and a clock on general routing has no skew guarantee at all. And a long sweep has to be launched detached -- `setsid nohup ... &` -- because a plain background job from a shell that later exits takes its children with it: the first attempt at this sweep died with all eight placements at the same line of their logs, having reached the analytical placer and nothing further, which looks like eight independent failures and is one process-group kill."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/sweep_build_seeds.py`, and its per-seed directories under `build/hw3/sweep/seed_N`"
    - "This repository, `build/hw3/sweep/seed_{1,3,4,6,8}/timing-gate.log` and `clock-plane.log`: the per-seed Fmax, hold and plane readings"
    - "This repository, `build/hw3/clock-plane.log`: the seed-14 build's `NO PLANE PIP: 'clk67' crosses no bridge pip, so it is on general routing`. That build exited at this gate, so it has no `timing-gate.log`; the 5 hold violations on the same placement were read by running `tools/timing_gate.py` against `build/hw3` by hand"
    - "This repository, `build/prod3/`: the seed 3 image packaged with `TINYGBA_STAGE=pack` from that seed's routed netlist"
    - "Records TCL-004 and TCL-005 for the clock-plane behaviour, and TinySNES BRD-005 as cited in `tools/clock_plane_report.py`"
  verification: "Measured on 2026-11-15. Each of the five deployable verdicts was re-derived by running `clock_plane_report.py` and `timing_gate.py` by hand against that seed's routed netlist and checking both exit statuses, rather than trusting the sweep's own parser. The sweep tool's parsing was validated first against a known outcome -- `build/hw3`'s routed netlist, which it reproduced as a plane failure with the same `NO PLANE PIP` text and a 5-violation hold count. Not verified: the eight seeds that did not complete, and any failure rate."

- record_id: TCL-072
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The IWRAM wrapper is this project's own RTL, and its sixteen blocks are eight banks of two halfwords rather than sixteen banks"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_iwram.v` -- sixteen `SP` blocks with 224 `MUX2` and 3 `DFFRE` around them, generated by Gowin EDA 1.9.9 -- was replaced by `fpga/gba/src/memory/iwram_ram.v`, written here, and its `.ipc` and `.mod` descriptors were removed with it. The structure is worth recording because it is not what the instance count suggests and it resolves a concern raised before the work began. IWRAM is 32 bits wide over 8192 words, and a block holds 16 bits at its widest, so the word is split into a low and a high halfword in separate blocks: instances 0..7 carry `din[15:0]` and 8..15 carry `din[31:16]`, which is why two instances always share a `BLK_SEL` value and 16 instances use only 8. They are not sixteen banks; they are eight banks of two halfword slices, addressed and enabled together, differing only in their data slice. TCL-069 flagged this as a case where the model's enable-by-match could not be right -- it is right, and the reading was wrong. The address splits three ways: bits [12:10] are the bank and are what `BLKSEL` carries, bits [9:4] are the word address inside a block after a 16-bit port's shift, and bits [1:0] are the byte write strobes, which the vendor passes straight through as `byte_en[1:0]` for the low half and `byte_en[3:2]` for the high. The bank select is registered on the launching edge and gated by `ce & ~wre` -- the same discipline as `eeprom_ram` -- so a write leaves the output mux where it was. Substitution was checked by simulation: `tools/sim/tb_iwram_equiv.sv` runs both wrappers side by side over 20000 random cycles with freely chosen byte strobes plus a directed pass writing every bank at one offset and another sweeping all 16 strobe combinations at one address -- 20050 samples, no mismatch. Three deliberately broken variants are rejected, each reproducing a fault a cell census could not see: gating the bank register on `ce` alone gives 2874 mismatches, transposing the halfwords in the output gives 6100, and driving the high halfword's strobe from `byte_en[1:0]` gives 4949. The build's primitive census is unchanged at 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM, and the memory-placement gate reported only the intended rename of sixteen `sp_inst_N` cells to `bank_i[N].u_lo` and `.u_hi`."
  consequence: "Three of six memory wrappers are now this project's own RTL, thirteen Gowin-headed files remain of which six are built, and the method is settled: restate the vendor structure, prove it by simulation against the original with injected defects that must fail, then confirm the census. Two things this conversion adds to that method. The first is that a high instance count does not imply a deep bank structure -- reading the `DI` and `DO` wiring rather than counting instances showed the halfword split, and that reading should come before any concern about a wrapper being too complex to restate. The second is about the runner: once a wrapper is converted its original is deleted, because the point is that the file stops being carried, so `tools/sim/run_mem_equiv_tb.sh` cannot re-run that comparison afterwards. It now says so instead of failing on a missing file, and the comparison remains reproducible from git history -- `git show <commit>^:fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_eeprom.v` into a temporary file, then the same testbench, still passes. Anyone auditing a conversion should do that rather than trusting the record's claim that it once passed."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/iwram_ram.v`, and `mem_iwram.v` at `git show 8c3e540:fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_iwram.v`"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/tb_iwram_equiv.sv` and `tools/sim/run_mem_equiv_tb.sh`"
    - "This repository, `build/m6/tinygba.json`: the full build with the replacement, primitive counts identical to `build/m5/tinygba.json`"
    - "`git show f8da3f9^:fpga/gba/src/memory/mem_eeprom.v` re-run through `tb_eeprom_equiv.sv`: still `RESULT: PASS` at 24018 samples, confirming a converted comparison stays reproducible from history"
    - "Records TCL-067, TCL-069 and TCL-068 for the first two conversions and the method"
  verification: "Verified on 2026-11-15 by simulation and synthesis. The simulation result stands only because all three injected defects are rejected in the same run, which the runner enforces. Verified on hardware the same day: `build/iw3` carries this replacement, the RetroRegen suite reported `RESULT: ALL REGIONS PASS` with IWRAM 32K cells PASS, and a retail cartridge ran on the same image (BRD-098)."

- record_id: BRD-098
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The replaced IWRAM passes the diagnostic suite's memory test on hardware, and a retail cartridge runs on the image"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`build/iw3` is an `OVERLAY_OFF` image carrying all three replaced memory wrappers -- `menu_ram`, `eeprom_ram` and now `iwram_ram` -- placed at seed 3 and packaged from that seed's routed netlist, with `.bin` sha256 `2dfe9b642ae29b456a47e65af7f5892adad63de9dc43af4601b177d65172d0fb`. The routed netlist was checked before packaging: 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM, identical to the image with the Gowin wrapper, and the memory-placement gate reported only the intended rename of sixteen `sp_inst_N` cells to `bank_i[N].u_lo` and `.u_hi`. On the board the RetroRegen GBA Test Suite v1.5 was loaded and driven to its memory test by host-side keypad injection, and it reported `RESULT: ALL REGIONS PASS` with IWRAM 32K cells PASS, EWRAM 256K PASS, VRAM 96K PASS, PRAM 1K PASS and OAM 1K PASS in 1377 ms -- the same suite and the same verdict BRD-069 records for the pre-conversion core, so the replacement did not disturb it. Super Mario Advance 2 was then loaded on the same image and drew its world map correctly at 97455 distinct colours. The input path used to drive the menu was `fpgapipe` rather than `tools/gba_input.py`, because the latter opens `/dev/ttyUSB1` -- the FT2232 -- which is not enumerated in two-wire mode; the pipe carries the same joystick frames to UART1 directly. The button encoding must be read from `tools/gba_input.py`'s `BUTTONS` table rather than assumed: those are bit *positions* (`DOWN` 5, `A` 8), not masks, and a first attempt using masks moved nothing and looked like a dead input path."
  consequence: "All three replaced memory wrappers are verified on hardware, and IWRAM specifically is verified by the diagnostic suite that exists to test it rather than by a cartridge inferring it. That matters more than the other two because IWRAM holds the live stack, so a fault there corrupts everything and would be easy to mistake for a CPU or GPU defect. The diagnostic's own note that the IWRAM decoder is skipped because the region holds the live stack also explains why its decoder column reads LIVE rather than PASS -- that is the suite's design, not a failure. Two operational facts were learned and are worth keeping: `tools/gba_input.py` is a one-wire tool and cannot be used in two-wire mode, so `fpgapipe` is the way to drive the keypad when the board is being loaded from the card; and the HID button encoding is a bit-position table, so any script reimplementing the frame should import or copy that table rather than reconstructing it from a guessed layout."
  sources:
    - "`build/iw3/tinygba.bin`, sha256 `2dfe9b642ae29b456a47e65af7f5892adad63de9dc43af4601b177d65172d0fb`"
    - "`build/iw3/memory-census.log` and `build/m6/tinygba.json`: 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM, 66 distributed RAM"
    - "Capture `/tmp/mem2.png`: `RESULT: ALL REGIONS PASS`, IWRAM 32K cells PASS, EWRAM 256K PASS, VRAM 96K PASS, PRAM 1K PASS, OAM 1K PASS, `TIME: 1377 MS`"
    - "Capture `/tmp/iw1.png`: the world map of Super Mario Advance 2 at 97455 distinct colours on the same image"
    - "`gbaload \"/root/media/gba/diagnostics/RetroRegen-GBA-Test-v1.5.gba\"` reporting `284384 bytes -> the core`, `save hardware = SRAM`, `the core is running`"
    - "Records BRD-069 for the same suite's pre-conversion result, and TCL-072 for the conversion"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, with the image's routed netlist and census checked before packaging. The diagnostic verdict was read from a capture of the suite's own screen rather than inferred, and it is the same verdict BRD-069 recorded before the change.
