Not verified: the suite's cartridge SRAM test reported NOT DETECTED, which is expected for a cartridge loaded into SDRAM rather than read from its own save chip and is unrelated to IWRAM."

- record_id: TCL-073
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The framebuffer wrapper is this project's own RTL: eighteen bits are two nine-bit slices and its nineteen banks cannot be named by a three-bit BLK_SEL"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`fpga/gba/src/fb.v` -- 6327 lines, 38 `DPX9B` blocks, 648 `MUX2` and 38 `LUT5`, generated by Gowin EDA 1.9.11 -- was replaced by `fpga/gba/src/fb_ram.v`, written here, with its `.ipc` and `.mod` removed. Two structural facts make it unlike the other three memories converted. First, eighteen bits of data is two nine-bit slices, so each bank is a pair of blocks, and 38400 words over 19 banks at 2048 words per block gives the 38 blocks. Second, and this is the part no other wrapper in the tree does, `BLK_SEL_n` is only three bits wide and so can name only eight blocks, while the memory has nineteen banks; the vendor therefore does not name them at all. Every instance carries the same `BLK_SEL = 3'b001`, and each receives its own one-hot decode of the bank field on `BLKSEL` bit 0, so a block is enabled when the two agree and exactly one bank acts at a time. That one-hot decode is what the 38 `LUT5` cells were for. Address bits [15:11] are the bank and [10:0] the word address in a block; there are no byte strobes, so the block's spare address bits are tied low rather than carrying strobes the way `iwram_ram` uses them. The block enables are combinational in the address and only the output mux select is registered, gated by the read enables -- registering the enables as well would have enabled the bank of the previous address. Substitution was checked by simulation: `tools/sim/tb_fb_equiv.sv` compares both wrappers over 20000 random cycles on two independent clocks plus a directed pass writing and reading all nineteen banks, giving 24078 samples and no mismatch, and three injected defects are rejected -- bank register gated on `ce` alone gives 5871 mismatches, transposed halves 2552, and a bank field cut to three bits so only eight banks are distinguishable 4485. The build's primitive census is unchanged at 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM, and the memory-placement gate reported the rename as 38 `dpx9b_inst_N` cells becoming 38 `bank_i[N].u_lo`/`.u_hi` cells, count unchanged."
  consequence: "Four of six memory wrappers are now this project's own RTL; twelve Gowin-headed files remain of which five are built, and only `vram_lo` and `vram_hi` are left. Three things this conversion adds to the method. The `BLK_SEL` width is the point that generalises: a three-bit parameter cannot express nineteen banks, so any wrapper with more than eight blocks must be read for how it selects them rather than assumed to use the indexed scheme the others use, and `eeprom_ram`, `iwram_ram` and `menu_ram` all fit under eight while this did not. The simulation caught a real defect that no census could see: the first version fed one concatenation to both slices, which is 27 bits into an 18-bit port and gave both halves the low nine bits; it built cleanly and would have drawn wrong colours. And removing `fb.v` retired the last `LUT5` instantiation in the tree, which then broke the build in a way worth recording: `tools/build_tinygba.sh` strips `LUT5` from its cell-library shim and `tools/cells_behavioral.v` supplied it, and with both still in place and nothing instantiating it, yosys pulled in the real library copy and collided with the behavioural one -- `ERROR: Re-definition of module 'LUT5'`. The fix is that both were removed, with a comment in each place saying the definition must return if a `LUT5` instantiation ever does. A shim kept for a consumer that no longer exists is not free."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/fb_ram.v`, and `fb.v` at `git show 8b6a4ff:fpga/gba/src/fb.v`"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/tb_fb_equiv.sv` and `tools/sim/run_mem_equiv_tb.sh` (the `fb` case)"
    - "This repository, `build/m8/tinygba.json`: the full build with the replacement, primitive counts identical to `build/m6/tinygba.json`"
    - "`build/m8/memory-census.log`: 38 `dpx9b_inst_N` removed and 38 `bank_i[N].u_lo`/`.u_hi` added, total unchanged"
    - "`tools/cells_behavioral.v` and `tools/build_tinygba.sh`: the `LUT5` removal and the note that it must return with any future instantiation"
    - "Records TCL-067, TCL-069, TCL-072 for the three earlier conversions, and TCL-034 for why `MUX2` and `LUT5` were shimmed at all"
  verification: "Verified on 2026-11-15 by simulation and synthesis, and on hardware the same day: `build/fb3` was placed at seed 3, reported deployable by `tools/sweep_build_seeds.py`, packaged, loaded, and Super Mario Advance 2 drew its world map correctly at 101838 distinct colours (BRD-099). The simulation result stands only because all three injected defects are rejected in the same run. One limit of the testbench is worth recording: its stimulus is constrained to addresses below 38400, because the nineteenth bank is partial and neither wrapper is required to agree above the end of the memory."

- record_id: BRD-099
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The replaced framebuffer draws correctly on hardware, so four of the six memory wrappers are verified on the board"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`build/fb3` carries all four replaced memory wrappers -- `menu_ram`, `eeprom_ram`, `iwram_ram` and now `fb_ram` -- placed at seed 3 and packaged from that seed's routed netlist, `.bin` sha256 `b8ed1b9ed324f0c03009788ffe1ce4a8e0ddcf28875e9a7d6c5f8278699b7aa7`. Both candidate seeds were deployable: seeds 3 and 4 passed the clock-plane and timing gates with zero hold violations. The routed netlist held 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM and 66 distributed RAM, identical to the image before the framebuffer replacement, and the memory-placement gate reported the change as 38 `dpx9b_inst_N` cells becoming 38 `bank_i[N].u_lo`/`.u_hi` cells with the count unchanged. The single change to `tools/cells_behavioral.v` in this cycle was removing the now-dead `LUT5` definition, which the build requires: the shim strip and the behavioural definition had been a matched pair supplying a cell nothing instantiated any more, and leaving both in place made yosys pull in the real library copy and fail with `Re-definition of module 'LUT5'`. On the board the image loaded as `fpga: core 3`, `gbaload` reported `save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM` and started the cartridge, and Super Mario Advance 2 drew its world map correctly at 101838 distinct colours through the capture card in the project's usual pillarboxed 1920x1080 frame."
  consequence: "The framebuffer is the one memory in this set whose failure would be immediately visible as a wrong picture rather than as a wrong value, so a correct full-colour frame is strong evidence that the replacement is right end to end: bank selection across nineteen partial banks, the nine-bit slice split, and the registered output mux all have to be correct for the image to look like this. With `menu_ram`, `eeprom_ram` and `iwram_ram` already verified by the OSD font, the save round trip and the diagnostic suite respectively, four of the six memory wrappers are now confirmed on hardware and only the two VRAMs remain. The capture also gives a second observation of the same launch: BRD-097 recorded the world map at 97455 distinct colours on the previous image and this one at 101838, which is the ordinary frame-to-frame variation of an animated map rather than a difference attributable to the change, and should not be read as one."
  sources:
    - "`build/fb3/tinygba.bin`, sha256 `b8ed1b9ed324f0c03009788ffe1ce4a8e0ddcf28875e9a7d6c5f8278699b7aa7`"
    - "`build/m8/sweep/seed_3/` and `seed_4/`: both `DEPLOYABLE`, clock-plane and timing exit 0, hold 0"
    - "`build/fb3/memory-census.log` and `build/m8/tinygba.json`: 66 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`, 125 block RAM, 66 distributed RAM"
    - "Capture `/tmp/fb1.png`: the world map of Super Mario Advance 2 at 101838 distinct colours"
    - "Board console: `fpga: core 3 answering on UART1 at 2000000 baud`, `gbaload: save restored, 8192 bytes of EEPROM`"
    - "Records BRD-097, BRD-098 and TCL-073 for the other conversions and this one's method"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, with the routed netlist and census checked before packaging. The picture was read from a capture rather than reported. Not verified here: the VRAMs are still Gowin wrappers in this image, so this says nothing about them, and the diagnostic suite was not re-run on this image -- the IWRAM evidence is BRD-098's, on an earlier image."

- record_id: TCL-074
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Neither placement gate was gating the production configuration, and the census gate called a missing baseline a pass"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Both placement gates key on a configuration name derived from the `-DTINYGBA_*` defines, and a production build defines none of them, so its key is `default`. Neither baseline file had a `default` entry: `tools/memory_baseline.json` held `TINYGBA_FETCHTRACE+TINYGBA_STALLTRACE` and `TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, and `tools/synth_baseline.json` held `TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF` and `TINYGBA_STATUS_TEST`. Every production image this project has deployed was therefore never checked by either gate. The two gates also disagreed about what a missing baseline means: `tools/synth_parity.py` returns 2 and `tools/build_tinygba.sh` prints `(no baseline for this configuration)` and continues, while `tools/memory_census.py` returned 0 under `--check`, which is indistinguishable from a pass. TCL-070 recorded the symptom as an `iosys` coverage gap and that reading was wrong -- the census tool covers `iosys` correctly and finds `iosys.disp.menu_mem.u_menu` in a production netlist; what was missing was the baseline entry, not the coverage. Three baselines were recorded: `default` at 12479 flops and 149 block RAM with a 124-array census, `TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF` corrected from 11471 flops and 156 block RAM to 12417 and 148, and `TINYGBA_STATUS_TEST` corrected from 9100 flops to 10004. The staleness mattered: `STATUS_TEST` was passing at `+904 flops (+9.9%)` against a ten per cent budget, one tenth of a point from failing on changes that were deliberate. `tools/memory_census.py` now returns 2 rather than 0 for a missing baseline under `--check`, matching `synth_parity`'s convention, and the build handles exit 2 as an informational warning exactly as it already did for gate 1b, with exit 1 still a failure. Its comment claiming an ungated configuration was 'reported and skipped rather than silently passing' was false and has been corrected."
  consequence: "The gates now gate the configuration that ships. A production build reports `change +0 flops` and `census matches the baseline for 'default'`, and an injected one-entry baseline change still produces exit 1 and a DIFFERS report, so the gate is live rather than merely present. Two things generalise beyond this fix. A count is only a regression test if the thing it counts is the thing that ships, and here the gates were thorough, correctly keyed, well documented, and pointed at three configurations none of which was the deployed one -- which is a failure mode that reading either tool in isolation would not reveal. And returning 0 for 'I have nothing to compare against' is the specific defect: the tool could not distinguish 'matches' from 'was never checked', so the only visible symptom was silence. Diagnostic builds are deliberately still ungated, because there are twenty such defines and most are used once by hand; that is now stated in the warning rather than implied by a pass."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `tools/memory_baseline.json` and `tools/synth_baseline.json`: the recorded keys before and after this cycle"
    - "`python3 tools/synth_parity.py build/m9/tinygba.json --key default` before the change: `no baseline entry for 'default'`, known keys `TINYGBA_OVERLAY_OFF`, `TINYGBA_STATUS_TEST`"
    - "`python3 tools/memory_census.py build/m9/tinygba.json --check --key default` before the change: exit 0"
    - "`build/g2/parity.log` and `build/g2/memory-census.log` from a production build after the change: `change +0 flops`, `census matches the baseline for 'default'`"
    - "Injected-drift check: one baseline entry replaced, `--check` returning 1 with `census DIFFERS from the baseline for 'default'`, then restored"
    - "Record TCL-070, whose `iosys` reading this corrects"
  verification: "Measured on 2026-11-15 against production netlists built from the current tree (`build/m9` and the reused-netlist run in `build/g2`). Both gates were run by hand on the same netlist before and after the change, and the failure path was proved by injecting a drift rather than assumed from reading the code. Not done: the diagnostic configurations other than `STATUS_TEST` remain without baselines by design, so a memory change confined to one of them would still go unreported."

- record_id: TCL-075
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The high VRAM is eight banks of two halfword slices with a bank select registered per port, one per clock"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`fpga/gba/src/gpu/vram_hi.v` -- sixteen `DPB`, 448 `MUX2` and six `DFFRE`, generated by Gowin EDA 1.9.9 -- was replaced by `gpu/vram_hi_ram.v`, written here, with its descriptors removed. The structure is `iwram_ram`'s: sixteen blocks are eight banks of two halfword slices, instances 0..7 carrying `dina[15:0]` and 8..15 `dina[31:16]`, with two instances always sharing a `BLK_SEL` because they are the halves of one bank. The distinguishing feature is that this memory is genuinely dual-port with independent clocks: the vendor has six `DFFRE`, three bits per port, each group on its own clock and gated by that port's `~wre & ce`, so a write on one side leaves that side's output mux where it was. Address bits [12:10] are the bank, [9:0] the word address, and the block's two spare address bits carry the byte strobes -- `byte_en[1:0]` for the low halfword block and `byte_en[3:2]` for the high, which is the detail a first draft got wrong by sending one pair to both. Substitution was checked by simulation: `tools/sim/tb_vram_hi_equiv.sv` compares both wrappers over 20000 random cycles on two independent clocks with every strobe combination, plus a directed pass over all eight banks and a sweep of all sixteen strobe patterns at one address, giving 24082 samples with no mismatch; three injected defects are rejected -- bank register gated on `ce` alone 5078 mismatches, transposed halves 8891, and the high halfword's strobe driven from `byte_en[1:0]` 7845. Primitive counts are unchanged at 67 `DPB`, 16 `SP` and 43 `DPX9B`."
  consequence: "Five of six memory wrappers are this project's own RTL, and this one establishes the shape the sixth reuses. The reusable finding is that a dual-port memory needs one bank-select register per port, on that port's clock -- a single register shared between ports would be wrong whenever the two clocks are unrelated, which they are here by construction. The strobe-split defect is worth recording separately because it is silent in synthesis and was caught only by the strobe sweep: sending `byte_en[1:0]` to both halves builds cleanly and writes the wrong bytes in the upper halfword. Neither fault would move any cell count."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gpu/vram_hi_ram.v`, and `vram_hi.v` at `git show da86a46:fpga/gba/src/gpu/vram_hi.v`"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/tb_vram_hi_equiv.sv` and the `vram_hi` case in `tools/sim/run_mem_equiv_tb.sh`"
    - "This repository, `build/m11/tinygba.json`: the build with both VRAMs replaced, 67 `DPB`, 16 `SP`, 43 `DPX9B`"
    - "Records TCL-072 for the halfword-slice shape this reuses, and TCL-073 for the one-hot decode the sibling wrapper needed"
  verification: "Verified on 2026-11-15 by simulation and synthesis, and on hardware the same day (BRD-100). The simulation result stands only because all three injected defects are rejected in the same run."

- record_id: TCL-076
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The low VRAM is the framebuffer's shape rather than its sibling's: sixteen banks, too many for a three-bit BLK_SEL, so it decodes one-hot"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`fpga/gba/src/gpu/vram_lo.v` -- 32 `DPB`, 960 `MUX2`, 32 `LUT4` and eight `DFFRE` -- was replaced by `gpu/vram_lo_ram.v`, with its descriptors removed. It sits between the two patterns already converted and taking either for granted would have been wrong. It is like `vram_hi` in being 32 bits wide over two halfword slices and in having two independent clocks: instances 0..15 carry `dina[15:0]` and 16..31 `dina[31:16]`, so two instances share a `BLK_SEL`, and each port's bank select is registered on its own clock and gated by that port's `~wre & ce`. It is like `fb` in its bank count: sixteen banks is more than the three bits of `BLK_SEL` can name, so every instance carries `BLK_SEL = 3'b001` and each receives its own one-hot decode of the four-bit bank field on `BLKSEL` bit 0 -- the 32 `LUT4` cells in the vendor's version, sixteen per port. Address bits [13:10] are the bank and [9:0] the word address, with `byte_en[1:0]` and `byte_en[3:2]` as the halves' strobes. Substitution was checked by simulation: `tools/sim/tb_vram_lo_equiv.sv` runs 20000 random cycles on two clocks with every strobe combination plus a directed pass over all sixteen banks and a sweep of all sixteen strobe patterns, giving 24114 samples with no mismatch, and three injected defects are rejected -- bank register gated on `ce` alone 4545 mismatches, transposed halves 5121, and a bank field cut to three bits so banks 8..15 alias onto 0..7 7459."
  consequence: "All six memory wrappers are now this project's own RTL and no memory in the shipped design is Gowin-generated; ten Gowin-headed files remain, of which three are built and all three are PLL wrappers. The lesson worth keeping is that the six wrappers used three different bank-selection schemes between them -- indexed with eight or fewer blocks (`menu_ram`, `eeprom_ram`, `iwram_ram`, `vram_hi`), one-hot because the block count exceeds what `BLK_SEL` can name (`fb`, `vram_lo`), and asymmetric in width (`eeprom_ram`) -- and which one applies is not visible from the instance count. `iwram_ram` and `vram_hi` both have sixteen instances and both use the indexed scheme; `vram_lo` has thirty-two and uses one-hot. Reading `BLK_SEL_0` is what settles it: a single repeated value across instances means one-hot, distinct values mean indexed."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gpu/vram_lo_ram.v`, and `vram_lo.v` at `git show da86a46:fpga/gba/src/gpu/vram_lo.v`"
    - "This repository, `tools/sim/tb_vram_lo_equiv.sv` and the `vram_lo` case in `tools/sim/run_mem_equiv_tb.sh`"
    - "This repository, `build/m11/tinygba.json`, and the memory-placement gate reporting 96 changed paths -- 48 removed and 48 added -- with the primitive count unchanged"
    - "Records TCL-073 for the one-hot scheme and TCL-075 for the dual-clock halfword shape"
  verification: "Verified on 2026-11-15 by simulation and synthesis, and on hardware the same day (BRD-100). The one-hot reading was confirmed by checking that all 32 instances carry the same `BLK_SEL = 3'b001` and that each has its own `lut_f_N` decode on the four-bit bank field, rather than inferred from the block count."

- record_id: BRD-100
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "All six memory wrappers are this project's own RTL and the machine passes every region on the board, VRAM included"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`build/vr3` is the first image in which no memory is Gowin-generated. It carries `menu_ram`, `eeprom_ram`, `iwram_ram`, `fb_ram`, `vram_hi_ram` and `vram_lo_ram`, placed at seed 3 and packaged from that seed's routed netlist, `.bin` sha256 `0d84877ed987e946c187d96c0cf987ef0909318f068a98ad2b9ab6c304d95282`. Both candidate seeds were deployable at zero hold. The routed netlist held 67 `DPB`, 16 `SP` and 43 `DPX9B`, unchanged from the image before the two VRAMs were replaced, and the register-parity gate reported `change +0 flops` while the memory-placement gate reported exactly 96 changed paths -- 48 removed and 48 added -- with the count unchanged. On the board the RetroRegen GBA Test Suite v1.5 reported `RESULT: ALL REGIONS PASS` with IWRAM 32K cells PASS, EWRAM 256K, VRAM 96K PASS, PRAM 1K and OAM 1K all passing, and VRAM's decoder also PASS, in 1377 ms. Super Mario Advance 2 then ran on the same image and drew its world map correctly at 89735 distinct colours."
  consequence: "The wrapper conversion that TCL-065 opened is finished for the memory system: six of six, with each verified by simulation against its original under injected defects and then on hardware. VRAM is the one that matters most for this last pair, because the suite tests it explicitly rather than a cartridge implying it, and the 1377 ms figure matches BRD-069's pre-conversion result and BRD-098's exactly -- the same suite, the same verdict, the same duration, which is a stronger statement than a pass alone because a timing shift would have suggested a different test path. Ten Gowin-headed files remain in the tree of which three are built, all of them PLL wrappers, and those are the only vendor-generated RTL left in the design. What this does not change: the persistence question, since BRD-094 still stands that the store decays rather than clears, and the licensing question, since whether redistribution of Gowin's IP-generator output is permitted still depends on a EULA nobody here has read and the three PLL wrappers still carry it."
  sources:

    - "Capture `/tmp/vr1.png`: the world map of Super Mario Advance 2 at 89735 distinct colours"
    - "Records BRD-069 and BRD-098 for the same suite's earlier results, and TCL-075 and TCL-076 for the two conversions"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, with the routed netlist and both gates checked before packaging and the image's digest recorded. The suite's verdict was read from a capture of its own screen. Not verified: the three PLL wrappers are still Gowin-generated in this image, and the suite's cartridge-SRAM field reports NOT DETECTED as it did in BRD-098, which is expected for a cartridge loaded from the card rather than read from its own save chip."

- record_id: TCL-077
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Gowin's EULA is not publicly retrievable, so the redistribution question cannot be closed by reading it here"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Eight cycles of records have deferred the question of whether Gowin's terms permit redistributing its IP-generator output by saying that reading the EULA would settle it. An attempt to do so on this machine did not settle it, and the reason is worth recording so the deferral stops being repeated. There is no Gowin EDA installation here: `find / -iname '*licen*' -path '*owin*'` returns nothing, and no `/opt`, `/usr/local` or home-directory Gowin tree exists, which matches TCL-065's finding that the vendor flow was only ever run once as an offline oracle under a temporary setup whose licence acceptance is not reproducible from this tree. The public licence page at `https://www.gowinsemi.com/en/support/license/` returns HTTP 200 with 142,556 bytes, and its extracted text is an **application form** rather than terms: it is headed `Apply License`, offers `Register / Member Login`, and contains thirteen occurrences of `license`, zero of `EULA`, zero of `redistribut` and zero of `generated`. It is a page for requesting a licence to use the EDA, not a document granting or restricting redistribution of what the EDA emits. Three further candidate URLs were probed and returned nothing usable before the attempt was stopped. The conclusion is that the terms are behind a registration wall and cannot be read from this workspace."
  consequence: "SUPERSEDED BY TCL-078, which read the agreement. The text was obtained by another agent through a route not reproduced here, and the clauses were then checked against it directly: Gowin's terms restrict the Software and are silent on its output, so the question this record called unanswerable is answered. The claim below that the action 'cannot be taken' was true of this agent working alone and from public URLs, and false in general -- the document is obtainable, just not by the route tried here. Kept as written so the correction is legible. The recommendation that has appeared in every entry's Next Steps since entry 80 -- 'reading Gowin's EULA would still settle whether any of the conversion is required for distribution' -- is now known to be an action this agent cannot take unaided, and should stop being carried forward as though it were a cheap next step. What can be said without the document is what TCL-065 already established: the sixteen original Gowin-headed files carried `All rights reserved` with no grant, the six memory wrappers have since been replaced so no memory in the shipped design is vendor-generated, and three PLL wrappers remain. What cannot be said is whether redistribution of the three that remain is permitted. That makes the practical position sharper rather than fuzzier: the conversion work already done removed the question for the entire memory system, and replacing the three PLL wrappers would remove it completely -- whereas confirming the licence would leave the files in place. Anyone who can obtain the EULA, meaning whoever holds or can create a Gowin account and accept the EDA licence, should read it and record what it says; an agent should not guess at it, and should not assert a permission or a prohibition it has not read. Distribution of a bitstream built from this tree, as distinct from distribution of the source, is a further question this record does not touch."
  sources:
    - "`find / -iname '*licen*' -path '*owin*'` on this machine: no results; no Gowin installation present"
    - "`https://www.gowinsemi.com/en/support/license/`, fetched 2026-11-15: HTTP 200, 142,556 bytes; extracted text is an `Apply License` / `Register / Member Login` form with no terms document"
    - "Keyword counts over the extracted text: `license` 13, `EULA` 0, `redistribut` 0, `generated` 0"
    - "Three further candidate licence URLs probed and returned no usable content before the attempt was stopped"
    - "Records TCL-065 and TCL-072 for the file inventory and the conversions that reduced the question's scope, and core-log entries 80 through 89 where the deferral recurs"
  verification: "Established 2026-11-15 by inspection of this machine and of one fetched public page. This record does not state what Gowin's terms say, because they were not obtained; it records only that they are not available here, which is a fact about this workspace rather than about the licence."

- record_id: TCL-078
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Gowin's EULA restricts the Software and is silent on its output, which resolves the redistribution question for these files"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Gowin's End User Licence Agreement for Gowin Software, version 2026.03, was read, and the clauses that bear on carrying Gowin-generated files in a GPL-3.0 repository were checked against the text rather than inferred. The agreement's operative word is `Software`, defined in §1(1) as \"the computer program(s) furnished to you by Gowin, in whatever media, form and by whatever method\"; a file the tools emit is not one of those programs, so it is not `Software` and the agreement's restrictions do not reach it. §3 is titled `General limit` and every one of its seven prohibitions binds the Software: (1) not to modify, reverse engineer, disassemble, decrypt, decompile or make derivative works of *the Software*; (2) not to transmit the Software or display its object code; (3) not to publish the results of any benchmarking *of the Software*; (4) not to copy it beyond one backup; (5) not to prepare derivative works of it; (6) not to distribute or transfer *the Software*; (7) not to remove notices on or in *the Software*. §4 vests ownership of \"the Software and all copies thereof\" in Gowin and claims nothing about designs or generated files, while requiring that Gowin's \"trademark, copyright notice and logo\" not be deleted or modified. §5(4), the termination clause, treats output as the user's: after the agreement ends \"you are not required to destroy output data which you obtained using the Software during the term of this Agreement and may continue to use it\". §2 grants use of the Software \"for the sole purposes of developing, synthesizing, place route, testing and verifying designs only for Gowin Devices\", which this project satisfies because its target is a GW5AST-LV138. A search of Gowin's own clauses for `distribut`, `transfer` and `sublicens` returns four hits, every one of them about the Software or about transferring rights in it, and none about output. The agreement therefore neither permits nor forbids redistributing generated files: it does not address them, and the `All rights reserved` header Gowin's generator stamps on each file is machine-written boilerplate that the agreement's own silence does not convert into a restriction on output."
  consequence: "The question carried in every entry's Next Steps from entry 80 to entry 90 is answered, and the answer is that the thirteen Gowin-headed files may be kept and redistributed with their headers intact. Two consequences follow. The headers must stay exactly as generated, because §4 forbids deleting or modifying them; that also means the twelve replaced files could not have been relabelled into compliance, so replacing rather than relicensing them was the only sound treatment, which is what TCL-065 concluded for a different reason. And the two grey areas are narrower than they first look but not nothing. §3(1) forbids reverse engineering *the Software*, and reading PLL settings back out of a bitstream examines output rather than the program, which §5(4) expressly permits continued use of; so the `gowin_unpack` readback that TCL-002 relied on is not what §3(1) forbids. §3(3) is the clause with real scope, because it forbids publishing benchmarking results of the Software *or using such results for any other software development activities*, and it is broad enough that comparing the vendor flow against the open flow in performance terms would be an uncomfortable thing to publish. Recording a PLL's operating point so that a design can be reproduced is not benchmarking the tool; publishing 'Gowin EDA takes N minutes and reaches X MHz where the open flow takes M and reaches Y' would be, and this project should not do the latter without advice. Three limits on this record. It is a reading, not legal advice. It is the 2026.03 text, while the files in this tree were generated in 2024 under an earlier version that was not available, so the 2024 terms are assumed rather than read. And the text was obtained by another agent through a route not reproduced here; the clause citations were checked against that extracted text by hand, but its authenticity rests on its content and its Arm licence appendices rather than on a verified download."
  sources:
    - "Gowin `END USER LICENCE AGREEMENT FOR GOWIN SOFTWARE`, `Version:2026.03`, extracted text at `/tmp/claude-1000/-run-media-vash-GIT-TinyGBA/bb9c613a-15c8-4302-850a-f1e95c47c548/scratchpad/eula_zip/eula.txt`, 1691 lines: §1(1) at line 28, §2 at line 40, §3 at line 79, §4 at line 101, §5(4) at lines 140-145, §10 at line 212 where Gowin's terms end and the Arm licences begin"
    - "Section map read from the text: 1 Definitions (line 27), 2 License Grants (40), 3 General limit (79), 4 Ownership (101), 5 Confidentiality (112), 6 Updates and support (147), 7 Disclaimers (158), 8 Limitation of Liability (177), 9 Termination (192), 10 General (212)"
    - "Search over Gowin's clauses only (lines 1-211) for `distribut|transfer|sublicens`: four hits at lines 42, 65, 89 and 110, each about the Software"
    - "Records TCL-065 for the file inventory and why replacing rather than relicensing was chosen, TCL-077 for the failed attempt to obtain the text, and TCL-002 for the Gowin EDA oracle use that §3(1) and §3(3) bear on"
  verification: "The clauses quoted here were read directly from the extracted text and each quotation was located in it before being used: §1(1) and §3 in full, §2(1), §4, and §5(4). The claim that nothing in the agreement addresses output is from searching Gowin's own clauses for the relevant terms rather than from recollection. Not verified: that the 2026.03 text is the version that governed the 2024 generation, and the authenticity of the extracted file beyond its content. This is a reading by an agent, not legal advice, and any decision to rely on it for distribution should be taken by the operator."

- record_id: BRD-101
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The CPU cannot write the cartridge SRAM window on the board: reads return restored data, writes are silently dropped"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "A new probe, `tools/roms/saveprobe.s`, writes a known pattern through the cartridge SRAM window at `0x0E000000` as a game would and paints two squares: one for whether the canonical pattern was already present when it started, and one for whether writing a *different* pattern and reading it back succeeded. Under mGBA 0.10.5 both squares are green with mGBA's own `.sav` present, so the probe is sound. On the board, with `gbaload` reporting `save hardware = SRAM` and `save restored, 32768 bytes of SRAM`, the first square is GREEN and the second is RED. The first square green means the CPU reads the SRAM window correctly -- it found the 32768-byte pattern that `gbaload` had just restored through the host path. The second red means a pattern the CPU itself wrote was not there when it read it back. Writes to the cartridge SRAM window are therefore silently dropped while reads from it work, and the two are the same address range through the same FSM. The colour readings are unambiguous single pixels sampled from the capture: `(18,255,0)` at the first square and `(226,0,0)` at the second. This is the first evidence that separates a game-written save from transport: BRD-092 proved the host could move bytes in and out of the window through the RISC-V port, which does not go through `gba_memory`'s write gate at all, and no earlier record tested the path a cartridge actually uses."
  consequence: "No cartridge with an SRAM save has ever been able to save on this board, and the four SRAM titles in the sweep -- Metroid Fusion, Metroid Zero Mission, Castlevania and Fire Emblem -- boot and play while silently failing to persist, which is why nobody noticed: a failed SRAM write is invisible unless something reads it back. The observed behaviour is exactly what `gba_memory.sv`'s `isreadonly()` gate would produce: for regions `0x0E` and `0x0F` it forces `sdram_wr = 0` and `sdram_rd = 1` unless `config_backup_type` is flash512K, flash1M or SRAM, so a wrong `config_backup_type` converts every CPU write into a read and returns the old contents -- which is what the probe sees, and which would not affect the host restore path because `rv_sdram_adapter` never consults that function. That is the leading hypothesis and it is not yet confirmed: the alternative is that the write reaches SDRAM and the read returns a buffered value, which the same evidence would also fit. Distinguishing them needs one diagnostic build that forces `isreadonly(0x0E)` false and re-runs the probe; if the second square turns green the gate was the fault, and if it stays red the fault is downstream in the SDRAM write path. Either way this record establishes that the defect is in the CPU-side write, not in the save transport, the save engine or the card, all of which BRD-092 and BRD-100 show working."
  sources:
    - "`tools/roms/saveprobe.s` and `build/test/saveprobe.gba` (complement `$1A`, 4096 bytes, `SRAM_V` marker at offset 572)"
    - "Capture `/tmp/saveprobe_mgba_pass.png`: mGBA 0.10.5, both squares green, with `/tmp/mgba_saveprobe.sav` present and passing `tools/save/sav_pattern.py check --size 32k`"
    - "Capture `/tmp/saveprobe_board_fail.png`: on the board, first square green `(18,255,0)`, second square red `(226,0,0)`"
    - "Board console: `gbaload: save hardware = SRAM`, `gbaload: restoring 32768 bytes of SRAM`, `gbaload: save restored, 32768 bytes of SRAM`, `gbaload: 4096 bytes in; the core is running`"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: `isreadonly()` at line 935 and its two call sites at lines 353 and 480, which force `sdram_rd = 1, sdram_wr = 0`"
    - "Records BRD-092 for the transport proof this distinguishes from, BRD-089 for the loader's save-type configuration, and BRD-070 for the read-only behaviour when `config_backup_type` is `BACKUP_NONE`"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, with the same probe passing under mGBA first as TCL-041 requires, so a probe defect is excluded before the board result is read. The probe also caught a defect in itself during this work: its first version tested the write by writing the *canonical* pattern and reading it back, which goes green whenever the window already holds that pattern, and it did exactly that on the second board run after a restore. It now writes a pattern that differs by one, and both the pass under mGBA and the failure on the board were re-taken with that version. Not established: the root cause, which is recorded as a hypothesis with the experiment that would settle it."
- record_id: BRD-102
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The CPU's store to the cartridge SRAM window is absent from SDRAM, not hidden by a stale read"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "BRD-101 established that a pattern the CPU writes to `0x0E000000` is not there when the CPU reads it back, and left two explanations: the write is dropped, or the write lands and the read returns a buffered value. The two are separated by reading the window through the host, because the RISC-V port does not share the CPU's read timing and cannot inherit a stale-read artefact. With a 32768-byte file of zero bytes planted as the save -- a pattern the probe never writes -- and the probe run to completion, `gbasave` returned 32768 bytes byte-for-byte identical to the planted file and differing from the canonical pattern in 32640 of 32768 positions. The window therefore still contains exactly what the host put there. The store is absent from SDRAM."
  consequence: "The fault is a lost write and not a read artefact, which removes an entire class of explanation -- read buffering, `cpu_rdata` timing, the `double_req` output mux -- and points at the write path itself. It also means the host restore is a trustworthy reader: it returns precisely what is in the window, so it can be used as the instrument for every later save question without going through the display. One measurement in this cycle is recorded as a confound so it is not repeated: an earlier run planted the *old* save, which already contained the canonical pattern, and then reported that the dump matched that pattern. It did, but only because `gbaload` restored it -- `gbaload: restoring 32768 bytes of SRAM` is what revealed the mistake. A dump comparison is only meaningful when the planted contents differ from everything the probe writes, which is why the zeros substitution is part of the record rather than an incidental detail."
  sources:
    - "`/tmp/gbatest/savetest2.py` -- plants zeros, loads `saveprobe.gba`, `gbasave`, compares"
    - "Dump `/tmp/gbatest/probe_dump_take2.sav`: 32768 bytes, all `00`, differing from canonical in 32640 positions"
    - "Canonical pattern `(i*31+7) & 0xFF`, identical to `tools/save/sav_pattern.py make --size 32k`"
    - "Board console: `gbaload: restoring 32768 bytes of SRAM`, `gbaload: save restored, 32768 bytes of SRAM`"
    - "Records BRD-101 for the probe result this explains, BRD-092 for the transport proof, and BRD-103 for the gate this rules out"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15. The run is only valid because the planted contents differ from the probe's pattern, which is what the earlier confounded attempt lacked; the invalidation and its cause are recorded above rather than discarded."
- record_id: BRD-103
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "`isreadonly()` is not the cause of the dropped SRAM write: with the gate forced open the store is still lost"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "BRD-101 named `gba_memory.sv`'s `isreadonly()` as the leading hypothesis for the dropped store, on the grounds that for regions `0x0E` and `0x0F` it forces `sdram_wr = 0, sdram_rd = 1` unless `config_backup_type` is flash or SRAM, which would turn every CPU write into a read returning the old contents. The new `TINYGBA_SRAM_WR_DIAG` define makes that function return 0 for those two regions and changes nothing else. On the diagnostic image, with the canonical pattern planted so that square 0 measures the read path, square 0 is GREEN `(18,255,0)` and square 1 is RED `(226,0,0)`; square 0 green proves the CPU reads the host-restored window correctly on that image, so the probe executed and a red square 1 is a failed write rather than a dead machine. A host dump taken twenty seconds after load is still all zeros. With the write gate held open and reads demonstrably working, the store is still lost."
  consequence: "The hypothesis BRD-101 recorded as leading is refuted, and the fault is below `isreadonly()` in the SDRAM controller's CPU write path. That is a narrower target than entry 92 had and a different one: `sdram_gba` decides a store with `hi = !cpu_be[1:0]`, issues `SDRAM_DQM <= ~ds_latch[p][3:2]` or `~ds_latch[p][1:0]` on that bit, and gates the CPU with `cpu_ready <= cpu_be[3:2] == 0 | cpu_be[1:0] == 0`. A separate real defect was found while reading that path and is recorded as a defect rather than as this cause: `gba_memory` forces a single byte lane for the entire `0x0E` window via `sdram_be = 4'b1 << ram_addr[1:0]` whenever `ram_addr[27:25] == 3'b111`, so a halfword or word store to SRAM writes one byte in two or four. It cannot explain this failure because the probe writes bytes, and for a byte the CPU's own `ram_be` is already that same one-hot value. What this record does not establish is whether the store is never issued or is issued and discarded below the gate; both are below the refuted hypothesis and the probe cannot yet tell them apart."
  sources:
    - "`fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv` -- `isreadonly()` under `ifdef TINYGBA_SRAM_WR_DIAG`"
    - "`build/sdiag3/tinygba.bin` sha256 `c80ccab6d5667667f9a1372007738396eb3a6cf57a5dd65aeea7234da558314f`, seed 3, 12477 flops against the default's 12479, block RAM 149"
    - "Capture `/tmp/gbatest/dgc_99.png`: square 0 `(18,255,0)`, square 1 `(226,0,0)`, background `(0,0,0)`"
    - "Control capture `/tmp/gbatest/ctl3_99.png` on production `tinygba_vr3.bin` with zeros planted: square 0 `(226,0,0)`, square 1 `(226,0,0)`"
    - "Dump `/tmp/gbatest/diag_dump.sav` from the gate-open image: 32768 bytes of `00`"
    - "Records BRD-101 for the hypothesis this refutes, BRD-102 for the host-read measurement it agrees with, and TCL-079 for the capture-card false reading"
  verification: "Measured on hardware, 2026-11-15, on one build seed, with the failing production image measured first on the same capture path so that a capture fault could not be mistaken for a build result. Not established: whether the store is issued and discarded, which the next probe extension is designed to separate."
- record_id: TCL-079
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "A dark capture is not a dark core: the capture card's own 'No Video' slate was read as an HDMI fault and mis-attributed to the clock-plane gate"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "During the `TINYGBA_SRAM_WR_DIAG` work the capture card stopped delivering the GBA field and instead delivered a 1920x1080 frame that is black except for white text reading `GUERMOOK` and `No Video` centred at x 878-1042, y 512-566. That text is drawn by the card, not by the core: it appears with no core running and its position and typeface are the card's own. It was read as an absence of HDMI signal from the core, and because the diagnostic image's clock-plane gate had reported `clk16` on `SPINE1/TOP3` and `clk50` on `SPINE18/TOP2` -- pips the gate marks `unmeasured[no record]` -- the dark screen was attributed to the clock planes and written into the commit message for 6c1f5d8 as a property of that build. The attribution was wrong. Two observations contradict it. No signal at all from the core was recorded earlier in the session on production image `tinygba_vr3.bin`, which had drawn the probe's squares correctly forty minutes before, so the fault followed the card rather than the build. And once the card recovered, the production image reproduced the failure exactly as before and the diagnostic image drew its squares normally, so the diagnostic image's HDMI was never faulty."
  consequence: "The clock-plane gate's warning was real but was not evidence about this build, and no clock-plane conclusion was warranted; the commit message that states otherwise is corrected by entry 93 rather than rewritten. The general lesson is worth more than the incident: an absence of picture has at least three distinguishable causes on this bench -- the core not driving HDMI, the card rejecting a valid signal, and the card not being ready -- and the card states its own verdict in white text when the third applies. Before any dark-screen reading is attributed to the RTL, the capture must be checked for that slate, and a known-good image must be re-measured on the same path first. That control is what produced the correct answer here: measuring the failing production image before trusting the diagnostic result is the reason the mis-attribution was caught in the same session rather than persisting."
  sources:
    - "Capture `/tmp/gbatest/ctl2_99.png` and `/tmp/gbatest/diag_99.png`: the card's slate, white text bbox x 878-1042, y 512-566, on a 1920x1080 frame"
    - "Capture `/tmp/gbatest/ctl3_99.png`: production `tinygba_vr3.bin` reproducing the probe failure once the card recovered -- square 0 `(226,0,0)`, square 1 `(226,0,0)`"
    - "Capture `/tmp/gbatest/dgc_99.png`: diagnostic image drawing normally on the same path -- square 0 `(18,255,0)`, square 1 `(226,0,0)`"
    - "`build/sdiag3.log` clock-plane gate: `clk16 SPINE1/TOP3=unmeasured[no record]`, `clk50 SPINE18/TOP2=partial[this project]`"
    - "Commit 6c1f5d8, whose message carries the incorrect clock-plane attribution; corrected by entry 93, not amended"
    - "Records BRD-103 for the result the mis-attribution had briefly obscured"
  verification: "Observed on hardware, 2026-11-15. The conclusion rests on the production image failing identically after the card recovered, which excludes the build as the cause; the card's slate was read directly from the captures rather than inferred from a pixel count."
- record_id: TCL-080
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "Probe ROMs had no build recipe, and `as` silently encodes instructions ARM7TDMI leaves UNPREDICTABLE"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Two defects in how this project builds its probe ROMs, both found while extending `saveprobe.s`. First, the probe binary this work depends on, `build/test/saveprobe.gba`, had no build recipe beside it, so reproducing it from `tools/roms/saveprobe.s` took three guesses -- the pad length, the header complement, and whether the assembler target mattered -- and the first two produced a file differing from it at byte 0xBD. A probe whose binary cannot be rebuilt from its source is not reproducible evidence, and the dependency was invisible because `build/` is gitignored from line 1 of `.gitignore`, so the binary that produced BRD-101 and BRD-103 is a scratch artifact rather than a repository file. (An earlier draft of this record said the binary was committed; that was wrong and is corrected here. No `.gba` is tracked anywhere in the tree.) `tools/make_probe_bin.py` now assembles a `.s` into a padded image, asserts the fixed 0x96 at 0xB2, and computes the header complement `-(sum of bytes 0xA0..0xBC) - 0x19`; run against `tools/roms/saveprobe.s` it reproduces that local 4096-byte `build/test/saveprobe.gba` byte for byte, which is the check that it is right. Second, and more dangerous, the GNU assembler accepts some instructions it only warns about and encodes them anyway: `mul r8, r9, r8` has Rd == Rm, which ARM7TDMI leaves UNPREDICTABLE, and it assembled with the message `Rd and Rm should be different in mul` and exit status 0. A probe built that way drew squares in plausible positions from garbage addresses and looked like a working instrument reporting a failing machine. `make_probe_bin.py` now assembles with `--fatal-warnings`, so that class of mistake stops the build instead of reaching the board."
  consequence: "The tool that builds probe ROMs is now part of the repository and is checked by reproducing an existing binary, where before it was a set of remembered commands and an unvalidated binary. The `--fatal-warnings` change matters more than the convenience: the failure mode it removes is a probe that assembles cleanly, runs, draws a picture, and lies, which is worse than a probe that fails to build because the picture invites a conclusion. The general lesson is the same one TCL-041 recorded about probing instruments -- an instrument has to be checked against something known before its readings are used -- with the addition that a successful build is not one of those checks. One consequence is a limit on this record: `tools/roms/savewprobe.s`, the nine-case width and lane probe written during this work, assembles cleanly under the new rule and is still WRONG -- it reports red for stores to EWRAM that a one-case ROM in `saveprobe.s`'s idiom reports green for -- so it is committed marked NOT VALIDATED and no result has been taken from it. What this record does not establish is why that probe fails; the addressing, the instructions, the VRAM clear and the `mul` fault were each ruled out before stopping."
  sources:
    - "`tools/make_probe_bin.py` -- the builder, with the `--fatal-warnings` rationale in a comment"
    - "`/tmp/asm/repro.gba` and `/tmp/asm/repro2.gba`, both byte-identical to the local `build/test/saveprobe.gba` (4096 bytes, complement 0x1A); `build/` is gitignored, so that file is a scratch artifact and no probe binary is tracked"
    - "Assembler message verbatim: `mul.s:52: Rd and Rm should be different in mul`, exit status 0, image still produced"
    - "`tools/roms/savewprobe.s` header: the NOT VALIDATED banner and the list of what was ruled out"
    - "Records TCL-041 for the emulator-before-board rule this probe has not yet satisfied, and BRD-103 for the last board result that is trustworthy"
  verification: "The builder is verified by reproducing an existing 4096-byte probe image exactly -- the one that produced the BRD-101 and BRD-103 board results -- which is a stronger check than a successful run would be. That image is not tracked because `build/` is ignored, and this record states that rather than implying repository provenance it does not have. The `--fatal-warnings` behaviour was verified by watching the old command succeed on the offending source and the new one fail on it. Not verified: that `savewprobe.s` is correct, which is why it is marked as it is."
- record_id: TCL-081
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The simulation harness ran the free BIOS while every board result used the retail one, and its window let any keystroke stop a measurement"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "Two faults in `tools/sim/build_gba_sim.sh`, the second pointed out by the operator. First, the script never defined `TINYGBA_BIOS_HEX`, so `gba_memory.sv` fell back to `gba_bios_cultofgba.hex` -- the vendored free image -- while every board result in this repository was built by `tools/build_tinygba.sh` with `TINYGBA_BIOS_HEX` pointing at `build/test/official_bios.hex`. Every simulation run in this session was therefore a different machine from the one on the bench, and none of its output was comparable with BRD-101, BRD-102 or BRD-103. Confirmed by inspecting the run directory, which contained only `gba_bios_cultofgba.hex`. Second, the harness builds an SDL window on the operator's desktop, and SPACE pauses the simulation while ESC quits it, so a stray keystroke silently ends or freezes a measurement; one controller trace returned every stage at zero because the run had been stopped by a SPACE at `sim_time=864300`, which in the log reads exactly like 'the CPU never issued the store'. The script now resolves the retail BIOS to an absolute path and refuses to build without it, with `TINYGBA_SIM_BIOS=cultofgba` as an announced opt-out, and `-headless` / `TINYGBA_SIM_HEADLESS` builds no window at all. The source list had also rotted -- it named `mem_eeprom.v`, deleted in the wrapper conversion, and omitted `rv_sdram_adapter.v` -- so the harness could not elaborate the current top."
  consequence: "Any simulation result this project produces is only comparable with a board result if the same BIOS is inside the model, and that is now enforced rather than assumed: the script fails with an explanation instead of quietly building the free one. This also retires the assumption that `TINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` was in routine use -- the harness could not elaborate the current tree at all, so the real-controller configuration had not been exercised since the memory wrappers were replaced, and it now builds and runs. The headless mode matters for a reason beyond convenience: a measurement that can be paused or ended from outside the model cannot be cited as evidence, and the failure is invisible in the log. The retail BIOS is markedly slower to boot in simulation than the free one -- 59 frames before the probe runs, against 12 -- so a measurement costs several minutes of wall clock, which is a fact the next cycle should budget for rather than discover. Two limits: the 2024-era note in the script's own comments about which files it omitted was itself stale and is corrected, and no board result is affected, because this record concerns only the simulation path."
  sources:
    - "`tools/sim/build_gba_sim.sh` -- the BIOS resolution block and the `-headless` documentation"
    - "`tools/sim/sim_main.cpp` -- `headless` flag, the `!headless` guards around SDL, and the event-poll guard"
    - "`build/sim_sdrtl/obj_dir/gba_bios_cultofgba.hex` present as the only BIOS before the fix; `=== BIOS: retail (/run/media/vash/GIT/TinyGBA/build/test/official_bios.hex) ===` after it"
    - "`build/run_t7/tr.log`: `Simulation stopped: sim_time=864300`, from a SPACE keypress, with every trace counter at zero"
    - "`build/run_h1/h.log` and later: retail BIOS reaching 59 frames before the probe's stores, against 12 on the free BIOS"
    - "Records TCL-080 for the probe-builder work in the same cycle, and BRD-101 through BRD-103 for the board results this makes comparable"
  verification: "The BIOS fault was verified by listing the run directory's BIOS file before and after, and by the script's own printed banner after the change. The keyboard fault was verified by reading `Simulation stopped` with its sim_time out of the run log and matching it to the SDL keydown handler. The source-list fault was verified by the harness failing to elaborate with `Cannot find file containing module: 'rv_sdram_adapter'`, then succeeding. Not verified: that the retail BIOS in the model reproduces the board's behaviour, since the run that would have shown it was stopped with the cycle."
- record_id: BRD-104
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The store reaches gba_memory and becomes a correct SDRAM write; whether sdram_gba consumes it is unresolved, and the traces that were meant to say are withdrawn"
  status: MEASURED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "One step of the dropped-store question is now measured above the SDRAM controller, and the step below it is openly unresolved after a day of instrumentation that kept being wrong. What is established, from two independent `VERILATOR`-guarded traces that agreed with each other and were never contradicted: the CPU's store to `0x0E000000` arrives at `gba_memory` with the probe's own data (`wdata=08080808` for the first byte, `27272727` for the second) and `be=0001`, the `isreadonly()` term evaluates false for region `0xE`, and `gba_memory` then forms a correct SDRAM write for it -- `wr=1, rd=0, addr=810000`, byte enable equal to the single lane, port 2. In the same runs the FSM is seen entering request 2 (`double_req` set with both `rom_en` and `ram_cen` high), which is the path a CPU that fetches from the cartridge while it stores must take. What is NOT established: whether `sdram_gba` accepts that request, and whether a Write command reaches the pins. The staged traces meant to answer both were withdrawn rather than reported, because three separate faults were found in them and each was found only after the previous reading had been believed."
  consequence: "The controller is now the only remaining suspect for the dropped store, and it is the suspect the evidence points at rather than one reached by elimination: the request is correct on the way in, BRD-102 shows nothing lands in SDRAM, and BRD-103 shows the write gate is not responsible. A real defect was also confirmed on this path and should be fixed on its own merits: `gba_memory` forces a single byte lane across the whole `0x0E` window with `sdram_be = 4'b1 << ram_addr[1:0]` whenever `ram_addr[27:25] == 3'b111`, so a halfword or word store to SRAM writes one byte in two or four. It cannot explain the failure being chased, because the probe writes bytes and a byte's `be` is already that one-hot value. The duration of this hunt is itself the finding worth carrying: six instrumentation attempts produced three false readings -- Verilog precedence (`a == b & c < d` parses as `a == (b & (c < d))`), an SDRAM address slice taken from the wrong bit field, and print budgets consumed by the loader's 128 KB fill before the CPU ever ran -- and the honest position is that the counters were treated as measurements before the instrument had ever been shown capable of reporting a non-zero. The next cycle should not add a seventh trace without first proving that trace fires on an EWRAM store, which is known to work."
  sources:
    - "`build/run_hc/h.log` and `build/run_h5/h.log`: `MAIN store 0e000000 be=0001 wd=08080808 -> wr=0 rd=1 addr=00008c ... ro=0` followed by `req2 formed for 0e000000: wr=1 addr=810000 be=0001 ro=0`"
    - "`build/run_h8/h.log`: `drives wr=1 rd=0 addr=810000 be=0001 port=2`, the request as formed for the controller"
    - "`build/run_hb/h.log`, `build/run_hc/h.log`: an independent trace showing `cpu_wr` arriving in `sdram_gba` with `addr=810000`"
    - "`build/sim_sdrtl` built with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` against the retail BIOS, which is what makes the model comparable with BRD-101 to BRD-103 (TCL-081)"
    - "Records BRD-102 for the host-side measurement it agrees with, BRD-103 for the gate it rules out, and TCL-080 and TCL-081 for the instrument faults found in the same cycle"
  verification: "Observed in simulation with the retail BIOS and the real `sdram_gba` controller, 2026-11-15. Only the claims confirmed by two agreeing runs are stated; the controller-side stages are recorded as open. The runs were headless, so nothing outside the model could alter them. Not verified: whether the request is consumed below `gba_memory`, and whether the model reproduces the board's red square at all -- the frame dump that would show it was launched and then stopped with the cycle."
- record_id: BRD-105
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The cartridge SRAM store was being swallowed by the flash command path, because `config_backup_type` was an implicit one-bit net"
  status: MEASURED
  verified_date: 2026-11-15
  statement: "`gba_memory` drives `config_backup_type` as a 3-bit code (0 none, 1 flash512K, 2 flash1M, 3 SRAM, 4 EEPROM) and `sdram_gba` reads it to decide whether the cartridge window speaks the flash protocol. The signal was never declared in `gbatang_top.sv`, so Verilog gave it an implicit ONE-BIT net and every consumer saw only bit 0. A cartridge whose type is SRAM (`3'b011`) therefore delivered `3'b001` -- FLASH512K -- to the controller. `is_flash` became true, and because `tosdram` maps the whole cartridge-RAM window onto `10'h204`, `cpu_addr[25:16] == 10'h204` is true for every access there, so `flash_cmd_en` opened on every CPU store. The flash interpreter read `f_addr = {cpu_addr[15:2], 2'b0}` = 0, matched no opcode, discarded the byte, and raised `cpu_ready` anyway, so the CPU believed the write had succeeded. Measured in the Verilator model with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL` and the retail BIOS: `saveprobe` issues 155,443,407 cartridge fetch cycles with 25.3% halted and renders both squares red `(252,0,0)`; at the pins only 4,096 WR commands appear, all from the loader's fill, while the controller's flash path consumes the probe's own alternative pattern `(i*31+8) & 0xFF` 65,480 times. The width is visible in the generated model as `bufp->chgBit(...)` for the net against `chgCData(...,3)` for the port, and was confirmed at runtime by a temporary print reporting `cfg=1 is_flash=1` beside the loader's own `backup type=  3`. Declaring `wire [2:0] config_backup_type;` removes the truncation and reduces the flash path to zero events in the same run."
  consequence: "This is the mechanism BRD-101, BRD-102 and BRD-103 were chasing, and it is not in `isreadonly()` and not in the SDRAM write sequencer: the store was diverted above both. It also explains why the fault was invisible for so long -- the diversion raises `cpu_ready`, so nothing stalls and nothing reports an error, and the host restore path (BRD-092) never consults `sdram_gba` at all. Two limits must be stated with it. First, the fix is necessary but NOT sufficient: with the flash path silent the probe's square 1 is still red `(252,0,0)`, so at least one further cause remains below this one and the store still does not persist. Second, nothing here is confirmed on hardware; the correction changes what every backup type delivers to the controller, so a board image needs an explicit check that type 3 arrives as 3, with a flash-type cartridge the case most likely to move. The class of defect is worth recording separately: an implicit net is silent in synthesis, silent in simulation unless lint runs with width checking, and the project's own build passes only `-Wno-*` flags to Verilator, so nothing in the flow could have reported it."
  sources:
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/gbatang_top.sv`: the added `wire [2:0] config_backup_type;` and the absence of any prior declaration across the file's six references"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/gba_memory.sv`: `output reg [2:0] config_backup_type`, `tosdram`, and the `localparam BACKUP_*` codes"
    - "This repository, `fpga/gba/src/memory/sdram_gba.v`: `is_flash = config_backup_type == 3'd1 | config_backup_type == 3'd2` and the `cycle[3] & flash_cmd_en` RAS block"
    - "`build/sim_gate/obj_dir/Vgbatang_top__Trace__0.cpp`: `chgBit(oldp+3134,((1U & (IData)(...__config_backup_type)))` beside `chgCData(oldp+3135,...,3)`"
    - "`/tmp/gate2.log`: `FLASHGATE t=0 cfg=1 is_flash=1 flash=0 f_mode=0 f_addr=0000`, against the same run's `backup type=  3`"
    - "`/tmp/fix1_run.log`: the probe run after the declaration, `FINALFRAME: nonzero=512`, zero `flash write:` lines, square 1 still `(252,0,0)`"
    - "Records BRD-101, BRD-102 and BRD-103 for the board measurements this explains, and BRD-104 for the request-as-formed result it is consistent with"
  verification: "Measured in simulation 2026-11-15 with the retail BIOS and the real `sdram_gba` against the pin-level `sdram_model`, run headless. The truncation is shown two independent ways -- the generated C++ net width, and a runtime print of `config_backup_type` inside the controller beside the loader's own report -- and the fix is shown by the flash path falling to zero events. Not verified: that the store now persists, because the probe's square 1 remains red, and nothing here has been confirmed on hardware."

- record_id: BRD-106
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "The SDRAM pin model let a deselected die drive the shared DQ bus, so every cart-RAM store was recorded with corrupted data"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-16
  statement: "`fpga/gba/diag/sdram_model.v` decides whether a die drives the shared 16-bit `SDRAM_DQ` bus with a priority list that tests a pending read before the write branch: `if (command == CMD_RD) dq_oen <= 1; else if (rd_valid_pipe[0]) dq_oen <= 0; else if (cs && command == CMD_WR) dq_oen <= 1;`. The wrapper wires die0 to `SDRAM_nCS` and die1 to `~SDRAM_nCS` on that one bus, and cart RAM (word address `0x40000`, chip select `cpu_addr[25]` = 1) lives on die1. A die that still holds read data therefore keeps driving while the other die is written, and the two drivers contend. Measured in one headless run with a temporary `BUSOBS` print that reported the pin, the controller's own `dq_out` register, and both dies' `dq_oen` at the same instant: for the probe's first store the controller drove `0808` correctly, die0 was simultaneously driving stale read data `e156`, and the pin resolved to `e95e`, which is `0x0808 | 0xe156` exactly. Across the whole run all 1024 corrupted writes were explained by that wired-OR and none required another cause. With a latched chip-select gate on the model's drive (`sel_at_cmd` captured when `command != CMD_NOP`, then `assign SDRAM_DQ = (dq_oen | ~sel_at_cmd) ? 16'hzzzz : dq_out_w`), the probe's write lands and its verification square turns green `(0,252,0)`."
  consequence: "The pin model, not the RTL, was the reason a CPU byte store to `0x0E000000` looked absent at the pins, and it corrupts the recorded data of every cartridge-RAM write on the bus whenever the other die has been read recently. Two earlier readings are affected and are named rather than rewritten: BRD-104's statement that whether a Write command reaches the pins was unresolved, and the reasoning in BRD-105 that treated the pin trace as a faithful record. The controller itself is exonerated for the write path -- request formed as `wr=1, addr=810000, be=0001`, `din_latch[0]` and `dq_out` both equal to the CPU's own byte, `dq_oen` driving -- so the dropped store is above or below that path, not in it, and the probe's remaining hardware failure has a different cause. A plain combinational gate on `cs` is NOT the fix: it suppresses reads, because the controller drops `nCS` before the read data appears, which took a wasted build to establish. The gate must latch the selection at command time."
  sources:
    - "`fpga/gba/diag/sdram_model.v`: the `dq_oen` priority list, and `wire cs = (SDRAM_nCS === 1'b0)` which makes the model's `cs` active-low"
    - "`fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v`: `wire cs_die0 = SDRAM_nCS; wire cs_die1 = ~SDRAM_nCS;` with the comment that die1 carries EWRAM and cart RAM"
    - "`build/sim_busobs` run log: `BUSOBS pin=e95e ctl_dq_out=0808 dqm=10 ctl_oen=0 d0_oen=0 d1_oen=1 d0_out=e156 d1_out=ffff`"
    - "`build/sim_lsel` run log: `FINALFRAME: nonzero=512 white=0 green=256`, squares `sq0 (252,0,0)` and `sq1 (0,252,0)`"
    - "`build/sim_dump1`, `build/sim_wrfix`, `build/sim_csgate` runs, each with a late die1 dump at `n_cycles == 370000000` reading `die1.mem[26'h40000 + i]`"
    - "Records BRD-104 and BRD-105 for the readings this supersedes, and TCL-082 for the die coverage fault found in the same harness"
  verification: "Measured in simulation 2026-11-16, headless, retail BIOS, with `-DTINYGBA_SDRAM_RTL`. The contention claim is arithmetic rather than inferred: `0x0808 | 0xe156` equals the recorded pin value, and the same identity holds for 1024 of 1024 mismatching writes. The corrected model is verified by the probe's own criterion, a green verification square, in a run whose dump shows die1 holding the expected pattern. Every diagnostic used a restore trap and the tree was confirmed byte-identical to `HEAD` afterwards. Not verified: that the same contamination explains any board-side result, since the board has no such model in the path."

- record_id: TCL-082
  kind: TOOLCHAIN
  topic_id: TCL
  title: "The SDRAM simulation wrapper traced and dumped die0 only, while cart RAM lives on die1"
  status: VERIFIED
  verified_date: 2026-11-16
  statement: "`fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v` instantiates two pin models for the board's two dice and gives a `TRACE` parameter to die0 alone: `snes_sdram_model #(.SIZE_WORDS(16*1024*1024), .TRACE(1)) die0` under `TINYGBA_SDRAM_PINTRACE`, against `snes_sdram_model #(.SIZE_WORDS(4*1024*1024)) die1` with no `TRACE` at all. Its one-shot memory dump is `die0.mem[i]` only, and fires at `n_cycles == 3000000`. The file's own comment states that die1 carries EWRAM and cart RAM, and `tosdram()` maps the cartridge window `0x0E000000` to `0x810000`, whose chip-select bit is 1, which is die1. So the write-only pin trace and the memory dump both watched the die the cartridge save window never touches. Measured: with the trace confined to die0 a whole probe run printed 4096 `sdram: WR` lines, all from the loader's fill of the cartridge ROM; with the same code given a `TRACE` parameter on die1 the same run printed 266240, and a late die1 dump read the cart-RAM row the trace had never covered."
  consequence: "Any conclusion drawn from `TINYGBA_SDRAM_PINTRACE` about a cartridge-RAM or EWRAM access is unfounded, including the reading that a CPU store never arrived as an SDRAM write at all. The dump had a second fault of the same shape: at `n_cycles == 3000000` it samples the loader's fill of the cartridge ROM, which is die0's business, and it cannot see anything the CPU does later. Both need fixing before the harness is used again for save work: give die1 the same `TRACE` parameter, and make the dump cover both dice at a time chosen relative to the event being measured rather than a fixed early cycle."
  sources:
    - "`fpga/gba/diag/gba_sdram_rtl.v`: the die0 and die1 instantiations, and the `for (i = 0; i < 16; i = i + 1) $display(\"cart[%0d]=%h\", i, die0.mem[i]);` dump"
    - "`build/sim_fix_pt` run log: 4096 `sdram: WR` lines with the trace on die0"
    - "`build/sim_die1` run log: 266240 `sdram: WR` lines with the same trace on die1"
    - "`build/sim_dump1` run log: `die1_cart[0]=e757 ...` before the model fix, and the `lsel` run's `die1_cart[0]=2607` after it"
    - "Records BRD-106 for the contention fault found in the same harness, and BRD-104 for the conclusion this makes unfounded"
  verification: "Measured 2026-11-16 by building the same source with and without a `TRACE` parameter on die1 and comparing the pin-line counts, then by adding a late die1 dump and reading the cartridge-RAM row out of it. Both builds used restore traps and left the tree byte-identical to `HEAD`. Not verified: whether any older simulation in this project relied on the die0-only trace for a die1 access, which would need each cited log re-read."

- record_id: BRD-107
  kind: BOARD
  topic_id: BRD
  title: "Two images placed at seed 1 render black on the Tang while seed-3 images render, and seed 1 is the build script's default"
  status: MEASURED
  verified_date: 2026-11-16
  statement: "On 2026-11-16 four bitstreams were loaded onto the board in one session with the same commands and the same probe cartridge: `build/prod3` and `build/sdiag3`, which entries 85 and 93 record as placed at seed 3, both render the probe's two squares -- green at the left square and red at the right, matching BRD-103's diagnostic result -- while `build/wrfix_board` and `build/nowfix`, both built at the default seed 1 from the current tree, render a uniform black field inside the capture's `(43,43,43)` pillarbox and never boot the BIOS. `build/nowfix` is `HEAD` with only the `wire [2:0] config_backup_type;` declaration removed, and it is black too, so the width fix is not the cause. The board was re-verified healthy by reloading `build/sdiag3` and re-capturing after both failures. Every image passed all nine build gates, and both seed-1 images report `hold 0 violation(s)` with no relaxation. The build script's own help text says of `TINYGBA_SEED`: `placement seed (default 1; not proven on hardware)`."
  consequence: "The obvious reading is that seed 1 does not place this design correctly on hardware, which would make the build script's default unsafe and would explain a black board with a passing gate report. It is not established: two images at seed 1 being black does not prove the seed is the variable, because no seed-1 image built from the same commit as a working seed-3 image has yet been tested, and no seed-3 image of the current tree has been produced. What is established is narrower and still useful -- the black screen is not the `config_backup_type` width fix, since removing that declaration leaves the behaviour unchanged, so any bisect should look elsewhere. Until this is settled, quote the seed with every hardware result and do not deploy a default-seed image without a capture."
  sources:
    - "`build/wrfix_board/tinygba.bin` sha256 `5ce94e9c478cbea510e66ae34a0a253b79308768555a3be64e1a209c17ac39bd`, capture `/tmp/wrchk/cap3.png` uniform black"
    - "`build/nowfix/tinygba.bin` sha256 `79fb7ef51b82f915352cbc9a0d37956d0ccad3e6b98fc8d397073a3c7bc41862`, capture `/tmp/wrchk/cap_nowfix.png` uniform black"
    - "`build/prod3/tinygba.bin` capture `/tmp/wrchk/cap_prod3c.png`: green square bbox x 152-255 y 0-106, red square bbox x 368-471 y 0-107"
    - "`build/sdiag3/tinygba.bin` capture `/tmp/wrchk/cap_diag3.png` and the re-check capture `/tmp/wrchk/cap_recheck.png`: the same two squares"
    - "`tools/build_tinygba.sh` header: `TINYGBA_SEED placement seed (default 1; not proven on hardware)`"
    - "Records BRD-103 for the diagnostic square result reproduced here, and BRD-105 for the width fix this exonerates"
  verification: "Measured on hardware 2026-11-16, all four images loaded through `tangload` in two-wire mode with `--no-flash` captures tied to the bitstream's map id, and the known-good image re-tested after both failures so that a board or capture fault could not be mistaken for a build result. Not verified: that the seed is what separates them, which needs a seed-3 build of the current tree and a matched pair from one commit."
